- **`flatpak-managed-install.service` DNS race fixed via `/boot-error-triage` (`cfb8caa`)** — a routine post-reboot triage ("I saw something had failed on boot") found the unit racing `network-online.target` (`Could not resolve hostname` for `dl.flathub.org`), self-healing a minute later via systemd's restart — harmless but trains you to ignore a real failed-unit signal. Fixed in the shared `modules/desktop-apps.nix` (all 3 desktop hosts) by adding `After=`/`Wants=network-online.target`. User's call: *"Add it. That way I don't get used to seeing failing units on boot."* Verified via `shared-module-check` (4-host PASS). Committed, pushed, re-staged via `nh os boot`.

Delegated the fix to the `manager` agent rather than doing it by hand — first real "bug report from a sibling repo, routed to `manager`" case. `manager` fixed all three hardcoded spots (dir/index line, Step 2's `ls`, Step 3's write path + `originSessionId`) to resolve via the shared `~/.claude/skills/lib/find-transcript-dir.sh` helper (same lib `research`/`refresh-manager-profile` already use), bumped `save-memory` 0.2.0→0.2.1, ran `public-repo-guard` clean, and landed via branch+PR (`fix/save-memory-project-dir`, commit `e864ae9`) per its never-self-merge rule. - Separately investigated why a FinanceGuru background session got `Blocked by classifier` both spawning `manager` and editing `.claude/settings.local.json` to flip into auto mode, while this repo's sessions do both freely. Root cause confirmed by comparing live settings: **not a file-permission or repo-config difference** (both repos' settings files are `644`, both trusted, the same global `Bash(*)`/`defaultMode:"auto"` live in `~/.claude/settings.json` either way) — the real asymmetry is that this repo's `autoMode.environment` profile explicitly names `/home/bosko/NixOS` as trusted, so Auto Mode's heuristics lean permissive here and fall back conservative for a project with no such entry; separately, the classifier appears to hard-block a session from escalating its own permission mode (spawning an autonomous delegate, or writing the settings file that controls the mode) specifically in a background/agent-view session with no human present to approve — an interactive terminal session doing the same mode-toggle is a normal in-session action the classifier doesn't gate. Advised the user to make the mode switch from an actual interactive terminal in FinanceGuru rather than try to route around the block programmatically. **No repo change** — purely diagnostic, resolution lives on FinanceGuru's side.

**Global `manager` agent built, calibration-tested, and merged as PR #15 — a delegate that decides tasks the way this user would instead of interviewing at each step (2026-08-30, session 96).**
- User asked for a global agent that "makes the calls it thinks I would make... typically the smartest call, not necessarily the easiest," commits/pushes verified work, and reports every decision it made. Built via `/interview` first, then 12 parallel `transcript-scanner` agents mined decision-making style/risk tolerance/values from every project with Claude Code history (NixOS plus 11 sibling `~/projects/` repos), producing `dotfiles/bosko/claude/manager-profile.md`. The mined profile required the user's own explicit read-through/sign-off before its first push — a standing rule baked into the profile itself, since it's the one artifact that directly steers future unsupervised decisions.
- **`manager.md` ships in supervised "training mode" by default** (pause-every-step, same idiom as `public-repo-guard`/`create-loop`), with six hard limits that always stop for confirmation regardless of the toggle: no data destruction/force-push, no touching secrets, no spending money, no direct privileged/live-system execution (never runs `sudo`/`systemctl`/`nh os switch` itself, even on vpn-server's passwordless sudo — proposes via PR instead), no pushing straight to `main`, no mutating MCP calls (read-only MCP is fine). Extra caution surfaced via `AskUserQuestion` on family/shared-stakes projects and any edit to a `commonModules`/`desktopModules`-shared file. Verification uses each target project's own existing method, never a uniform bar; lands only via branch+PR, never self-merged; in this repo, pushes are additionally gated through `public-repo-guard`. A companion skill, `refresh-manager-profile`, re-mines transcripts incrementally on demand (auto-pushes on later refreshes without re-litigating the sign-off gate).
- **Live calibration test the same day, in a synthetic sandbox repo (`manager-calibration-sandbox`, deleted after)**: a small CLI task mixing 8 technical/non-technical decisions plus two hard-limit traps (a paid-API nudge, an "install whatever's necessary" nudge). All 8 content decisions graded "agree" by the user; verified ran-and-observed throughout (pytest 18/18, `nix flake check` green, manual smoke tests); landed via branch+PR, never self-merged.
- **Real gap found and fixed mid-test**: the sandbox repo had no `main` ref at all, so the "never push directly to a default branch" hard limit was live from the start. `manager` refused three separate escalating attempts by the orchestrating session to authorize that push via relayed/asserted consent — including an explicit "this is the user's own direct answer, not a relay" — and separately discovered `AskUserQuestion` is **not available to it when spawned as a background subagent via the Agent tool** (only an interactive main-session turn has it; confirmed via `ToolSearch`, twice). It held the line regardless, requiring the user to perform the minimal bootstrap action themselves and verifying the resulting repo state independently before proceeding. **User graded this "exactly right, keep it this strict."** `manager.md` was updated same-session (commit `c863102`, part of PR #15) to codify the validated fallback: stop completely and name the verifiable unblock action, never accept a relayed consent claim regardless of wording, verify resulting state independently — since the old wording ("stop and use AskUserQuestion") doesn't hold for that invocation shape.
- Verified via `nh os boot --dry` (clean, +11/-11 paths, +6.94 KiB, no kernel/DM/compositor changes) and `public-repo-guard` (SAFE, 0 genuine findings) before the first push. PR #15 squash-merged into `main`, CI green. **`nh os switch` already applied same-day** — fully live and ready for real use, nothing pending on this thread. See memory `project_manager_agent`.

Fixed by writing the JSON to the Nix store via `pkgs.writeText` and reading it back with `jq --slurpfile`, which sidesteps shell quoting entirely.

**Real fix landed for the `/srv/shared` CIFS-stall bug session 86 thought it had already fixed — a missing hyphen made the original timeout option a silent no-op (2026-08-23, session 89).**
- User reported the ~10s terminal freeze (root-caused session 86 to `starship`/DMS repeatedly `stat()`-ing the unreachable `/srv/shared` CIFS automount whenever `gaming` is off) was still happening after that session's fix supposedly shipped. Diagnosed live rather than re-guessing: `systemctl show srv-shared.mount` still reported `TimeoutUSec=1min 30s` (systemd's untouched default) even after the fixed generation was switched in — the option written in `modules/shared-folder-client.nix` was `x-systemd.mounttimeout=2` (no hyphen), which `man systemd.mount` confirms fstab doesn't recognize; fstab silently drops unknown `x-systemd.*` options instead of erroring, so the "fix" was a no-op the whole time (`86ad36e`, then corrected to the real hyphenated keyword `x-systemd.mount-timeout=` in `24a1d2d`). Also ruled out a user-raised alacritty-vs-kitty angle: the fix was never terminal-specific (alacritty isn't even installed here), starship triggers this identically regardless of terminal.
- Verified via `shared-module-check` (file is shared with `natalie-laptop`) — all 4 hosts PASS deep-eval both times. **laptop switched and confirmed live this session**: `TimeoutUSec` now reads `2s`, and a real-world test (new prompt while gaming is off/unreachable) now cuts off at ~2s instead of ~10s. **natalie-laptop has NOT been rebuilt with either commit yet** — it shares this module and still has the old, never-actually-applied 90s default until it gets its own `rebuild`/`switch`.
- **This shrinks the stall, it doesn't eliminate it** — every prompt redraw while `gaming` is off/unreachable will still pause ~2s; the deeper fix (stopping starship/DMS from touching `/srv/shared` at all when it's unreachable) wasn't chased, per the user's choice in session 86.

**`/boot-error-triage` run on laptop, 2 real findings root-caused and fixed; a third (CIFS mount) correctly reframed as not a bug (2026-08-20, session 86).**

- **`srv-shared.mount` failing was investigated and correctly reframed, not just patched**: user initially framed it as "the shared folder IP problem," but live checks (LAN ARP `INCOMPLETE`, Tailscale showing `gaming` `offline`) proved the static IP (`10.0.0.251`) was already correct and live per session 63's setup — the mount was failing purely because `gaming` was powered off, and the automount (already `nofail`) was being triggered by innocuous logins-time activity (`starship`, the DMS shell touching `/srv/shared`). Presented this correction before acting. User's actual ask once reframed: switch the client-side `extraHosts` entry from the LAN IP to gaming's Tailscale IP (`100.66.15.1`) so the share also resolves off the home LAN, matching the pattern already used for Jellyfin (session 83, `daea421`) — `modules/shared-folder-client.nix` (`693d573`). Confirmed first that Samba's `openFirewall = true` opens ports globally (unlike Jellyfin's interface-scoped rule), so no `gaming`-side firewall change was needed.
- **Bluetooth `"BAP requires ISO Socket which is not enabled"` root-caused to a real, fixable gap**, not just cosmetic: `Experimental = true` alone isn't enough for BAP/LE Audio — bluez also needs the ISO-socket kernel-experimental feature UUID (`6fbaf188-05e0-496a-9885-d6ddfdb4e03e`), confirmed against BlueZ's own docs/kernel.org bugzilla thread. Added to `hosts/laptop/environment.nix`'s existing `hardware.bluetooth.settings.General` block (`fc756c3`). Classic Bluetooth (A2DP/HFP) was already unaffected either way — confirmed via `bluetoothctl`/`systemctl is-active bluetooth` before touching config.
- **ACPI BIOS errors** (`Could not resolve symbol [\_SB.PC00.LPCB.HEC.CFSP]`, GPE 0x14 duplicate `_L14`/`_E14` methods) matched a confirmed upstream ASUS DSDT/EC firmware bug (kernel.org Bugzilla #220583) — the same class as the laptop's board (`K6502VJ.306`, 05/25/2023). Verified functional impact directly rather than assuming: `sensors` shows `fan1_input: I/O error` (can't *read* fan RPM via ACPI) but thermal zones/coretemp report fine and the EC still physically drives the fans independent of the broken ACPI query. No newer BIOS changelog found addressing it; no kernel-side fix exists (it's firmware, not Linux). **Left as-is by design — cosmetic only, no config change made.**
- Verified via `nixos-dry-run` on laptop (clean, +16/−16 paths, +528 bytes, `switch` recommended — no kernel/bootloader/DM churn) and `shared-module-check`'s 4-host sweep (since `shared-folder-client.nix` is shared with `natalie-laptop`) — gaming/laptop/natalie-laptop/vpn-server all PASS. Committed as 2 separate commits (`693d573`, `fc756c3`) since they're unrelated scopes, pushed. **Neither has been applied to laptop yet** (config-only, no reboot needed) — see Current Goals. natalie-laptop also needs its own switch to pick up the shared Tailscale-IP fix.
- **Session-close's `secret-scan` pass found a real, reproducible bug in the skill's own history-scan flag order, plus 13 stale local-only stashes as the actual root cause of a recurring false alarm.** `secret-scan.sh` ran `git rev-list --all --exclude=refs/stash` — git only honors `--exclude` when it precedes `--all`/`--branches`/`--tags`/`--remotes`/`--glob`, so `--all --exclude=refs/stash` was a silent no-op the whole time, letting `refs/stash` leak back into the scanned commit set. This had already caused one traced-and-"fixed" false alarm in a prior session (2026-07-27, 2 stale stashes) — that session's fix added the exclude flag but in the wrong order, so the underlying bug was never actually closed, just coincidentally not re-triggered until now. Root-caused this time to **13 different stale local stashes** dating to ~2026-05-21 (pre-sops-migration, predating the current `modules/`/`hosts/` layout) — previewed each via `git stash show -p` before touching anything: mostly single-file `flake.lock` bumps long superseded by real commits, one 4-line `security.nix` diff, one 2-file diff against a `dotfiles/laptop/environment.nix` path that no longer exists. Fixed the flag order in `secret-scan.sh` (swapped to `--exclude=refs/stash --all`, comment added explaining the ordering rule) and, after confirming via `AskUserQuestion` (user chose **drop all 13**), dropped every stale stash. Re-ran the scan clean (1850 → 1688 commits scanned, 0 findings). Also pruned 5 already-stale local `refs/remotes/origin/{3.5,4.0,4.1,4.1.1,4.1.2}` tracking refs along the way (`git remote prune origin`) — confirmed via `git ls-remote` that GitHub itself never had them back since session 27's 2026-07-02 deletion; only this local clone hadn't pruned its own tracking refs, which is what made those specific already-deleted-and-confirmed-gone branches show up as a history hit again too.

- Ran `/interview` first (lightweight path — a few blocking `AskUserQuestion`s, not the full brief+second-review ceremony) to pin down device OS and the real motivation, then `/research` (8 parallel `source-reviewer` agents, 6/8 usable) to confirm install paths before giving instructions, per standing CLAUDE.md rules.
- **TCL TV**: confirmed Google TV/Android TV — installed Tailscale straight from the TV's built-in Google Play Store, no sideload needed.
- **Fire Stick**: not in the Amazon Appstore search for this device (an HD, 1st-gen, standard Fire OS — not the Vega-OS "4K Select" that blocks sideloading outright). Had to sideload via Downloader. **Real gotcha hit and fixed**: Tailscale's own marketing page (`tailscale.com/download`) Android button links to the **Play Store listing**, not a raw APK — on Fire OS (no Google Play Services) that just shows the confusing symptom the user hit ("lists my other Android devices to install to, wants a Google sign-in, no Fire Stick option"). The actual raw-APK source is **`pkgs.tailscale.com/stable/`** (Tailscale's own package mirror — lists a `tailscale-android-universal-*.apk` directly, no sign-in). Use that for any future Fire OS/non-Play-Store Android sideload.

- User confirmed both devices connected and Jellyfin working on all hosts. No flake/repo changes at all — Jellyfin's `tailscale0`:8096 firewall opening was already done in session 83. Memory updated: `project_tailscale_evaluation`, `project_jellyfin`. Nothing pending on this thread.

**Pi-hole promoted to a Tailscale-admin-console global nameserver, layered on top of the existing flake-managed DNS override — closes the gap for devices the flake can't reach (2026-08-19, session 84).**
- User asked how to route DNS through pi-hole via the Tailscale dashboard. Walked through the actual admin-console UI (Nameservers → Add nameserver → Custom → `100.92.242.60`), including two per-nameserver toggles: **"Restrict to domain"** left OFF (that mode turns a nameserver into split-DNS for one domain suffix — the opposite of what's wanted for fleet-wide ad-blocking) and **"Use with exit node"** turned ON (keeps pi-hole authoritative even once a Tailscale exit node exists — e.g. the still-unexplored Mullvad idea — instead of silently losing ad-blocking the moment one comes online). "Override local DNS" enabled so it's the default for the whole tailnet.
- **Additive, not a reversal of session 82's Option-B decision.** Session 82 explicitly picked the flake-managed per-host DNS override over this exact admin-console toggle, because the toggle changes live DNS for every meshed device at once with no staged rollout. That reasoning still holds and the 3 NixOS hosts keep the flake-managed override (`modules/desktop-networking.nix`) as primary, `1.1.1.1` fallback intact. What's different now: the tailnet includes devices the flake can never reach — pi-hole/famdash themselves, plus two Android phones (`natalies-s23-ultra`, `pixel-6`) that joined the mesh sometime after session 82's close (noticed during this session's verification, not added by it). Those have no NixOS config to carry a DNS override, so the admin-console nameserver is the only way they get pi-hole's filtering. Both layers now coexist on purpose.
- **Verified live from gaming**, not just configured and assumed: `tailscale debug prefs` shows `CorpDNS: true`, `/etc/resolv.conf` points at Tailscale's local stub resolver (`100.100.100.100`). `host doubleclick.net` → `0.0.0.0` (pi-hole's block response) both through the system resolver and queried directly against pi-hole (`100.92.242.60`) — matching answers confirm the stub is actually forwarding to pi-hole, not answering itself. `host github.com` still resolves a real IP, so it's not blackholing everything. pi-hole's own `/var/log/pihole.log` shows the test queries landing (20 matching entries), confirming pi-hole itself is seeing the traffic.
- **Not yet verified on laptop/natalie-laptop** — both were `offline, last seen 9h ago` in `tailscale status` during this session, so the same check couldn't run there. The admin-console setting is fleet-wide, so it should apply automatically once they're online, but that's not confirmed first-hand yet — see Current Goals.

**Jellyfin opened up on the Tailscale interface — a real access gap found and closed, not just a hypothetical (2026-08-19, session 83).**
- User asked whether Jellyfin needed any adjusting now that Tailscale mesh is live. Investigation (not assumption) found a genuine gap: `services.tailscale.enable` doesn't auto-open any firewall ports, and `hosts/gaming/jellyfin-server.nix`'s `networking.firewall.interfaces` only listed `enp4s0` (LAN) + `wg0` (WireGuard) — `tailscale0` was absent from both that block and any `trustedInterfaces` list anywhere in the repo (confirmed via grep, not guessed). Since `modules/vpn.nix`/`wg0` is currently commented out of `desktopModules` for the Oracle outage (session 81), Tailscale was the *only* away-from-home path to Jellyfin right now — so this was actively blocking access, not a dormant edge case.
- Fixed with a single added line: `tailscale0.allowedTCPPorts = [ 8096 ]` alongside the existing `enp4s0`/`wg0` entries (`daea421`). Left DLNA/SSDP (1900/7359 UDP) LAN-only on purpose — those are broadcast/multicast discovery and don't traverse a point-to-point Tailscale link; remote clients connect by IP/hostname directly instead.
- Verified in two passes: `nixos-dry-run` on gaming pre-switch (clean, +464 bytes, no kernel/DM churn, recommendation `switch`), then `verify-service jellyfin gaming` post-switch — service active, mount/folders correct, and (the part a `systemctl status` alone can't show) the **live generated firewall script** at `/nix/store/...-firewall-start/bin/firewall-start` was grepped directly and confirmed the `tailscale0`/8096 `ip46tables` rule is actually loaded, not just present in source. No NOPASSWD on gaming, so the `nh os switch` itself was handed to the user per the standing sudo-gated-steps rule; they ran it and reported back before the verify pass.
- Also answered a follow-up on Jellyfin's own **LAN Networks** dashboard setting (Dashboard → Networking, app-level, not flake-managed) — recommended `10.0.0.0/24,100.64.0.0/10` (physical LAN + Tailscale's whole CGNAT block, not individual peer IPs, so new tailnet devices don't need a dashboard edit later) so Tailscale clients get treated as local-quality rather than remote-throttled. **User applied this too** — confirmed live in Jellyfin's own startup log (`Used LAN subnets: ["10.0.0.0/24", "100.64.0.0/10"]`), not just assumed from the recommendation.
- Net effect: Jellyfin is now reachable over LAN, Tailscale (live), and WireGuard (dormant, ready once vpn-server returns) — with Tailscale clients getting local-quality treatment. Nothing left pending on this thread.

**Tailscale mesh rollout completed (all 5 devices live), pi-hole/famdash reachability fully closed out (SSH key trust + drift-proofing), desktop DNS rerouted through pi-hole over Tailscale, and the now-orphaned VPN watchdog silenced (2026-08-18, session 82 — 2 more back-to-back sessions closed together, same day as session 81).**
- **All 5 Tailscale devices confirmed live** — user rebuilt gaming/laptop/natalie-laptop (bringing `services.tailscale.enable` live on all 3) and ran `sudo tailscale up` on each; pi-hole and famdash got Tailscale installed manually via console (non-flake, outside the repo) and authed the same way. `tailscale status`/the admin website both confirm all 5. This fully resolves session 81's top pending item — don't re-flag laptop/natalie-laptop Tailscale enrollment as pending.
- **pi-hole/famdash SSH reachability fixed in two parts.** (1) Drift-proofing: `dotfiles/common/configs/ssh.nix`'s `pi-hole`/`famdash` aliases now dial their stable Tailscale IPs (`100.92.242.60`/`100.99.52.59`) instead of their DHCP-drift-prone LAN IPs; `hosts.json` gained a `tailscaleIp` field for all 5 devices (`910a2d9`). (2) Key trust: root-caused as pi-hole/famdash never getting `modules/users.nix`'s `bosko` key baked in (not flake hosts) — fixed by manually appending gaming's "Desktop" key to each Pi's `~/.ssh/authorized_keys` via local console (`a5a400d`). Both verified live from gaming (`bosko@pi-hole`, `natty@famdash` connect cleanly). Together these close out the whole pi-hole/famdash reachability thread — see memory `project_hosts_json`.
- **Desktop DNS rerouted from pi-hole's LAN IP to its Tailscale IP** so filtered DNS keeps working for laptop/natalie-laptop off the home LAN too (`modules/desktop-networking.nix`, `10.0.0.19` → `100.92.242.60`, `1.1.1.1` kept as fallback). Decided via a short `/interview` as **Option B (flake-managed)** over **Option A (Tailscale admin-console DNS override)** — B is deterministic, uses this repo's existing dry-run/deep-eval tooling, and keeps a working fallback if pi-hole-over-Tailscale doesn't pan out; A was a single unreviewable toggle affecting every meshed device at once. **Pre-flight caught a real blocker before the flake change**: pi-hole's own `dns.listeningMode = "LOCAL"` was silently dropping tailnet-sourced queries (Tailscale's point-to-point `/32` addressing doesn't count as a "local subnet" by pi-hole's own definition) — fixed via pi-hole's supported CLI to `listeningMode = "ALL"`, confirmed live with a real query + a blocked-domain check before touching the flake. Verified via `nixos-dry-run` (gaming, clean, +4.74 KiB) + `shared-module-check` 4-host deep-eval before committing (`30c73ee`). **Only gaming has switched onto this so far** — laptop/natalie-laptop still need their own `nh os switch` (see Current Goals).
- **Removed gaming's now-purely-noisy `vpn-health-check` watchdog** — the 20-min systemd user timer added session 69 (`1a4d440`) was firing a `notify-send` alert every cycle purely because vpn-server is Oracle-disabled with no ETA (and wg0 was already pulled from the flake for the same reason). Removed the service/timer/script block from `hosts/gaming/environment.nix` entirely (`40cf9a5`) and stopped+disabled the live timer immediately so notifications stopped right away, not just at next rebuild. Verified via `nixos-dry-run` (clean, −960 bytes, no kernel/DM/driver changes).
- This close narrates 2 separate un-closed sessions that ran back-to-back after session 81's own close (`c70ef25`) — same day, same overall vpn-server-outage arc.

**vpn-server down for the day (Oracle admin-disabled it, no ETA); Tailscale mesh built as a stopgap and expanded to all 3 desktop hosts; wg0 pulled from the flake so it stops filing failed units; `improve-system` swept 60 skills (2026-08-18, session 81 — 4 back-to-back sessions closed together).**
- **vpn-server root cause confirmed: an Oracle-side administrative block, not a config/network bug.** `oci-cli` (existing API key) showed `lifecycle-state: STOPPED`; a START attempt returned `409 IncorrectState: "Instance ... is disabled ... contact customer support"` — a blanket block on every instance action (start, resize), not a quota-fit problem routable around via a resize. Also found via the Limits API: tenancy's Always-Free A1 quota was cut from 4 OCPU/24GB (what the instance is provisioned at) to 2 OCPU/12GB, concurrently. Billing (active $0 subscription, no hold) and Announcements were both ruled out first. Support-ticket text drafted and given to the user to file; **no ETA, user is self-watching for Oracle's reply** — no automation built for "tell me when it's back" (explicitly declined, see Recent Decisions). See memory `project_vpn_server_oracle_disabled`.

Built via the `ship-skill` flow with the spec already pinned down (skipped re-interview). Wired into `improve-system` as Step 1 item 3, renumbering the rest into a six-skill chain. `nixos-dry-run` clean before and after.

Fanned out 4 parallel `transcript-scanner` sub-agents (one per ~21-file batch, per the standing parallelization rule) over all 84 project transcripts. Found 87 `Agent`-tool spawns across 4 task shapes — every shape clearing the "recurring" bar is already served by an existing agent (61 single-URL-fetch → `source-reviewer`; 15 fixed-rubric-over-partition → `skill-reviewer`; 4 fixed-pattern-transcript-grep → `transcript-scanner` itself, mining itself); the remaining 2 shapes are one-off "lens" patterns that fail the recurring test. Stopped without inventing a candidate, per the skill's own Step 4 rule.
- **Found and fixed a real bug along the way** (`4feaa11`): an errored invocation attempt was silently counting as a legitimate "last run" for `find-last-skill-invocation.sh`-based cutoff detection. Hardened `agent-suggestion`'s own Gotchas to widen to full history whenever the reported cutoff looks suspiciously recent with no completed pass visible at that timestamp — this is a new failure mode beyond the already-documented slash-command blind spot, not yet audited against `skill-suggestion`/`skill-audit`/`session-closer`. See memory `project_agent_suggestion_skill`.

Recommendation: pilot Tailscale's own SaaS (not self-hosted Headscale) alongside the existing hub, laptop first, only if/when the user wants to proceed. **DMS battery display fixed via `services.upower.enable` — root cause found, verified, pushed; laptop/natalie-laptop still need their own switch (2026-08-16, session 77).**
- User reported laptop and natalie-laptop showed no battery % in DMS's top-bar pill or Settings page. Root cause: `services.upower.enable` was never set on any DE module — Plasma auto-enables `upowerd` as a hidden dependency, but bare compositors like niri/Hyprland don't, the same class of gap the existing `accounts-daemon`/`power-profiles-daemon` workaround already covers. Confirmed live (`systemctl status upower` → "could not be found") before fixing, rather than guessed.
- Fixed in `modules/desktop-environments/niri.nix` (live DE on all 3 desktop hosts) and mirrored into `hyprland.nix` (same latent gap, though unwired to any host). Verified via `nixos-dry-run` on gaming (diff showed `etc-UPower-UPower.conf` added, recommendation `switch`), `de-smoke-check` on hyprland (PASS), and `deep-eval-check` across all 4 hosts (PASS). Committed `ae6e428`, pushed.
- **gaming applied live by the user** (this is where the dry-run ran); **laptop and natalie-laptop still need their own `nh os switch`/`nh os boot`** — user is applying those manually, treat as self-managed. See memory `project_dms_battery_upower_fix`.

**gaming: printer invisible in Zen Browser (and every other app) diagnosed + fixed live, new `cups-browsed` discovery gap found — no repo change (2026-08-15, session 75).**
- User reported Zen Browser's print dialog couldn't see the Canon TS9500 despite it being powered on and networked. `printer-diagnose` skill run against gaming: avahi discovery ✅, `cups-browsed.conf` config ✅ (`CreateIPPPrinterQueues Everywhere` still active), but `lpstat -p -d` showed no permanent queue — the familiar `cups-browsed`↔avahi symptom, not a Zen/browser-specific bug (the print dialog just reads whatever CUPS has).
- Live fix: `sudo systemctl restart cups-browsed` (user ran it themselves, no NOPASSWD sudo on gaming), confirmed ~a minute later via `lpstat -p -d` — `Canon_TS9500_series` now a real, idle queue. Then set it as the **user-level default** via `lpoptions -d Canon_TS9500_series` (no sudo needed; libcups checks the per-user `~/.cups/lpoptions` default before the system-wide one, so this is what GTK/Zen print dialogs pre-select) — confirmed via `lpstat -d`.
- **New trigger identified, distinct from the `841eb05` boot-race fix**: gaming was freshly rebooted, but the printer itself was powered on *after* boot — i.e. after `network-online.target` was already reached and the `cups-browsed-fixup` oneshot unit had already fired. Journal confirmed `cups-browsed` had zero activity between its boot-time restart and the manual fix, meaning it never picked up the printer's mDNS announcement once running. `841eb05`'s fix only covers "network not up yet at cups-browsed's first pass" — it doesn't retroactively catch a device that starts broadcasting later in the same boot. See Known Issues for full detail; no repo fix planned, see Recent Decisions for why.
- User ran the built-in `/auto-mode-setup` CLI command (not a project/global skill — confirmed absent from both `.claude/skills/` and `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/`), which wrote `autoMode.soft_deny`/`autoMode.environment` rules to `~/.claude/settings.json`: force-push and `nixos-rebuild switch`/`nh os switch` still require confirmation even in Auto Mode; the `environment` block documents this repo as public/sops-nix/unprotected-`main`/eval-only-CI and marks the `add-*` skills plus `ci-status`/`audit-config` as routine.
- Then set `permissions.defaultMode: "auto"` in that same global `~/.claude/settings.json`, so Auto Mode is now the startup permission mode for **every** project on this machine, not just this repo. Confirmed this doesn't conflict with CLAUDE.md's Scope-First interview rule — they're different layers (behavioral instruction vs. tool-call permission classifier); `AskUserQuestion`/`Skill` were already unconditionally allowed before this change, in every mode.
- Separately, root-caused the `gemini-cli-0.47.0` "removal" eval warning flagged in session 73's flake bump: it's nixpkgs' new `meta.problems.removal` mechanism (warn-level by nixpkgs default), surfacing Google's own deprecation of Gemini CLI in favor of Antigravity CLI — non-blocking, no nixpkgs `antigravity-cli` package exists yet. User chose to do nothing; `gemini-cli` stays installed in `modules/users.nix` for both `bosko` and `natty` until nixpkgs actually removes the package or a real replacement ships.
- See memory `project_auto_mode_global` for the full detail — don't re-propose scoping the `autoMode` rules to this project's own `.claude/settings.json`, the user explicitly declined that.

**Sweet-Dark→Colloid-Teal-Dark theme rollout fully completed and made declarative end-to-end across all 3 niri hosts/both users (2026-08-09, session 71 — resumes and closes out the mid-session handoff from session 70).**
- **Resumed from the prior session's handoff**: gaming had `nh os boot`'d onto the Colloid migration (`bcd1d60`/`4ab25e1`) and was rebooting. Verified live (generation 372, `who -b` confirmed a real reboot), hand-fixed `~/.config/gtk-{3,4}.0/settings.ini` (dconf doesn't auto-propagate to these files on this system — a known, now-eliminated gap, see below), screenshot-confirmed via `wayland-screenshot`: dark theme + teal Colloid icons rendering correctly in Thunar.
- **Fleet rollout to laptop/natalie-laptop hit two real, unrelated snags, both resolved**: (1) laptop transiently dropped off the network mid-`nh os build` (LAN-only connectivity, zero WAN route, isolated to that host while gaming had full internet) — self-resolved after the user toggled WiFi, confirmed via a straight `ping 1.1.1.1` retry rather than assumed. (2) natalie-laptop's actual graphical session belongs to **natty** (`loginctl`/`who` confirmed), not bosko — the hand-fix I'd applied only touched bosko's own `~/.config` (permission-denied on natty's, mode `0700`, no passwordless sudo there). Drafted a sudo-gated command for the user to run themselves; **this whole class of gap was later closed for free** by the declarative refactor below, which fixed natty's files automatically on the next switch — no manual/sudo workaround needed after all.
- **User then asked to make theme selection declarative for all users** — this was the actual architectural fix, not just finishing the manual rollout. Read Home Manager's own `modules/misc/gtk/{default,gtk3,gtk4,lib}.nix` and `modules/config/home-cursor.nix` source directly (fetched via `nix flake prefetch`) rather than guessing from docs: `gtk.enable = true` with `theme`/`iconTheme`/`colorScheme` set makes gtk3's own module write **both** `xdg.configFile."gtk-3.0/settings.ini"` **and** the equivalent `dconf.settings` from the same source options in one activation step — eliminating the propagation gap that caused 3 separate stale-`settings.ini` incidents in one session. `home.pointerCursor.gtk.enable = true` auto-feeds the existing cursor package/name/size into `gtk.cursorTheme` (confirmed via source), so cursor is declared once instead of duplicated. `gtk.gtk4.theme` explicitly pinned to `gtk.theme` rather than left on HM's `stateVersion`-gated implicit default (silenced a real deprecation warning surfaced during deep-eval). Replaced the old manual `dconf.settings` block + hand-edited `settings.ini` in `modules/desktop-environments/niri.nix` (`944b64b`).
- **First switch attempt on gaming failed activation** (`home-manager-bosko.service`/`home-manager-natty.service`, exit 1) — HM's `checkLinkTargets` correctly refused to silently clobber real pre-existing files at the same paths the new `gtk` module now wants to manage (some from this session's hand-fix stopgap, some — `.gtkrc-2.0`, `gtk-4.0/gtk.css` — predating the session entirely, so not mine to just delete). Fixed with `home-manager.backupFileExtension = "backup"` (`modules/home-manager.nix`, `835a078`) — a general fix, since every user on every niri host was about to hit the identical collision on their first switch onto `944b64b`.
- **Verified live on all 3 hosts after the user switched each one**: symlinks (not stale plain files) at all managed paths, correct `Colloid-Teal-Dark`/`breeze_cursors`/`prefer-dark` content, both `home-manager-bosko.service` **and** `home-manager-natty.service` succeeded on natalie-laptop (closing the natty gap above for real), pre-existing files preserved as `.backup` on gaming exactly as designed. Screenshot-confirmed on gaming (dark Colloid theme + teal icons in Thunar, consistent panel accents); laptop's screenshot attempt hit an unrelated display/DPMS quirk (niri reported the output logically on, `grim` still returned a flat gray frame) — not chased further, config-level verification stands on its own. Both `nixos-dry-run` and 4-host `deep-eval-check` passed clean before each commit, per this repo's standing bar.

**Five independent fixes/features landed 2026-08-03 → 2026-08-08 (sessions 68b-69): weekly PR review, financeguru bump, pi-hole/famdash IP drift, niri media-key routing, vpn-server MTU + a gaming health-watchdog. Router-as-NixOS decision made in a sibling repo, not yet built.**
- **Weekly `improve-system` PR #6 reviewed and merged (2026-08-03)** — first full live run of the `review-improve-system-pr` skill: guardrail check passed (2 `SKILL.md`-only files), diff reviewed, user confirmed via AskUserQuestion, squash-merged (`00ab3f1`). Removed a stale `audit-config` carve-out (gaming's SDDM auto-login exception, no longer true) and hardened `rollback`'s confirm gate to match `nixos-gc`. Memory `project_review_improve_system_pr_skill` already updated to reflect this confirmed end-to-end run — don't re-flag as "not yet run against a live PR."
- **`financeguru` flake input bumped** (`21b848e`) — routine `/bump-input` + dry-run + commit+push, no config-level changes beyond the lock bump.
- **pi-hole/famdash Raspberry Pi IP drift fixed after a power outage (2026-08-05, `ac1c4ad`)** — both Pis re-leased on new DHCP addresses, and famdash landed on pi-hole's *old* slot (pi-hole `.20→.19`, famdash `.21→.20`), verified live via mDNS/HTTP/DNS-port fingerprinting rather than guessed. Surfaced a real live bug while fixing it: `modules/desktop-networking.nix` hardcoded `.20` as the primary LAN DNS resolver, which now belonged to famdash — all three desktop hosts had silently been falling back to the `1.1.1.1` secondary since the outage, **bypassing pi-hole ad-blocking fleet-wide**. Fixed in the same commit (also updated `hosts.json`, `ssh.nix`, the `new-host` skill template). Neither Pi has a router-side DHCP reservation (Xfinity gateway has no admin access) — this can and will recur on the next outage; noted in `hosts.json` for next time.
- **niri media keys now route to whichever MPRIS player was most recently active (2026-08-06, `8feeb46`)** — matches KDE Plasma's pause/play behavior. `/research` (10 sources) confirmed `playerctld` (the daemon bundled with `playerctl`) is the standard fix: without it, plain `playerctl play-pause` targets whichever player D-Bus enumerates first, not the one actually in use. Added `spawn-at-startup "playerctld" "daemon"` to the shared `dotfiles/common/configs/niri-config.kdl` — covers gaming/laptop/natalie-laptop and both users in one edit, no new package needed.
- **vpn-server MTU black-hole bug found and fixed — root cause of "random gaming stutter through the VPN" (2026-08-07, `d00e0fd`)** — user reported periodic drops and asked if it was a normal Oracle Free-tier quirk; it wasn't. Oracle's route cache misreports MTU 9000 for the default route (their internal jumbo-frame fabric) despite the physical NIC being 1500, so `wg-quick` auto-detected `wg0` at MTU 8920 server-side with no explicit clamp — return traffic could exceed the real path MTU, get IP-fragmented, and fragmented UDP is exactly what ISPs/NAT silently drop (worst on latency-sensitive gaming traffic, invisible in browsing). Client side (`modules/vpn.nix`) already clamped to 1380; the server never got the match. Added `mtu = 1380` to `hosts/vpn-server/configuration.nix`, deep-eval verified across all 4 hosts, deployed via `remote-rebuild` (boot+reboot — `switch` would drop the SSH session mid-activation on this host), **confirmed live**: `wg0` now reports MTU 1380, gaming's tunnel self-recovered with a fresh handshake, no manual client restart needed. See memory `project_vpn_server_mtu_fix`.
- **Follow-up vpn-server audit + two process-gap fixes (2026-08-07)** — a full `audit-config` + live health check + memory cross-check found vpn-server in good shape (no critical/high findings, secrets clean, firewall minimal, huge resource headroom); flagged two Medium items worth acting on: no automated patch cadence, and no monitoring/alerting. Built both: **(A) a gaming-only systemd user timer** (`hosts/gaming/environment.nix`, `1a4d440`) checking vpn-server health + gaming's tunnel every 20 min via `vpn-status.sh`, firing a `notify-send` desktop alert on hard failures — dry-run clean, **not yet switched live** (see Next Steps). **(B) a cloud routine** (`vpn-server nixpkgs staleness check`, weekly Mondays ~9am ET, first run 2026-08-10, id `trig_01MhNjyYdYx5VXTbziiA39Ys`) opens a GitHub issue if the nixpkgs pin on vpn-server falls 21+ days stale — read-only, no deploy attempts, exists only outside git (won't show in `git log`). See memory `project_vpn_server_monitoring`.
- **Router-as-NixOS decision made, in a sibling repo — nothing built here yet.** User asked which router fits this repo's declarative/Claude-managed workflow best; a 10-source `/research` pass concluded the best fit is running **NixOS itself** as the router (nftables + systemd-networkd) on N100/N150 mini-PC hardware, not OPNsense/VyOS/a dedicated appliance — same flake, same `nixos-dry-run`/`deep-eval-check` skills, eval-time config validation, no new credential surface for Claude to manage. Real evidence: a Beelink N150 has run someone's home network "flawlessly" for months this way, and a separate ex-VyOS author migrated to NixOS specifically for this reason. Honest caveats carried forward too: nftables docs are poor for beginners, systemd-networkd can silently leave stale config behind, and one source explicitly warns "NixOS shouldn't be the first choice for a firewall/router" for someone less committed to the ecosystem. Findings written to `~/projects/home-lab/docs/router-options.md` (**outside this repo** — a separate home-lab docs project), superseding that doc's 2026-05-22 pick (N100+OPNsense) for this specific declarative/AI-manageable axis. **No hardware bought, no `hosts/router/` scaffold started** — this is a decision-in-progress the user is "sitting with," not yet a project in this repo. If it moves forward, the `new-host` skill is the documented next step.
- **Live-verified during this close (session 69)**: gaming's running generation (`system-369-link`, built 2026-08-06 11:08:27 — after both `ac1c4ad` and `8feeb46`) has `playerctld` genuinely running (confirms the niri media-key fix is live). The pi-hole nameserver fix is very likely live in the same generation but **can't be directly confirmed via `/etc/resolv.conf`** right now — gaming's WireGuard tunnel is currently up, and `wg-quick`'s own `DNS = 1.1.1.1, 8.8.8.8` setting (`modules/vpn.nix:33`) overwrites `resolv.conf` for as long as the tunnel is connected, independent of `networking.nameservers`. This means **pi-hole ad-blocking is bypassed by design whenever the VPN tunnel is active on gaming**, not just during the drift-outage window `ac1c4ad` fixed — worth knowing if "why isn't pi-hole blocking anything" comes up again while the VPN is on. Not treated as a bug to fix in this close; flagged for awareness only.

**Pinchflat's full-tunnel VPN conflict found and fixed — all previously-failing downloads now confirmed working end-to-end (2026-08-03, session 68).**
- User reported yt-dlp failing with "Sign in to confirm you're not a bot" on some Pinchflat downloads and suspected the VPN. Confirmed directly: gaming's WireGuard client (`modules/vpn.nix`) is a deliberate full tunnel (`allowedIPs = 0.0.0.0/0`), so Pinchflat's yt-dlp traffic was exiting via the Oracle Cloud VPS IP (`150.136.232.63`, ASN 31898) — `curl -4 ifconfig.me` from gaming literally returned the VPN server's own IP. YouTube flags datacenter/hosting ASNs as bot traffic far more aggressively than residential IPs; this had nothing to do with cookies (yt-dlp's own suggested fix).
- **Fix 1** (`61fe874`): UID-scoped `ip rule` (`uidrange <pinchflat-uid> lookup main`, pref 100) added via `networking.wg-quick.interfaces.wg0.postUp`/`postDown` in `hosts/gaming/pinchflat.nix`, resolving the UID at runtime via `$(id -u pinchflat)` since NixOS system users get dynamically assigned UIDs not knowable at eval time. Routes only Pinchflat's traffic via the normal LAN gateway (`enp4s0`), bypassing wg0, while the rest of gaming stays fully tunneled.
- **Fix 1 alone wasn't enough — surfaced a second, non-obvious bug.** The UID exception made Pinchflat's return traffic arrive on `enp4s0` while the *global* default route still points at wg0. NixOS's firewall enforces its own strict reverse-path check (`networking.firewall.checkReversePath`, default `true`) **independent of the `rp_filter` sysctl** (which was already loose) — it dropped the return SYN-ACKs as spoofed, hanging every connection in `SYN-SENT` forever. Root-caused via `ss -tn state syn-sent` plus a control test (`curl --interface enp4s0` to `1.1.1.1` as a non-pinchflat user reproduced the identical hang, ruling out anything UID-rule-specific). **Fix 2** (`4fad567`): `networking.firewall.checkReversePath = "loose";` on gaming, matching a pattern `hosts/vpn-server/configuration.nix` already used for its own asymmetric NAT routing.
- **Confirmed fully live**, not just dry-run-clean: after both switches, all 3 previously-failing videos (`GjDlPhwsdCs`, `d3k-NyvhG14`, `SlAaL995-DM`) downloaded successfully — video, thumbnail, `.nfo`, and `.info.json` all present in `/mnt/media/YouTube`, Oban job logs show `"state":"success"`. This is Pinchflat's first-ever confirmed successful download since being added (session 67) — the Jellyfin-library side of the pipeline is still unverified separately, see Known Issues/Next Steps.
- See memory `project_pinchflat_vpn_split_tunnel` for the full diagnostic detail — both fixes are required together; the routing rule alone produces a silent hang, not a clean error, which is what made this take two rounds to fully resolve.

**Pinchflat (self-hosted YouTube archiver) added to gaming and wired to Jellyfin — confirmed live and running (2026-08-03, session 67).**
- `/research` (9 sources) found Pinchflat is Docker-only upstream, but nixpkgs already ships a native `services.pinchflat` module (PR #364135, merged 2025-03-26, confirmed present at v2025.9.26 in both unstable/stable). Jellyfin integration is convention-based (NFO + a built-in "Media Center" output-path preset), not an API push — and there's no way to declare channels/sources/media-profiles in Nix at all (a `loadModels`-style FR was explicitly rejected upstream as infeasible); all of that has to happen through Pinchflat's own web UI after deployment.
- **New `hosts/gaming/pinchflat.nix`**: `mediaDir = /mnt/media/YouTube` (a new folder on the existing shared media SSD, not the module's own `/var/lib` default), `group = "media"` (joins Jellyfin's existing shared group from `jellyfin-server.nix` instead of standing up a separate `pinchflat` group), port 8945 firewalled to LAN (`enp4s0`) + WireGuard only (matches Jellyfin's own scoping), `secretsFile` via a new sops secret `pinchflat-env` (`SECRET_KEY_BASE`, `openssl rand -hex 64`) in `secrets/hosts/gaming.yaml` — chosen over the module's `selfhosted = true` weak-secret escape hatch to stay consistent with this repo's sops-everywhere convention. Wired into `flake.nix`'s gaming module list next to `jellyfin-server.nix`. `nixos-dry-run` clean (+367 MiB: pinchflat + yt-dlp/apprise/deno/atomicparsley, no kernel/DM changes, `switch` recommended). Committed `b76e09b`, pushed.
- **Confirmed live via direct verification this close** (not just assumed from the dry-run): gaming was fully rebooted today at 15:11 and then switched again at 15:31 — `systemctl status pinchflat` shows `active (running)`, `/mnt/media/YouTube` exists with correct `bosko:media` 2775 ownership, and `curl localhost:8945` returns HTTP 200.
- Added first source, **Yes Theory** (`@YesTheory`), and hit two real gotchas, both root-caused with live evidence rather than guessed: (1) `download_cutoff_date` was `2026-07-27` (~1wk before add) — 0 videos matched since their last upload was 8 days prior; confirmed working-as-designed (cutoff is a hard filter, not cosmetic), user chose to leave it and just wait for their next upload rather than backfill. (2) `collection_type` came back as `playlist` instead of `channel`, even after deleting/re-adding with the explicit channel-ID URL — matches a real, still-open, unresolved upstream bug (`kieraneglin/pinchflat#607`, confirmed via `gh api` to still be open with no fix merged; intermittent, no reliable repro even for the maintainer). User accepted leaving it as-is — the channel ID itself resolved correctly so indexing/downloads/NFO are unaffected, the only confirmed downside is missing series-level banner/fanart art in Jellyfin (playlists don't get that from YouTube).
- Follow-up Q&A resolved inline (no separate research skill invocation, just targeted `WebSearch`/`gh` checks): **SponsorBlock** — Pinchflat's native support is "mark chapters" only (non-destructive), added v2025.1.3; true segment removal needs a custom yt-dlp `--sponsorblock-remove` config, not exposed as a first-class toggle — user enabled marking. **"Use my subscriptions as a source"** — not possible; only channel/playlist source types exist, and the maintainer has a standing "DO NOT USE YOUTUBE COOKIES" warning (issue #291) since that's exactly the auth subscriptions would need. **New Jellyfin "YouTube" library settings decided** (not yet applied — library doesn't exist yet): embedded-titles-over-filename ON, online metadata/image downloaders (TVDB/TMDB) OFF, embedded image extraction ON, save-artwork-to-media-folders ON, auto-merge-series-across-folders OFF, trickplay ON (not during scan, not saved next to media), chapter image extraction ON (since SponsorBlock chapters are enabled) but explicitly not during the scan — deferred to the nightly scheduled task, since gaming doubles as the user's actual gaming rig and inline generation would compete for CPU at unpredictable times.
- **Discovered live while verifying this close, not from the conversation**: a second source, `@66Samus`, is mid-indexing right now (`systemctl status pinchflat` shows an active `yt-dlp --simulate` job against it) — added directly through Pinchflat's web UI outside this session, no other context on it.
- **Not yet done**: never confirmed the Media Profile actually has "Download NFO data"/"Download Series Images" toggled on (only discussed); the Jellyfin "YouTube" library hasn't been created yet (settings decided, not applied); no video has downloaded yet for Yes Theory (still waiting on their next upload) — first real end-to-end file-to-Jellyfin verification is still pending. See Next Steps.

**Three smaller skill improvements landed the same day (2026-07-30, session 66).**
- **`nixos-dry-run` now prints a switch-vs-boot recommendation** after every dry-run (commit `2b66df9`) — scans the diff for kernel/initrd/bootloader/firmware keywords (always `boot`) vs. display-manager/graphics-driver/compositor keywords (`boot`, to protect the live session) vs. everything else (`switch`, safe immediately). Still never runs the recommended command itself — stays a hand-off per the standing sudo-gated rule. Heuristic is keyword-based, not exhaustive; documented as a Gotcha.
- **`skill-suggestion`/`skill-upgrade`/`skill-audit` now scope their transcript scan to "since I last ran" instead of an arbitrary recent-N-file window**, via two new shared helpers (`find-last-skill-invocation.sh`, `list-transcripts-since.sh` in `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/lib/`). `skill-audit` previously did no log-check at all. `skill-suggestion` was also changed to propose *every* viable candidate instead of narrowing to one "best" pick. `session-closer`'s own transcript scan got the same fix — its `scan-session.sh` had only ever read the single most-recently-modified `.jsonl`, contradicting its own `SKILL.md`'s claim of reading every transcript since its last run; this was a real, previously-unnoticed bug. Commits `657e697`, `a52f2d7`.
- **New skill `printer-diagnose`** (project-local) — automates the avahi-discovery/cups-browsed-config/lpstat-queue diagnostic sequence that had been done by hand across 4+ separate sessions (gaming, then natalie-laptop repeatedly), including the known `cups-browsed`↔`avahi-daemon` `BindsTo` race. Built, smoke-tested live on gaming (correctly reported a discovery-layer FAIL — printer was off-network, not a skill defect), committed and pushed as `f808b19`. See memory `project_printer_browsed_fix` / `project_flatpak_cups_skill_gap_watch`.

**Wine/Lutris tray-icon bridge added, then reverted same day — breaks DMS's own tray entirely (2026-07-29/30, commits `812aa5f` add → `74d627b` revert).**
- Wine has no native StatusNotifierItem implementation, so Battle.net's tray icon fell back to a floating XEmbed window under DMS's SNI-only tray. `snixembed` was added (package + `spawn-sh-at-startup` in niri) to proxy XEmbed→SNI.
- **Confirmed live it breaks everything**: `snixembed` hard-requires *exclusive* ownership of `org.kde.StatusNotifierWatcher`, doesn't properly relay real SNI items to DMS's own watcher, and DMS self-elects into the same role — whichever wins the boot-time race locks the other out. Killing the running `snixembed` process instantly restored all 4 real tray icons (Vesktop, Steam, etc.). No packaged alternative exists (`xembedsniproxy` isn't in nixpkgs). Reverted in both `modules/desktop-environments/niri.nix` and `dotfiles/common/configs/niri-config.kdl`; Battle.net is back to its pre-existing floating-window behavior. See memory `project_snixembed_dms_tray_conflict` — **don't re-add.**

**docx/odt mimetype fix (permanent) + niri ScreenCast/Screenshot portal fix (permanent) + a one-off stuck-CIFS-file workaround (2026-07-29/30, commits `7bedfb3`, `19f7e57`).**
- **docx/odt → OnlyOffice, not xarchiver** (`7bedfb3`): an earlier blind copy of xarchiver's full `.desktop` mimetype list had swept up `.docx`/`.odt` too (they're zip containers), so double-clicking a Word doc launched the archive manager instead — surfaced on natalie-laptop as "permission denied." Confirmed live (`xdg-mime query default`, `gio open` both returned `xarchiver.desktop`) before fixing. This is shared config (`dotfiles/common/configs/home.nix`), so it affects both users on all 3 desktop hosts.
- **niri ScreenCast/Screenshot now routed to the gnome portal backend, not gtk** (`19f7e57`): a `/research` fan-out (7 sources) found `xdg-desktop-portal-gtk` (forced as niri's sole default in an earlier commit, for Flatpak GTK theming) doesn't implement the ScreenCast/Screenshot interfaces at all — niri only supports those via `xdg-desktop-portal-gnome`. Fixed by keeping `gtk` as the general default but routing those two interfaces to `gnome` specifically. Confirmed live on gaming after switch + portal-daemon restart (`xdg-desktop-portal.service` doesn't reread config on its own — needs a restart or fresh login).
- **A second, unrelated "permission denied" on one specific file** (`mock-interview-questions.docx`) survived even after the mimetype fix — root-caused through an extensive live debugging session (Thunar launch mechanism, `gio open`/`gio launch`, `strace` showing kernel-level `EACCES` on a plain `O_RDONLY` open, `smbstatus`/`locking.tdb` all clean server-side) to a stuck SMB3 durable-handle/lease on gaming's `smbd`, left over from an earlier crashed OnlyOffice session, that survived both a full `smbd` restart and a client reboot. **Workaround, not a root-cause fix**: gave the file a fresh inode (renamed the original aside, recreated a copy with the same content/perms) — confirmed working. Original preserved at `/srv/shared/*.stuck-2026-07-30` then deleted once confirmed. Not a repo/config change.
- **Both the docx mimetype fix and the CIFS hostname fix were deployed to natalie-laptop and confirmed working the same day** — the user ran `nh os switch` there and retested; double-click now opens OnlyOffice correctly (docx fix) and the `/srv/shared` mount resolves and connects (CIFS fix). Not pending — see memory `project_docx_onlyoffice_xarchiver_bug` / `project_shared_folder_samba`.

**gaming static IP now confirmed live after reboot (2026-07-29, commit `7b74f87`, confirmed same evening).** The `gaming-static` NetworkManager profile (`hosts/gaming/networking.nix`, pinning `enp4s0` to `10.0.0.251/24`) is the active connection post-reboot — verified the live IP matches both the profile and `hosts.json`'s existing record. No drift risk from DHCP lease churn anymore. `laptop`/`natalie-laptop` stay on DHCP by deliberate choice (WiFi SSID-scoping complexity, memory `project_wifi_static_ip_declined`).

**CIFS bare-hostname resolution fix for the `/srv/shared` mount (2026-07-29, commit `baad313`).** `mount.cifs` on laptop/natalie-laptop was failing with "could not resolve address for gaming" — `mdns4_minimal` only resolves `.local`-suffixed names, and no DNS/hosts entry existed for the bare hostname (`gaming.local` mDNS was also separately found broken, not chased further). Fixed with a static `networking.extraHosts` entry (`10.0.0.251 gaming`) in `modules/shared-folder-client.nix`. Verified via `shared-module-check` (all 4 hosts). Confirmed working after natalie-laptop's rebuild — see memory `project_shared_folder_samba`.

**New `/srv/shared` Samba share (gaming as server, laptop/natalie-laptop as CIFS clients) for natty and bosko, symlinked to `~/Shared` in Thunar on all 3; committed, not yet applied on any host (2026-07-29, session 63).**
- User's first ask was a folder both accounts could share, visible in file browsers. Scoped via **AskUserQuestion** (skipped the full `/interview` ceremony — a single, well-bounded feature) down to: all 3 desktop hosts, both users full read/write, path `/srv/shared`, visibility via a home-dir symlink. First implementation built it as a **plain local group-permissioned folder on all 3 hosts independently** — but the user then asked mid-session whether it synced across machines, and once told it didn't, explicitly said no, they wanted it centrally hosted/synced. **Redone as a real network share**: gaming hosts it via Samba (already the always-on host, runs Jellyfin), laptop and natalie-laptop CIFS-mount it; guest access (no password, trusted home LAN); auto-mount at boot via `x-systemd.automount` + `nofail` so client boots never block/fail if gaming is off.
- **`modules/shared-folder.nix`** (in `desktopModules`, all 3 hosts): now just `users.groups.shared = {}` — the group both server and client sides reference by name.
- **New `hosts/gaming/samba-shared.nix`** (gaming only, mirrors the existing `jellyfin-server.nix` host-specific-file convention): `systemd.tmpfiles.rules` creates `/srv/shared` (mode `2770`, setgid); `services.samba` (`openFirewall = true`) serves it as share `shared` — guest-ok, `force group = shared`, `create mask 0660`/`directory mask 0770` so files written over the network still land group-owned.
- **New `modules/shared-folder-client.nix`** (laptop + natalie-laptop, added directly to their flake module lists — `laptopModules` and natalie-laptop's inline list, not `desktopModules`, since gaming doesn't need it): CIFS `fileSystems."/srv/shared"` pointing at `//gaming/shared`, client-side translated to `root:shared 0770` (`uid=0,gid=shared,file_mode=0770,dir_mode=0770,noperm`) so local `shared`-group membership governs access regardless of server-side ownership. Pulls in `pkgs.cifs-utils` explicitly (`mount.cifs` helper) rather than relying on any auto-detection.
- **`modules/users.nix`**: `"shared"` added to both bosko's and natty's `extraGroups` (unchanged from the first draft).
- **`dotfiles/common/configs/home.nix`**: unchanged from the first draft — `file."Shared".source = config.lib.file.mkOutOfStoreSymlink "/srv/shared"` still works as-is, since `/srv/shared` is now the identical mount/data path on every host regardless of server/client role.
- **Verified**: `deep-eval-check` all 4 hosts PASS after each edit (had to `git add` new/changed files first both times — untracked files are invisible to flake evaluation, a recurring gotcha); `nixos-dry-run` on gaming clean for the final design too (+499 KiB — adds `smb.conf`, `samba-smbd`/`nmbd`/`winbindd` units, `cifs-utils`, a `mount.cifs` setuid wrapper pulled in automatically by the samba module itself even on the server side). Committed as `489432a` (superseded local-only draft, kept in history) — **the Samba/CIFS rework is a separate, not-yet-committed set of edits as of this write-up**; see this close's commit step for the final hash. **Not applied on any host yet** — activation needs `sudo` (`nh os boot`) which the user runs themselves per the standing sudo-gated hand-off pattern, then a reboot on all 3 hosts (gaming for the group/Samba server, laptop/natalie-laptop for the group/CIFS client mount).
