**Sudo-gated `switch`/rebuild skill steps now hand off to the user instead of attempting the privileged command directly; a real `fleet-rollout` vpn-server deploy bug fixed alongside it (2026-07-28, session 61).**
- User asked whether any skills that perform an actual rebuild/switch (not just a dry-run) should be removed, since Claude can't supply an interactive sudo password. Audited every skill that touches `nixos-rebuild switch`/`nh os switch`: `rollback` and `nixos-gc` already handed off their privileged step correctly (documented Gotchas, not blind failures), and `remote-rebuild` works end-to-end because `vpn-server` alone has passwordless sudo. Only `fleet-rollout` had a real, unpatched gap.
- **Fixed `fleet-rollout` step 5** (`.claude/skills/fleet-rollout/SKILL.md`): the switch step now hands `nh os switch`/`nixos-rebuild switch --target-host` off to the user for every host except `vpn-server` (no NOPASSWD rule on the local host or gaming/laptop/natalie-laptop), and made explicit that this pause applies even in Training Mode **OFF** — unattended/`/loop`-scheduled runs still stall here, same as `flake-update-verify`'s mandatory commit gate. Added a Gotchas section (the skill previously had none).
- **Found and fixed a second, independent bug while in there**: `fleet-rollout` was telling `vpn-server` to deploy via `nixos-rebuild switch --target-host`, but the `remote-rebuild` skill's own Gotchas already document that `switch` over SSH drops the connection mid-activation on that host (networking restart tears down the pipe) and leaves firewall/NAT state inconsistent. Corrected `fleet-rollout` to invoke `remote-rebuild`'s actual `boot` + reboot method for `vpn-server` instead.
- **Fixed `rollback`'s Step 3**, which said "Run rollback" directly above the `sudo` command — contradicting its own bottom-of-file Gotcha (which already noted 9+ past sessions where this exact command was mis-attempted via Bash before failing). Rewrote Step 3 as an explicit hand-off instruction; Step 4 now reuses the existing unprivileged `rollback.sh` helper instead of issuing a second `sudo` call. Bumped skill version `0.2.0`→`0.3.0`.
- **Considered and rejected adding a NOPASSWD sudo rule** to remove this friction. `nixos-rebuild switch` activates an arbitrary system config — effectively unrestricted root code execution — so even a rule scoped to just that command would remove the last human checkpoint against a compromised/prompt-injected agent session. Decision: keep the hand-off pattern as the standing design; not revisited without a specific reason.
- Saved memory `feedback_sudo_gated_skill_steps` — a reference note (pointing at `rollback`/`nixos-gc`/`fleet-rollout` as the correct shape) for any future skill step that touches `switch` or another privileged command. Commit `595b759`.

**Catch-up note:** three commits from an earlier, separately-run session today (`a1493d3`, `87531b8`, `cb7e495`) landed between the 2026-07-27 close (`81e5637`) and this session without their own `/session-closer` run — narrated below from commit messages only, no transcript available for them. This is the same gap pattern already flagged once in the 2026-07-26 catch-up (see memory `project_session_closer_gap_202607`, which said to surface a repeat directly rather than silently repeating the workaround) — **surfacing it now**: two same-day, unclosed sessions in a row is worth a lower-friction habit (e.g. running `/session-closer` at the end of each sitting rather than only at literal end-of-day) rather than relying on catch-up closes to keep reconstructing accurately.
- **`fix(gaming): disable amd_pstate to stop CPPC probe-fail log spam`** (`a1493d3`) — gaming's BIOS (ASRock X570 Phantom Gaming 4, P3.40) reports zeroed CPPC frequency tables for the 3700X, so `amd_pstate` fails to probe on every core at boot and falls back to `acpi-cpufreq` anyway; added `boot.kernelParams = [ "amd_pstate=disable" ]` to `hosts/gaming/environment.nix` to stop the log spam. Not yet rebuilt/rebooted on gaming — see Known Issues.
- **`feat(skills): add boot-error-triage skill`** (`87531b8`) — new global skill generalizing the amd_pstate/BIOS root-causing workflow above into a reusable pattern: severity-filtered journal triage, hardware/firmware cross-reference, then an external lookup before proposing a fix. Distinct from the existing `journal` skill, which only tails a known service name. Not yet live in `~/.claude` — see Known Issues.
- **`docs(skills): add absolute-path gotcha to git-commit and git-push`** (`cb7e495`) — both skills' scripts had been run with a guessed project-relative path instead of the actual home-symlinked absolute path, 404ing with exit 127 (happened twice: git-push 2026-07-25, git-commit 2026-07-27); added a Gotchas note to both `SKILL.md`s. Not yet live in `~/.claude` — see Known Issues.

**natalie-laptop's nvidia driver fix confirmed working via a live boot-log check; an unrelated SSH host-key mismatch surfaced and deliberately not bypassed (2026-07-27, session 60).**
- **Verified live on natalie-laptop** (this conversation went local on the host itself, not over SSH) that both of an earlier unclosed session's nvidia fixes actually resolved the issue: `journalctl -k -b 0` shows `NVRM: loading NVIDIA UNIX x86_64 Kernel Module 580.173.02` bound to the MX350 (`0000:2d:00.0`), `nvidia-smi` reports `580.173.02, NVIDIA GeForce MX350` live, and `systemctl --failed` is empty — no GSP-firmware probe failure, no RmInitAdapter failure. Only benign warning-level lines remain (proprietary-module taint notices, `[drm] No compatible format found`/`Cannot find any crtc or sizes`, expected for a secondary/offload GPU not driving a display).
- Those two fixes themselves — closing the open kernel module (`ee07039`, GSP firmware needs Turing+, this is Pascal) and pinning the package to the legacy 580.xx branch (`c22737e`, since 595.84 silently dropped Pascal support) — were made in an earlier session today not captured in this conversation's transcript; narrated here from their commit messages only, not first-hand. A third commit (`140d33d`, adding simple-scan to natalie-laptop) landed in that same unclosed session.
- **Hit and did not bypass an SSH host-key mismatch** trying to reach natalie-laptop remotely from gaming (`Host key verification failed`, key `SHA256:gRCoYTd74vMWkYJagu2mvumD9EzOlgGQwoZmLh7GkR0`) before realizing the conversation was already running locally on natalie-laptop, making the SSH path moot for this check. The mismatch itself was never diagnosed (stale `known_hosts` entry vs. genuine host re-key) — see Known Issues.
- Also noticed kernel log timestamps show `Oct 08` while `uptime -s` reports the real date (`2026-07-27`) — this is the already-documented RTC/CMOS-battery clock skew (sessions 57/58), not a new issue.
- **`secret-scan` false-alarmed a "leaked password hash in public git history" finding during the routine session-close scan — traced, found to be a false positive, and the scanner fixed.** 20 commits under `dotfiles/common/modules/users.nix` (pre-rename, pre-sops-migration plaintext `$6$` hashes for bosko/natty) showed up in the history pass. Initially treated as a real gap in the 2026-06-15/2026-07-02 purges (session 4/27) and escalated to the user with a proposed `git filter-repo --replace-text` + force-push — but before acting, checked reachability first and found **all 20 commits existed only inside two local `git stash` entries** (`stash@{0}`, `stash@{1}`), unreachable from any branch or tag and never transmitted by `git push` — i.e. never actually public. Root cause: `secret-scan.sh`'s history pass used `git rev-list --all`, which includes `refs/stash` — the same "`--all` range footgun" already documented for a different script in session 50 (line ~72 of this file), just not yet applied here. **Corrected the user immediately once found**, dropped both stale stashes (predated the `dotfiles/common/modules/`→`modules/` rename, no current value), and fixed `secret-scan.sh` to use `git rev-list --all --exclude=refs/stash` with a comment explaining why. Re-ran the scan clean. A safety bundle (`~/nixos-pre-hash-purge-rewrite.bundle`) was made before the false alarm was resolved — harmless, left in place as a valid full backup, no action needed on it.
- **Both session 58 fixes (`6877f4b` cups-browsed config, `21df99c` wg0 autostart=false) are now confirmed active on natalie-laptop** — the user reported VPN starting off and manual `vpn-on` working as intended (autostart fix live), and `/etc/cups/cups-browsed.conf` was read live and correctly contains `CreateIPPPrinterQueues Everywhere` (previously baked empty due to the stale-checkout timing bug). The rebuild+reboot Known Issues item for both is now resolved.
- **Solved the open thread from session 58** ("Kate could print but CUPS had zero destinations — ask what Kate's dialog actually showed"): **not** a stale memory or print-to-PDF — Kate's native system CUPS client (2.4.19) does live DNS-SD printer discovery inside `cupsGetDests()`, so it can show a network IPP printer before any permanent queue exists. OnlyOffice's print dialog (its own bundled binary, running in the `buildFHSEnv`/bubblewrap sandbox already documented in `modules/fonts.nix` for font issues) does not do live DNS-SD discovery — it only shows printers that already exist as real, permanent CUPS destinations. At the time of this session, `lpstat -p -d` again showed zero permanent queues even though `CreateIPPPrinterQueues Everywhere` was correctly configured and the printer was fully discoverable via `avahi-browse`/`lpinfo -v`/`lpstat -e`.
- **New root cause found for why the permanent queue kept failing to materialize despite correct config**: `cups-browsed.service` has `BindsTo=avahi-daemon.service` / `PartOf=avahi-daemon.service`, so it gets reset every time avahi-daemon restarts. Journal showed cups-browsed bouncing every few minutes to hours on this host — plausibly driven by the same network/VPN churn session 58 fixed — never giving it a clean-enough run to finish instantiating the permanent queue. **Fix applied live, not via repo change**: `sudo systemctl restart cups-browsed`, confirmed ~30s later via `lpstat -p -d` that `Canon_TS9500_series` now exists as a real queue, then confirmed directly in OnlyOffice's print dialog that it now shows the printer. No config change was needed or made — this was a live service-state issue, not a config bug. See Known Issues for the residual recurrence risk (cups-browsed's `BindsTo=avahi-daemon.service` coupling is unchanged and could repeat this if avahi restarts frequently again).
- Could not use `sudo` directly in this session (no passwordless sudo, Bash tool can't supply an interactive password) — all `sudo` commands were handed to the user to run themselves via the `!` prefix, consistent with the same constraint noted in session 58.

**natalie-laptop's three reported issues (clock desync, VPN kills internet, printer invisible in OnlyOffice) all root-caused directly on the host via SSH; printer + VPN fixes committed, RTC confirmed as unfixable hardware (2026-07-27, session 58).**
- **Time desync = dying CMOS/RTC battery, confirmed reproducible across multiple boots.** `journalctl -u chronyd -b -1`/`-b` both show the identical offset (`System clock wrong by 151528561.xxx seconds` ≈ 4.8 years, matching 2021-10-07 → 2026-07-27 exactly) at every boot; `hwclock --show` can't access the hardware clock at all. Expected on this ~5-year-old ASUS X532EQ. Checked NixOS's default chrony config (`makestep 0.1 3`, `rtcautotrim 30`, `rtconutc`) and it's already doing everything reasonable — immediate step-correction (recovers in ~60s once network is up) and best-effort RTC write-back. **No config change made** — the fault is the physical battery; nothing in Nix can repair it. Real fix is a physical CMOS battery replacement.
- **VPN "no internet" root-caused to `wg-quick-wg0` auto-starting at boot and racing WiFi association, not a config difference from gaming/laptop.** Two parallel forks first ruled out a config bug (`modules/vpn.nix` identical across all 3 hosts; `wg-quick` activation logs clean across 5+ boots; no WiFi firmware errors) and a live 15-minute on-host test (continuous ping, DNS, real HTTPS page load, a 5MB download at ~110 Mbps, all through the tunnel) found **zero reproduction** with VPN manually enabled after boot — confirmed working in the user's own apps too. The actual mechanism: `networking.wg-quick.interfaces.wg0.autostart` defaults to `true` (never overridden), so all 3 hosts try to bring the tunnel up at boot; the full-tunnel kill-switch route (table 51820, `suppress_prefixlength 0`) captures **all** traffic — including chrony's own NTP retries — the instant `wg-quick` starts, even before the WireGuard handshake completes. If WiFi is still associating at that exact moment (more likely on this host's older WiFi hardware than gaming's wired connection or laptop's), every packet vanishes into the not-yet-negotiated tunnel with no fallback, and nothing recovers until `vpn-off` tears it down manually. **Fix**: `hosts/natalie-laptop/networking.nix` now sets `networking.wg-quick.interfaces.wg0.autostart = false;` (natalie-laptop only — gaming/laptop haven't shown this failure, so their autostart is left at default). Commit `21df99c`.
- **Printer invisible in OnlyOffice was never an OnlyOffice/Flatpak sandboxing issue** — `lpstat -p -d` showed zero CUPS destinations configured system-wide, meaning every app's print dialog was empty, not just OnlyOffice's. Root cause: the currently-running generation (63, built 2026-07-27 16:50:37, only 54 seconds after commit `6877f4b` landed) was built from a checkout that predated the fix in substance — confirmed via `nix derivation show` that the deployed `cups-browsed.conf` derivation was baked with a genuinely empty `text` field, propagating to a literal 0-byte `/etc/cups/cups-browsed.conf`. `avahi-browse` confirms the printer (Canon TS9500) is fully discoverable via mDNS right now, so discovery itself was never broken. Re-ran `nixos-dry-run` against the current (already-correct) checkout: clean build, `unit-cups-browsed.service` shows as changed, confirming the fix now evaluates correctly. **Open thread, not resolved**: the user said Kate could print to this printer even though CUPS had zero destinations at the time — worth asking what Kate's dialog actually showed (could be print-to-PDF, or predates this regression) next time it comes up.
- **Both fixes are staged (`nh os boot` built and cached) but not yet activated** — `nh os boot` itself needs an interactive sudo password the Bash tool can't supply non-interactively, so the user is running it themselves, followed by their own reboot. See Known Issues / Next Steps.
- **Process note**: this session leaned heavily on the standing rules — two parallel forks for the independent VPN/printer investigations while doing the RTC/chrony analysis directly; AskUserQuestion gated every risky/ambiguous step (rebuild-now vs. dry-run-only, live-VPN-test-now vs. defer, repo-wide vs. natalie-laptop-only autostart fix); background `run_in_background` captures (ping/dmesg/journal/nmcli signal polling) collected live evidence around the user-triggered `vpn-on` moment without blocking the conversation.

**Cloud `improve-system` routine hardened after a second real bug found — its 6-item "backlog" from the past 3 weeks turned out to be a phantom caused by a stale persisted-session baseline; routine rearchitected (persist_session off, reuse-shortcut removed, new self-verifiable auto-apply tier) and live-tested end-to-end — 1 PR merged, pushed (2026-07-27, session 57).**
- **Investigated why the Sunday routine had re-reported the same 6 "structural findings" for 3 straight runs.** All 6 turned out to already be fixed by commit `29e40c1` (2026-07-20), landed ~27 minutes after issue #1 closed — verified directly by reading each of the 6 skill files' current content, not by trusting the auditor's own claim. Root cause: `RemoteTrigger get` showed `persist_session: true` reusing the *same* `persistent_session_id` every week; today's run justified reusing "last week's audit" by claiming no commits touched skill dirs since `8507de3` — but that commit (2026-07-18) predates the actual fix (`29e40c1`, 2026-07-20), so the persisted session's own remembered belief was simply wrong and had been compounding silently for two weeks.
- **First patch (later superseded): a freshness check.** Added an instruction requiring the routine to check the last tracking issue's `closedAt` via `gh` and diff git log against that timestamp before reusing any audit — a targeted fix around the stale-memory bug rather than removing it.
- **Asked the user how to harden the routine toward parity with a live session; all three offered options were chosen, superseding the freshness-check patch:**
  1. Removed the reuse-shortcut entirely — skill-audit now always runs the full fan-out fresh, every run, no caching.
  2. `persist_session` flipped to `false` — each weekly run now starts genuinely stateless, deriving everything from live git/GitHub state instead of carried-over agent memory.
  3. Added a new "self-verifiable structural fixes" auto-apply tier — a skill-audit finding can now be applied (landed as a PR, never merged) if it touches only `SKILL.md`/`scripts/`/`assets/` under a skills dir (never `.nix`) and has a scriptable way to verify; the routine must re-read the current file content itself before touching anything, specifically to avoid repeating today's phantom-finding mistake in either direction.
  4. Confirmed via the `schedule` skill that only one CCR environment exists with no exposed setup-script mechanism — the `nh`/`nix`-unavailable-in-sandbox gap (no real dry-run ever possible in cloud) is not fixable from Claude Code tooling; flagged for the user to check claude.ai → Settings → Environments themselves if they want to pursue it.
- **Live-tested the hardened routine immediately via `RemoteTrigger run`.** All three changes confirmed working: two checkpoints explicitly noted "no persisted history from prior weekly runs" (stateless confirmed); the audit fanned out 5 sub-agents over all 55 skills from scratch (no shortcut); and it found and applied **7 genuinely real drift bugs** — a "four"→"five" standing-rules typo, a false claim that `natty` has no wheel/packages access, a stale `security.audit.rules` description that risked a future false-positive Critical finding, a wrong DE-host roster in `de-smoke-check`, and three hardcoded host rosters replaced with live `jq` enumeration against `.claude/hosts.json`. It also correctly **refused** one sub-agent's false-positive claim (that `git-commit`/`git-push`/`/commit` references were broken) after cross-checking — the "read current file before applying" safeguard working in the opposite direction, preventing a *new* phantom finding rather than perpetuating an old one. Run took ~10.5 minutes (vs ~90s under the old shortcut) — still comfortably inside the 1-hour cron floor.
- **Reviewed and merged PR [#4](https://github.com/CommanderBosko/NixOS/pull/4)** — independently re-verified all 7 claimed fixes against live repo content (not just the PR's own narrative) before approving; all checked out exactly, including cited line numbers. CI ("evaluate all hosts") passed. Squash-merged, commit `bdcf9b6`; local `main` fast-forwarded to match (was 1 commit behind after the squash-merge happened on GitHub only).
- **PENDING**: `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/improve-system/SKILL.md`'s "five standing rules" fix is a global repo-managed skill — needs `nh os boot` + a new session to reach the live `~/.claude/skills/improve-system/SKILL.md`. The other 6 fixes are project-local and already live. Watch the next scheduled fire (2026-08-02 16:06 UTC) to confirm the hardening holds under a real unattended Sunday run, not just this manual test.

**Catch-up close note:** the prior `chore(session)` close was 2026-07-20 (session 52). Six days and 11 commits (`a3d5c59`..`7e6e896`) accumulated across at least 4 unclosed sessions (2026-07-23, -24, -25, -26) before this close ran — the invoking conversation for *this* close itself did no repo work (just `/clear` then `/session-closer`), so everything below is narrated from commit messages + the memory already saved during those sessions, not from a live transcript. Flagging this so the gap doesn't repeat: prefer running `/session-closer` at the end of each individual session rather than letting several stack up.

**`flake-update-verify` gained a mandatory pre-commit gate; Training Mode default flipped ON→OFF (2026-07-26).** Discussed directly with the user whether the loop's per-step pauses were still needed now that deep-eval + auto-revert-on-failure have proven reliable across several incidents (pnpm/electron/rtk). Resolution: flip Training Mode's default to `OFF` so steps 1-5 (memory load, pre-flight, update, verify/deep-eval) run autonomously — but add a **new mandatory `AskUserQuestion` gate before step 6** (commit) that applies in *both* modes and is explicitly carved out of the existing "pre-approved runs skip pauses" gotcha: even a "do the whole thing" invocation still stops and shows the per-input old→new revs + eval verdict before committing+pushing. Rationale (distinct from the general "skip commit confirmation" preference): this loop pushes to the shared `origin/main` remote unattended, a different risk class than a local commit. Commit `7e6e896`.

**Flake inputs bumped, all 4 hosts deep-eval clean (2026-07-26).** `dms` (`8bb7396`→`bab2078`) and `financeguru` (`969b983`→`f7f0549`); nixpkgs/nixpkgs-stable/home-manager/disko/sops-nix/nix-flatpak/nix-colors already at latest, unchanged. Commit `85d82bf`.

**niri cursor theming made comprehensive across shape-protocol/XWayland/Qt rendering (2026-07-25).** Found that niri renders its own context-aware cursor shapes (text I-beam, grab, resize, wait) via the Wayland `cursor-shape-v1` protocol, driven by a top-level `cursor { xcursor-theme; xcursor-size; }` KDL block that didn't exist — only the GTK/dconf `cursor-theme` key was set, so shape-protocol clients fell back to niri's bundled default instead of `breeze_cursors`. Added the `cursor{}` block to `dotfiles/common/configs/niri-config.kdl` and `home.pointerCursor` (`kdePackages.breeze`, `x11.enable = true`, **`gtk.enable` deliberately left off** — HM's `gtk.cursorTheme` force-manages `gtk-3.0`/`gtk-4.0` `settings.ini` and would silently clobber the hand-set Sweet-Dark `gtk-theme-name`/`gtk-icon-theme-name` keys) to `modules/desktop-environments/niri.nix`, for both `bosko` and `natty`. `breeze_cursors` ships inside `kdePackages.breeze`, not `breeze-icons` — confirmed via `nix build` + `find`. Dry-run clean on gaming; **visual confirmation after reboot is still pending** (needs a `wayland-screenshot` check of a text-field I-beam, drag-resize, etc.). Commit `a5a6582`. See memory `niri-cursor-comprehensive`.

**Flake bumped; resolved electron insecure-package waiver dropped; new `rtk` build (not just eval) break found and patched (2026-07-24).** nixpkgs now ships vesktop on `electron_43` with no known vulnerabilities, so the `electron-40.10.5` `permittedInsecurePackages` waiver in `nix.nix` (added session 43) was removed — resolved, don't re-add. Separately, this same bump was the first time `pkgs ? rtk` evaluated true on the unstable-tracking hosts (rtk-0.43.0 landed in nixpkgs) — but that derivation's own `cargo test` `checkPhase` fails under `-D warnings` (dead-code lints promoted to hard errors), which broke `nh os boot`'s real build (not just eval) on every unstable host. Patched with `pkgs.rtk.overrideAttrs (_: { doCheck = false; })` in `modules/users.nix`, tagged TEMPORARY — remove once nixpkgs ships an rtk revision whose tests pass cleanly. Reconfirmed 2026-07-26 via a real `nix build` of the *unmodified* `pkgs.rtk` that the checkPhase failure is still genuine (unstable hadn't moved past `e2587cae`). Commit `839cbc0`. See memory `project_vpn_server_rtk_gap`.

**Skills batch: `/init`+`claude-rules` pairing, 5th standing rule, secret-scan generator + a real bug fix, git-commit safety improvement, new commit-and-push skill, one niri keybind tweak (2026-07-23).**
- **`/init` now always chains into `claude-rules`** — both globally (new `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` symlinked from `dotfiles/bosko/claude/CLAUDE.md`) and documented locally in this repo's `CLAUDE.md`, so the five standing rules get checked/restored every time `/init` touches a `CLAUDE.md`, in any project. Commit `4f209f6`.
- **Added a 5th standing rule, "Ask via AskUserQuestion"** — route `/interview`'s waves and key-decision confirmations through the tool instead of plain-text prose; promoted into `claude-rules`' canonical rule set so it propagates everywhere. Commit `1e7f14e`.
- **New global meta-skill `/create-secret-scan`** — interviews a project about its secret-handling conventions and generates a project-local `secret-scan` skill tuned to it, rather than a one-size-fits-all global scanner (user explicitly chose this over a global generic scanner). `session-closer` now soft-prompts to create one when missing, before the README pass. **Found and fixed a real bug while testing it**: `git grep -E "$pat"` silently no-ops when `$pat` starts with `-` (e.g. the private-key BEGIN-block pattern) because git parses it as an unknown flag and a trailing `2>/dev/null` swallows the error — meaning private-key detection had never actually worked in the shipped `secret-scan.sh`. Fixed in both the live script and the new generator's template with `-e "$pat"`. Commit `229e505`. See memory `create-secret-scan` and `git-grep-leading-dash-pattern`.
- **`git-commit` now asks before silently dropping unrelated uncommitted work** — previously it would commit only the files matching the current task and leave anything else untouched with no comment; now flags any extra uncommitted files via `AskUserQuestion` first. Commit `218b893`.
- **New `commit-and-push` skill** chaining `git-commit`→`git-push` in one invocation — built after noticing the pair gets invoked back-to-back in nearly every session across other projects. Commit `9e69730`.
- **niri: Mod+B/Mod+G now also open a Zen private window** alongside the normal one (spawned ~0.5s later, lands to the right via niri's default column placement). Commit `21c7d2d`.
- **gaming: Jellyfin media drive now visible in Thunar's sidebar** — fstab mounts default to `udisks2` `HintSystem=true`, which hides them from GVfs-based sidebars even though the mount itself was correct; added `x-gvfs-show` to the fstab options. Commit `a3d5c59`.

**All 6 structural findings from the improve-system routine's first clean run fixed — 1 commit, pushed (2026-07-20, session 52 continued).**
- **`de-smoke-check` + `public-repo-guard`**: their free-prose pick-one/confirm gates (ambiguous-module fallback; baseline-append yes/no) now route through the **AskUserQuestion tool**, matching the pattern already used by `bump-input`/`nixos-gc`/`rollback`/`pin-input`.
- **`shared-module-check`**: gained the `## Arguments` section it was missing despite Step 1 already accepting a user-supplied file/target, modeled on `verify-service`'s format.
- **`create-loop`**: Step 4's mechanical structural checks (frontmatter, name/directory match, required sections, done-rule presence, dual-file output section) extracted into a new `scripts/lint-loop.sh <path>`; the judgment-only checks (are done-rules actually measurable, does a verification pass resolve) stay in prose. Smoke-tested against two real generated loops (`public-repo-guard`, `fleet-rollout`).
- **`skill-suggestion`**: its prose transcript-directory resolution was duplicating logic already scripted in two other skills. Extracted a genuinely shared `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/lib/find-transcript-dir.sh` (new shared-lib convention for global skills, mirroring the project-local `.claude/lib/` pattern) — and went further than the finding asked by also rewiring `skill-upgrade/find-skill-misfires.sh` and `session-closer/scan-session.sh` to call it, closing the duplication in all three places instead of just adding a fourth copy. New `.claude/skills/lib` recursive-dir entry added to `bosko-claude.nix`.
- **`skill-upgrade`**: its Gotchas-append approve/reject gate now routes through AskUserQuestion too.
- **Verified**: `nixos-dry-run` clean on gaming (+16.2 KiB, just the HM source closure), then `shared-module-check` → full 4-host `deep-eval-check` sweep (gaming/laptop/natalie-laptop/vpn-server) all PASS, since `bosko-claude.nix` is shared via `modules/home-manager.nix` → `desktopModules`. Commit `29e40c1`, pushed.

**"Weekly improve-system sweep" cloud routine's silent-failure root cause found and fixed — no repo changes, diagnostic-only session (2026-07-20, session 52).**
- **Root cause: the "Claude" GitHub App was authorized but never installed.** Following session 51's checkpoint-per-step GitHub-issue redesign, the routine was triggered manually and re-checked ~20h later — it had fired, but `gh issue list`/`gh pr list` on `CommanderBosko/NixOS` were still completely empty, meaning even the routine's literal first instructed action (`gh issue create`) never reached GitHub. Traced with the user through GitHub's settings UI: not under `github.com/settings/installations` (classic GitHub Apps), not under Authorized OAuth Apps — but present under **Authorized GitHub Apps** ("Claude", owned by `anthropics`, Issues/PRs permissions listed) while simultaneously **absent from Installed GitHub Apps**. GitHub Apps require both gates (user authorization + repo installation) independently; authorization alone grants zero actual repo access.
- **Fixed by installing the app** at `github.com/apps/claude`, scoped to `CommanderBosko/NixOS`, with Contents/Issues/Pull-requests read & write.
- **Verified end-to-end**: re-triggered the routine via `RemoteTrigger run` immediately after install. Tracking issue [`#1`](https://github.com/CommanderBosko/NixOS/issues/1) created within 15 seconds, all 6 checkpoint comments posted, issue closed with a full consolidated report ~90 seconds after opening — first-ever clean run of this routine. Zero-diff (nothing needed applying); 6 minor structural findings logged for manual review only (small AskUserQuestion/script-extraction polish across `de-smoke-check`, `public-repo-guard`, `shared-module-check`, `create-loop`, `skill-suggestion`, `skill-upgrade`).
- **PENDING**: nothing — the routine is confirmed working and back on its normal weekly cadence (`0 16 * * 0` UTC). No action needed unless a future run goes silent again, in which case check whether the App install got revoked before re-litigating the prompt design.

**Skill-library model/script paradigm rolled out fleet-wide, two real cross-host bugs found and fixed (natalie-laptop DE conflict, vpn-server flake-eval break), two new skills shipped — 12 commits, pending push (2026-07-18, session 50).**
- **Per-skill model tiering**: classified all 52 skills (project-local + global) as grunt-work vs. judgment-work via 4 parallel sub-agents, then pinned `model: haiku` in frontmatter on the 32 grunt-work skills (mechanical fetch/format/relay/template-fill) while leaving the 22 judgment-work skills (synthesis, auditing, planning, drafting) on the session's top model. Confirmed live that `SKILL.md`'s `model:` field is an official, documented Claude Code feature (turn-scoped override). Taught `new-skill` (step 2a) and `create-loop` (interview question 8) to apply this classification to every future skill/loop, not just retroactively. Commit `8222fb5`.
- **Deterministic-work-to-scripts sweep**: ran `/skill-audit` scoped to just its "lens 2" rubric item across all 52 skills via 4 parallel sub-agents — 13 real findings. Implemented Tier 1-3 (11 skills) across 6 phase-boundary commits: a new shared `.claude/lib/resolve-host.sh` (alias-normalize + hosts.json lookup, de-duping `ssh-host`+`journal`'s independently-re-derived logic), `session-closer`'s git-scan+transcript-extraction (`scripts/scan-session.sh`), `ci-status`'s run-watch+log-tail chain, `add-secret`'s 3-mode sops wrapper, `pin-input`'s state table (via `nix flake metadata`, following the existing `.claude/lib/` shared-script convention), `vpn-status`'s parse+classify (extended its existing script), `switch-de`'s host-scoped current-DE parser, `new-peer`'s IP-arithmetic+pubkey read, `de-smoke-check` and `add-default-app`'s verification chains. Every script `bash -n`-checked and run for real against live state before being wired in. Tier 4 (2 low-value/high-overhead findings) explicitly skipped per user call. Commits `be2337d`..`53fb023`. Taught `new-skill` (new step 2b) to apply the same extraction going forward, including a preference for a **recursive** `home.file` entry over file-by-file when a new global skill is likely to grow `scripts/`/`assets/`. Commit `741cd62`.
- **`switch-de`'s new `current-de.sh` immediately caught a real bug**: `natalie-laptop`'s `flake.nix` imported both `niri.nix` and `plasma.nix` (added `ca0c426`, 2026-07-11, for an SDDM dual-session choice that was never actually used — niri is the DE in active use). Dropped `plasma.nix` from the host's module list; kept the `niri.nix` systemd `ConditionEnvironment` gates that exist because of the old dual-boot (DMS-vs-Plasma-panel conflict, kdeglobals ColorScheme) since they're harmless with Plasma absent and correct again if it returns — just fixed their now-stale present-tense comments. Commit `e2e0a1c`.
- **The Tier 1-3 sweep's own `deep-eval-check` run surfaced a second, unrelated, real bug**: `vpn-server`'s flake evaluation has been broken since `95bbbc5` (the prior, unclosed session's `rtk` install) — `rtk` only exists in nixpkgs-unstable, not the `nixpkgs-25.11` stable channel `vpn-server` pins for its `aarch64-linux` build, so the hardcoded package reference in `modules/users.nix` (shared via `commonModules`) hit `undefined variable 'rtk'` on that host alone. Confirmed via `git stash` this predated today's session. Fixed by gating both the package (`modules/users.nix`) and the PreToolUse hook that shells out to it (`modules/claude-code.nix`) on `pkgs ? rtk` existence rather than hardcoding — self-heals once a future stable point-release backports the package, and stops `claude` from hitting "command not found" on a host that never got the binary. Verified: `gaming` still gets `rtk` + both hooks, `vpn-server` correctly gets neither, all 4 hosts pass `deep-eval-check`. **Not deployed to vpn-server** — user explicitly held off ("no reason to do it until stable nixpkgs is updated"); the live vpn-server generation predates the break and is unaffected. Commit `3cd3a31`.
- **New `shared-module-check` skill** (project-local, via `/skill-suggestion`) — closes the exact gap that let the `rtk`/vpn-server bug through uncaught: after editing a `commonModules`/`desktopModules` file, forces the full 4-host `deep-eval-check` sweep instead of trusting a local dry-run that silently verifies nothing about the other hosts. Documents the `pkgs ? <attr>` availability-guard pattern (citing the `rtk` fix as the reference example) as the first thing to check on a `vpn-server`-only failure. Smoke-tested against the actual commit that caused the break. Commit `06e5e3f`.
- **`nixos-dry-run` gained a Gotchas entry** (via `/skill-upgrade`) — documents that it takes no host argument and always builds the local machine, caught from directly mis-invoking it with `natalie-laptop` while working from `gaming` earlier in the session. Commit `06e5e3f`.
- **`session-closer`'s brand-new `scan-session.sh` (written this session) already had a bug, caught during this very close**: its baseline/range git-log calls used `--all`, reproducing the already-documented "`--all` range footgun" (pulls in stash entries as "WIP on main:"/"index on main:" commits, plus unrelated branch history) because the sub-agent that wrote it didn't have access to that memory. Fixed by dropping `--all` from both the baseline search and the range query. Commit `8507de3`.
- **Retroactively closing a prior unclosed session (session 49, 2026-07-17/18, 5 commits, narrated from commit messages/memory only — no transcript exists for it in this conversation)**: `rtk` (nixpkgs token-optimizing Bash proxy) installed and wired as a second PreToolUse hook alongside the fork-bomb guard (`95bbbc5`) — this is the install that session 50 above found broke vpn-server. CLAUDE.md trimmed from one file into a directory (`4aaf63a`). Unused team-member advisory-board skill cluster removed, 0 usage (`7a2ca9d`). Sweet theme + candy-icons installed for the shared niri theme (`f51fc62`). adw-gtk3 installed so GTK apps get the dark theme (`9c5b356`).
- **PENDING**: all 12 of this session's commits (plus 2 of session 49's) are committed but not pushed — session-closer will push. `vpn-server` fix is verified at the eval level but deliberately **not deployed** (held pending a stable-nixpkgs backport of `rtk`, per user). Desktop-host rebuilds (gaming/laptop/natalie-laptop) for the `natalie-laptop` Plasma removal are the user's to run themselves, per their explicit call ("I'll handle the 3 desktop host rebuilds myself").

**Declarative default-application associations (`xdg.mimeApps`) added, then the whole KDE/`ksycoca`-dependent file-opening stack replaced after it proved unreliable under niri; a `kded6` workaround was tried and abandoned in favor of the app swap; two unrelated live bugs (broken `apparmor.service` reload, missing USB auto-mount) also root-caused — 11 commits, 10 pushed, 1 pending push (2026-07-17, session 48).**
- **`xdg.mimeApps.defaultApplications` set up in `dotfiles/common/configs/home.nix`** — Kate for code/text, Ark for archives, Okular for PDFs, Gwenview for images, VLC for video/audio; every mimetype verified against each app's real `.desktop` `MimeType=` declaration via `xdg-mime query filetype`, never guessed. Required `xdg.configFile."mimeapps.list".force = true` (+ the matching `xdg.dataFile` override) after the first rebuild failed activation ("Existing file ... would be clobbered") — HM's `xdg.mimeApps` module doesn't force-overwrite by default, and a real hand-created `mimeapps.list` already existed on gaming. Before clobbering it, read the live file and found real working associations (GitHub Desktop OAuth, Discord/FreeTube/r2modman/Claude-Code URL handlers, browser defaults) that the new declarative list didn't cover yet — restored those, dropped genuinely dead entries (uninstalled vivaldi/podman-desktop/old deezer-enhanced flatpak id). Commits `4b2e188`, `798d396`.
- **Dolphin's "Open With" picker turned out completely broken under niri**: user hit it opening qBittorrent's backup zip with an empty app list. Root cause, confirmed live over several diagnostic passes: Dolphin/Ark resolve default apps via KDE's `KService`/`ksycoca` database, not plain XDG (`xdg-open`/`xdg-mime` resolved correctly the whole time) — and niri never runs `kded6`, which is what normally keeps `ksycoca` valid (announced over D-Bus) in a full Plasma session. Clearing/rebuilding the sycoca cache by hand didn't fix it even after a genuinely fresh Dolphin process; also found the cache is fragmented per-app under Nix (each wrapped KDE binary gets a different `XDG_DATA_DIRS`, hence a different cache-file hash). Tried adding a standalone `kded6` systemd --user service as a real fix (gated on `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=niri`, matching the DMS/swayidle pattern already in `niri.nix`) — flake-checked clean, but the user decided to abandon the whole KDE-app approach instead of keep patching around it.
- **Swapped the entire KDE/`ksycoca`-dependent stack for apps that resolve associations via plain XDG**: Dolphin → Thunar (`Mod+E` rebound), Ark → xarchiver, Okular → zathura (mupdf backend), Gwenview → imv. Kate was kept (never actually broken — it's a target app, not one resolving associations itself) and Double Commander (added earlier the same session as a Dolphin alternative) was also dropped in favor of Thunar per the user's explicit call. The `kded6` service was removed again since nothing left needs it. Net **-123 MiB** on gaming — removed KDE-only transitive deps (okular/ark/gwenview/dolphin's KIO/KDE-Frameworks closure) outweighed the new lightweight packages. Commit `413f45f`.
- **New `add-default-app` skill** (project-local) — captures the now-twice-repeated "resolve an app's real `.desktop` mimetypes, add to `xdg.mimeApps.defaultApplications`, verify by building+grepping the actual generated `mimeapps.list`" workflow. Built via `/skill-suggestion` → `/new-skill`, initially shipped **without** a smoke-test (the exact gap `/ship-skill` exists to close) — caught when the user asked directly whether `/skill-suggestion` should hand off to `/ship-skill` instead of `/new-skill`. Answer: no (that would be circular — `ship-skill`'s own Step 1 already calls `skill-suggestion`), but `new-skill`'s final report now explicitly flags its output as untested and points at `ship-skill`. Retroactively smoke-tested `add-default-app` for real by setting qBittorrent as the default for `.torrent`/magnet-link files (a genuine, previously-unset gap) — passed cleanly. Commits `120ec0c`, `2d3211e`, `298c21f`.
- **Three niri window-rule skills (`add-niri-window-rule`, `add-niri-keybind`, `add-niri-fullscreen-rule`) found stale** — all still pointed at the shared `niri-config.kdl` for workspaces/window-rules, a location that stopped being correct once the per-host overlay split (session 47) moved that content to `hosts/<host>/niri-overlay.kdl`. Surfaced when `add-niri-window-rule` was used to pin Thunar to a new `dell-5` workspace on gaming and had to be manually redirected to the right file. Fixed all three: corrected the target path, replaced the now-moot "cross-host leakage" caveat (the overlay split already prevents that) with an app-availability check, and fixed the stale `gaming`+`laptop`-only host list to include `natalie-laptop`. Commits `300179e` (the actual dell-5 rule), `5ee7ddd` (skill fixes).
- **Two unrelated live bugs root-caused and fixed**: (1) **USB drives not auto-mounting under Thunar** — the kernel/`udisks2`/polkit layer works perfectly (`udisksctl mount` succeeded live with no auth prompt), but nothing was watching for insertion events; Thunar needs `services.gvfs.enable` + `thunar-volman` explicitly (Dolphin's KIO/Solid backend never needed them). Commit `1e5a8ce`. (2) **`nh os boot` activation failing outright** with "Failed to reload apparmor.service" — a real nixpkgs packaging bug in `apparmor-parser-5.0.0`: `apparmor-utils`' `aa-remove-unknown` script (the service's `ExecReload`) hardcodes a path to `apparmor-parser`'s `lib/apparmor/rc.apparmor.functions`, which this build doesn't ship. AppArmor's actual enforcement is unaffected (boot-time initial load succeeds fine); only the incremental-reload path breaks. Fixed via `systemd.services.apparmor.reloadIfChanged = lib.mkForce false;` in `security.nix`, forcing activation to use a full restart instead of the broken reload. Commit `c4690e5` (**not yet pushed**).
- **A third, separate live issue found but not yet resolved**: even after the `gvfs`/`thunar-volman` fix rebuilt and activated successfully (confirmed via `nh os info` — new generation active, no more activation errors), USB auto-mount still didn't work. Root-caused to **session staleness, not config**: the user's session-level `dbus-broker.service` (`systemctl --user`) has been running continuously since login this morning and never restarted, so it never re-read the updated `/etc/dbus-1/session.conf` that now lists gvfs's D-Bus service directory — `gio mount -l` returns nothing because the volume-monitor service was never registered on the live session bus. User chose to reboot later rather than live-restart the session bus now (a live restart would briefly drop every app's D-Bus connection). **Not yet verified working** — needs a reboot (or `systemctl --user restart dbus-broker.service`) to confirm.
- **Niri keybinds confirmed shared, not per-user**: user asked whether keybinds are bosko-only or shared with `natty`. Confirmed by reading `niri.nix`: `niriHomeConfig` (which includes `config.kdl`, holding all the binds) is assigned identically to both `home-manager.users.bosko` and `home-manager.users.natty` — fully shared. Only window-rules/workspaces differ, and that's per-*host* (the overlay split), not per-user.

**Niri config split into a shared base + per-host overlay; a second, distinct qBittorrent placement bug found and fixed; gaming rebuilt live and confirmed — 2 commits, pushed (2026-07-17, session 47).**
- **`niri-config.kdl` split into shared base + per-host overlay**, via `/interview` (user wanted per-host keybinds/window rules for gaming/laptop/natalie-laptop). Discovered mid-interview that niri's KDL format has a native `include "path"` directive (already in use for `dms/outputs.kdl`) — no Nix-level text concatenation needed, simpler than the originally-proposed approach. `dotfiles/common/configs/niri-config.kdl` stays shared (input/layout/binds/standard rules) and gains `include "niri-overlay.kdl"`; gaming's 7 named workspaces (`asus-1..3`/`dell-1..4`) and 7 app-pinning window-rules moved verbatim to new `hosts/gaming/niri-overlay.kdl`; `hosts/laptop/niri-overlay.kdl` and `hosts/natalie-laptop/niri-overlay.kdl` are empty placeholders (per user: keybinds stay identical across all 3 hosts; laptop/natalie-laptop start with just the standard window rules, none of gaming's monitor pins). `modules/desktop-environments/niri.nix`'s `niriHomeConfig` gained `osConfig` to resolve `hosts/${osConfig.networking.hostName}/niri-overlay.kdl` per host. Verified via `niri validate -c` on all 3 hosts' composed configs (scratch dir, base+overlay assembled), `nh os boot --dry` on gaming, and `nix build --dry-run` eval checks on laptop/natalie-laptop. Commit `3960600`.
- **Second, previously-unknown qBittorrent workspace-pin bug found and fixed** — distinct from session 45's autostart-race fix (a different root cause, not a duplicate/regression of it). User reported qBittorrent still opening on the current workspace instead of `dell-4`. Live-diagnosed via `niri msg windows`/`niri msg -j workspaces`: qBittorrent's actual window app-id is `org.qbittorrent.qBittorrent`, but the rule matched `^qbittorrent$` — a regex that had *never* matched, so the rule silently no-op'd every time and the window always fell back to whatever workspace was focused. All 6 other pinned apps were confirmed correctly placed live, isolating this to qBittorrent specifically. Fixed the regex to `^org\.qbittorrent\.qBittorrent$`. Commit `2cbe86c`.
- **Verified live, not just statically**: ran `nh os switch` on gaming (generation 332→333) to activate — this also picked up every previously-staged niri change from sessions 43-46 (keybinds, per-monitor pins, `open-maximized` rules, the kdeglobals fix, flake bumps) that had been sitting PENDING across those sessions' notes; all now confirmed live on gaming. Confirmed the overlay symlink resolved to `hosts/gaming/niri-overlay.kdl` and the qBittorrent fix by minimizing it to tray and restoring — it now lands on `dell-4` correctly.
- **Laptop and natalie-laptop still need their own `rebuild` + reboot** to pick up the overlay split (they'll get the shared base + their own empty overlay — no functional change until host-specific rules are added to those files).

**A weekly cloud-scheduled `/improve-system` routine was set up, plus a small kitty window-rule fix from a concurrent session — 1 commit, pushed (2026-07-16, session 46).**
- **New cloud routine: "Weekly improve-system sweep"** (`trig_01QZPjKtQdMtX5bqwtC8oz8g`, via the `schedule` skill / `RemoteTrigger`) — runs every Sunday at 16:00 UTC (noon Eastern during EDT; will drift to 11am Eastern once EST resumes in November, since cron is fixed-UTC and doesn't auto-adjust for DST). Not a local job: it runs entirely in Anthropic's cloud, independent of any machine being on or a Claude Code window being open. First create attempt failed with `HTTP 401` ("Connect your GitHub account before saving a routine that uses a GitHub repository") — user installed the Claude GitHub App on `CommanderBosko/NixOS`, retry succeeded.
- **Adapted `/improve-system` for unattended execution** — the routine's prompt cannot rely on `~/.claude/skills/*` slash commands resolving (those symlinks are Home-Manager/local-machine state that doesn't exist in the cloud sandbox), so it instead points the agent directly at the repo-relative `SKILL.md` files (`dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/{improve-system,skill-upgrade,skill-suggestion,claude-rules,skill-audit}/SKILL.md`) and has it follow them as plain instructions. Also overrides `improve-system`'s own `AskUserQuestion` structural-approval gates (no human present on a schedule): low-risk additive changes (Gotchas entries, CLAUDE.md rules, `permissions.allow` additions) are auto-applied; anything structural (a new skill, a skill-audit refactor) is reported but never applied. Step 4's `nixos-dry-run` check is skipped gracefully if `nh`/`nix` aren't present in the sandbox rather than treated as a failure.
- **Decided: PR, not direct push** — the routine opens a PR against `main` for any auto-applied low-risk changes rather than pushing directly, since this is a new, unverified unattended workflow; a fully clean run (nothing to apply, nothing structural) just reports clean with no PR. User chose this over matching the normal local session-closer direct-push convention, specifically because nobody has seen this workflow's real output yet.
- **Decided: hold off building a "review the weekly PR" skill** — user asked whether a skill was warranted for merging/pulling/rebuilding after the routine's PR lands. Declined for now: the individual steps are already covered by existing tools (`gh`, `git`, `nixos-dry-run` + `rebuild`), and the routine has never actually run, so the real shape of its PR output is unknown — revisit after the first live Sunday run if reviewing it turns out to be more than a quick glance-and-merge.
- **Small side fix from a concurrent local session** (commit `c0005e0`, not part of this conversation — reconstructed from that session's transcript per session-closer's ground-truth rule): user asked to stop kitty from opening maximized; the session-45 `open-maximized true` line was removed from kitty's window-rule, keeping its `asus-1` workspace pin. Verified via `nixos-dry-run`. The other four `open-maximized` rules from session 45 (Vesktop, Zen Browser, Deezer, qBittorrent) are unaffected. That same concurrent session also explored "add the claude github app... in `modules/users.nix`" — no file changes resulted (likely the same GitHub App connection done via the browser flow above, not a NixOS config change); nothing to carry forward.
- **PENDING**: the routine itself needs no reboot (cloud-side only) and will fire on its own starting 2026-07-19. The kitty fix rides along with the already-pending gaming/laptop reboot, same as the other four `open-maximized` rules.

**qBittorrent's Mod+G workspace bug root-caused and fixed, `open-maximized` fullscreen-on-open added to 5 apps, and a new `add-niri-fullscreen-rule` skill shipped — 1 commit, pushed (2026-07-16, session 45).**
- **qBittorrent workspace-placement bug root-caused, not just re-verified**: user reported qBittorrent opening on workspace 1 instead of its session-44 `dell-4` pin despite the window-rule matching correctly. Confirmed the app-id itself was fine (`StartupWMClass=qbittorrent` in its `.desktop` file matches the existing rule exactly) — the real cause was qBittorrent's own "run on startup" preference, which had written a login autostart entry (`~/.config/autostart/org.qbittorrent.qBittorrent.desktop`, picked up by `systemd-xdg-autostart-generator`) that launched ~12s before Mod+G. Since qBittorrent is single-instance (`SingleMainWindow=true`), Mod+G's `qbittorrent &` was just raising that pre-existing, mis-placed window rather than creating a fresh one the window-rule could act on. **Fixed live, outside the nix flake (not git-tracked)**: stopped `app-org.qbittorrent.qBittorrent@autostart.service` and moved the autostart `.desktop` file out of `~/.config/autostart/` (backed up to the session scratchpad, not deleted) — qBittorrent now only ever opens via Mod+G, so this fix is already in effect with no reboot needed.
- **`open-maximized true` (the Mod+F / maximize-column equivalent) added to 5 window-rules**: Kitty, Vesktop, Zen Browser, Deezer, qBittorrent — user wants these to launch already-maximized. A 6th (Steam) was added then explicitly reverted per the user's follow-up request, leaving Steam's rule unchanged.
- **New `add-niri-fullscreen-rule` skill shipped** (project-local, via `/skill-suggestion` → `/new-skill`) — sibling of `add-niri-window-rule`: that one pins an app to a monitor/workspace, this one only ever sets `open-maximized true` on an app's existing (or new) window-rule block. Bakes in two gotchas surfaced this session: Steam-style apps can have several windows sharing one app-id (one rule covers all of them), while individual games launched *from* Steam each get their own distinct app-id (no blanket rule is possible, and most games manage their own fullscreen state independently of the compositor anyway); and the qBittorrent-style autostart-vs-single-instance trap above.
- **`nixos-dry-run` skill gained a Gotchas section** (via `/skill-upgrade`) — `nh os boot --dry`'s output is a redrawing TUI tree whose useful `PATHS:`/`SIZE:`/`DIFF:` summary sits at the very end behind a wall of progress-bar noise; documented `tail -8` / `grep -E '^(PATHS|SIZE|DIFF):'` as the fix, after hitting this friction firsthand twice in-session (first guessed `tail -60`, still noisy).
- **PENDING**: the 5 new `open-maximized` rules ride along with the already-pending gaming reboot (sessions 43-44's niri keybinds/window rules) — nothing new added to the reboot's scope beyond more `niri-config.kdl` content. The qBittorrent autostart fix is the exception: it's a live systemd/dotfile change outside the flake, already in effect on gaming, no reboot required.

**natalie-laptop gained a niri session option; DMS's home-manager wiring fixed so `natty` gets it too, not just `bosko` (2026-07-12, session 42).** Commits `ca0c426`, `4dd1759` (from a prior, unclosed session — no transcript exists beyond the commit messages, narrated from those only) and `aa8b0c9` (this session, pushed).
- `ca0c426`: natalie-laptop's flake entry gained `niri.nix` alongside `plasma.nix`, giving it an SDDM session choice between the two.
- `4dd1759`: adding niri surfaced a Plasma/niri leak — niri.nix's DMS autostart, swayidle, and kdeglobals `ColorScheme` override applied unconditionally in `home-manager.users.bosko`, which would fight Plasma's own panel on a Plasma login. Fixed by gating `dms.service`/`swayidle.service` on `ConditionEnvironment=XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP=niri` and the kdeglobals activation script on the invoking shell's own `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP`.
- **This session**: user (on gaming) reported DMS works under niri for `bosko` on natalie-laptop but not for `natty`. Root-caused by reading `niri.nix`, `modules/home-manager.nix`, and `flake.nix` together: the *entire* DMS/`config.kdl`/`qt6ct.conf`/kdeglobals Home Manager block only ever targeted `home-manager.users.bosko` — `natty` gets the shared `dotfiles/common/configs/home.nix` base like bosko does, but none of the DMS-specific wiring, so DMS never started in natty's niri session on *any* niri host (gaming, laptop, natalie-laptop), not just natalie-laptop. Confirmed with the user via `AskUserQuestion` that the fix should cover all niri hosts rather than a natalie-laptop-only override. Refactored `niri.nix` into a shared `niriHomeConfig` let-binding assigned to both `home-manager.users.bosko` and `home-manager.users.natty`. Verified via `deep-eval-check` across all four hosts (gaming/laptop/natalie-laptop/vpn-server — all PASS). Committed `aa8b0c9`, pushed. User is rebuilding natalie-laptop themselves and will test the natty niri login live — not yet verified end-to-end.

**`omarchy.nix` reworked onto a real theme-switching pipeline + deep-dive comparison against the community `omarchy-nix` flake (2026-07-11, session 41).** Commit `e7031e3` (committed, not yet pushed — session-closer will push).
- User asked whether session 40's from-scratch `omarchy.nix` was based on `github:henrysipp/omarchy-nix`. It wasn't — confirmed independent origin, then did a full deep-dive (downloaded and read all ~30 files of that repo) to compare. Findings: that repo is architecturally deeper (real `nix-colors` base16 theme-switching, full Wayland/Qt env glue, hyprpaper, mako, a much fuller keybind set, window/layer rules) but also unmaintained (author's own words: moved to Arch Omarchy full-time), hard-assumes greetd+tuigreet+its own NetworkManager/Bluetooth/PipeWire stack (conflicts with this repo's shared SDDM + `desktop-networking.nix`), builds Hyprland from source instead of the nixpkgs cache, and bakes in the author's personal defaults (1Password, HEY.com/ChatGPT/X keybinds, Dropbox/Spotify). User asked for the specific downsides of importing it as a flake input rather than hand-porting — answered with 6 concrete conflict points (see Recent Decisions) — then chose to hand-port the good ideas instead of importing the dependency.
- **Adopted `nix-colors` as a real base16 theme-switching pipeline** (new flake input, no `follows` needed — it only depends on a lib-only nixpkgs repackage + the tinted-theming schemes repo). `omarchy.nix` now has a single `colorScheme = inputs.nix-colors.colorSchemes."tokyo-night-dark";` line feeding Hyprland borders, Waybar, Hyprlock, and mako — replacing the old hand-copied hex constants. This is the actual mechanism behind Omarchy's headline feature (instant, unified theme-switching); previously the module only had Omarchy's *look*, not the *mechanism*.
- **Ported the "quick wins" from the comparison**: Wayland/Qt/GTK env vars (`GDK_BACKEND`, `QT_QPA_PLATFORM`, `MOZ_ENABLE_WAYLAND`, cursor theme — NVIDIA vars deliberately *not* duplicated since `modules/nvidia.nix` already sets those system-wide for any host that imports it), `swaybg` solid-color background (no bundled wallpaper asset in this repo, so no `hyprpaper`+image), `mako` notifications, a fuller keybind set (`hyprshot`/`hyprpicker` screenshots+color-picker, `clipse` clipboard manager, arrow-key focus movement, Omarchy's real Escape-key session menu replacing the old placeholder `$mod+M` exit bind), and window/layer rules (opacity, maximize-suppress fix, clipse floating, Waybar blur). Checked Walker's actual source before adding a blur layerrule for it — its Rust rewrite does its own blur via `ext-background-effect-v1`, not a compositor layerrule, so that one was correctly left out.
- **Investigated and reverted a wrong fix**: initially added an `XDG_DATA_DIRS` env line (borrowed from the upstream repo's wofi-specific note) so Walker could see Home-Manager-installed apps. Live-checked the actual running session's `$XDG_DATA_DIRS` and found this repo's HM-as-NixOS-module setup already puts `/etc/profiles/per-user/bosko/share` on the path globally (not `~/.nix-profile/share`, the standalone-HM convention the borrowed line assumed and which doesn't exist on this system) — so the line was dead weight referencing a path that isn't real here. Removed it.
- Verified entirely via `lib.deSmoke.omarchy` deep-eval (module isn't wired into any host — same status as the other 8 unused DE modules now that hyprland is used by the omarchy comparison but still unwired) — `nix eval --raw .#lib.deSmoke.omarchy.config.system.build.toplevel.drvPath` resolved cleanly before and after the `XDG_DATA_DIRS` removal — plus a full `flake-check` across all 4 real hosts (unaffected, none reference `omarchy.nix` or `nix-colors`).
- **Built the `de-smoke-check` skill** (project-local) after realizing CLAUDE.md's documented DE-module verification step (`nh os boot --dry`) silently verifies nothing for any of the 9 (now still 9) unwired DE modules — dogfooded immediately against `omarchy`, passed. Added a matching note to CLAUDE.md's "Adding a New Desktop Environment" section pointing at the skill for this case.

**Two new DE modules: Hyprland gained DMS parity, and a from-scratch Omarchy-style module was added (2026-07-10, session 40).** Commits `b7e50a1` (pushed), `7d81c4c` (committed, not yet pushed — session-closer will push).
- **`hyprland.nix` wired up to match `niri.nix`'s DMS integration**, per direct user request. Added: DMS shell (`programs.dank-material-shell.enable` + `systemd.enable`, same autostart mechanism as niri), the qt6ct/kdeglobals Qt theming glue (identical to niri.nix), `accounts-daemon`/`power-profiles-daemon` (DMS System Check deps), and HM-managed `programs.swaylock`/`services.swayidle` in place of plain system packages. Dropped `waybar`/`rofi`/`mako` (replaced by DMS). Deliberately did **not** add a Nix-managed `hyprland.conf`/keybinds — discovered via inspecting the actual `dms` flake input source (`distro/nix/` only has a `niri.nix` submodule, no Hyprland equivalent) that DMS manages Hyprland's own config **imperatively** at runtime since Hyprland 0.55 (`dms setup` generates/migrates a Lua config, `~/.config/hypr/hyprland.lua` + `dms/*.lua` fragments, sweeping any existing `hyprland.conf` into its own backup folder) — a Nix-managed file would just get backed up out of the way on first `dms run`, so that goal was dropped after surfacing this to the user mid-task. Verified via deep-eval of `lib.deSmoke.hyprland` (Hyprland isn't live on any host) plus all four live hosts.
- **New `omarchy.nix` DE module**, built after a 10-source `/research` pass (parallel sub-agents) on Omarchy (DHH's opinionated Arch+Hyprland desktop). Key research findings: Omarchy is Arch-only per its own official manual (NixOS explicitly unsupported, community-port-only); the existing community port `omarchy-nix` (Henry Sipp) is architecturally a clean importable `nixosModules`/`homeManagerModules` pair, but hard-assumes greetd+tuigreet+PipeWire+its own NetworkManager/Bluetooth stack — directly conflicting with this repo's shared SDDM (`modules/sddm.nix`) and `desktop-networking.nix` — and is ~8 months stale (last commit 2025-11-13) with a history of nixos-unstable breakage. Decision (user-confirmed via 3 scoping questions): build a **from-scratch, fully independent module** (not layered on `hyprland.nix`, not importing `omarchy-nix`) replicating Omarchy's *actual* shell — Waybar + Hyprlock + Walker (confirmed via research as Omarchy's real launcher, not DMS or rofi/fuzzel) — themed in Omarchy's default Tokyo Night palette, with Omarchy's keybind philosophy (`Super+Return` terminal, `Super+Space` launcher, `Super+1-9` workspaces) declared via `wayland.windowManager.hyprland.settings`. Scoped to theme+keybind parity only — kept this repo's existing app choices (kitty, dolphin) rather than Omarchy's own (Alacritty, etc.). One bug caught and fixed during verification: home-manager's Hyprland module warned that its default `configType` is migrating from `"hyprlang"` to `"lua"` — pinned explicitly to `"hyprlang"` since the `settings` attrset used follows the classic schema, not Lua. Verified via deep-eval of the new `lib.deSmoke.omarchy` target plus all four live hosts; not wired into any host yet (same status as the other 9 unused DE modules — picked up automatically by the weekly `de-smoke` CI job).
- **Known gap, noted in-code**: `omarchy.nix`'s dolphin has no Qt/KDE theming glue (unlike niri/hyprland's qt6ct+kdeglobals setup, which is coupled to DMS's matugen color generation and doesn't apply without DMS running) — it'll render in Qt's default light style until/unless someone adds a standalone (non-DMS) qt6ct config for it.

**Two new skills shipped via a fresh `ship-skill` orchestration skill, pushed (2026-07-10, session 39).** Commits `cf9e38b`, `fb79baa`.
- User asked "any other `/skill-suggestion`?" with no prior conversation this session — `skill-suggestion` forked a sub-agent to mine all past-session transcripts (`~/.claude/projects/-home-bosko-NixOS/*.jsonl`) rather than the (empty) live conversation. Found: `nix flake check` is a documented shallow check for `nixosConfigurations` (see session 36/pnpm-waiver precedent) — the real per-host deep-eval trick (`nix eval --raw .#nixosConfigurations.<host>.config.system.build.toplevel.drvPath`) had been run manually across 13+ past sessions but never turned into a skill.

- User then asked to commit+push it (git-commit → git-push, standard flow) — noticed this exact sequence (propose → build via `new-skill` → test → commit → push) is itself a recurring, un-skilled pattern, so built **`ship-skill`** (global, repo-managed: `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/ship-skill/`, wired into `bosko-claude.nix`) — an Orchestration skill chaining `skill-suggestion`/`new-skill` → smoke-test → `git-commit` → a deliberate confirmation pause → `git-push`. The push-step pause is intentional: by that point the skill has already made two consequential decisions (built + committed) with no user checkpoint, so it doesn't compound a third autonomously.
- Verified `ship-skill` went live without a new session: after the user ran `nh os boot` (they said "Rebuilt"), `~/.claude/skills/ship-skill/SKILL.md` already resolved to a real `/nix/store` path pointing at the just-committed file — the session-38 assumption that global-skill fixes need "rebuild + reboot + new session" only needed the rebuild; the running session picks up the new symlink immediately once it exists on disk. Session 38's PENDING note below is now resolved (see edit).
- Ran a self-paced `/loop /ship-skill` to look for a third skill: the loop's own iteration re-invoked `skill-suggestion`, which forked another mining pass (112k tokens, checked all 76 transcripts + memory + one near-miss on DankMaterialShell theming commands) and returned a clean **"nothing found"** verdict — every recurring pattern is already covered by an existing skill. The loop self-stopped after one iteration, exactly as designed (stop condition: skill-suggestion finds nothing left).

**Dolphin dark-theme fix + second full `/improve-system` pass — 5 commits, pushed (2026-07-10, session 38).** Commits `58f44a0`..`ea91d32`.
- **Dolphin theming bug, root-caused and fixed.** User reported the newly-added `kdePackages.dolphin` (added last session, `3f20488`) rendering light despite `qt6ct` being configured for dark theming. Live-diagnosed on gaming via `grim` screenshots (confirmed with display access in this session): two independent causes stacked. (1) Unwrapped Qt binaries (dolphin, qbittorrent) only search plugin dirs baked into their own build-time RPATH, which never includes an unrelated package like `qt6ct` — `QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME=qt6ct` was unresolvable without `environment.profileRelativeSessionVariables.QT_PLUGIN_PATH` pointing every session app at the merged system-profile plugin dir. (2) Even after fixing that (confirmed via qBittorrent going dark), Dolphin specifically stayed light — full KDE Frameworks apps resolve their palette via `KColorScheme` reading `kdeglobals`, a path entirely separate from `QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME`; outside a real Plasma session nothing keeps `kdeglobals`'s scheme pointer live. Root cause + fix (`[UiSettings] ColorScheme=*`) came from a 4-agent `/research` pass across NixOS Discourse/Arch BBS threads, cross-referencing an upstream `nixpkgs` issue. Fix applied in `modules/desktop-environments/niri.nix`: `QT_PLUGIN_PATH` session var + a `kwriteconfig6`-based `home.activation` script (not a `home.file` symlink, since `kdeglobals` is actively written back by KDE apps — force-managing it read-only would break that). **Verified live, not just in theory**: screenshotted dolphin light → applied `nixos-rebuild switch` (user-run) → still light (systemd --user session hadn't refreshed its env) → pushed the var into the running session and re-tested → confirmed dark. Committed `58f44a0`.

Commit `ea91d32`.

Turns out a full new session isn't required for a managed-global skill to become invocable, only the rebuild — the running session picks up a new/changed `~/.claude/skills/*` symlink as soon as it exists on disk (confirmed directly with `ship-skill` in session 39).

**Repo layout restructured — `modules/` moved top-level, desktop hosts deduped, DE smoke checks added to CI; pushed and CI-green (2026-07-09, session 37).** One commit, pushed: `589bece` (59 files, net −72 lines). A second commit in the close range, `601d824` (niri: workspace-switch keys rebound to arrows, `Mod+T` dropped), predates this conversation — no transcript rationale to record.
- **`dotfiles/common/modules/` → top-level `modules/`** via `git mv` (100% renames, history preserved). `dotfiles/` now truthfully holds only Home Manager material (`common/configs/`, `bosko/`). The two depth-dependent relative sops paths (`vpn.nix`, `sops.nix`: `../../../secrets/` → `../secrets/`) were the only in-file changes the move itself required.
- **Desktop-host dedup** — new `modules/desktop-apps.nix` (the 13 packages + 3 flatpaks previously copy-pasted per host) and `modules/desktop-networking.nix` (NetworkManager/DNS/firewall/chrony/sshd block, previously ~95% identical across the three `networking.nix` files, already drifting). Host `networking.nix` files now hold only the wg0 address (plus natalie-laptop's extra `AllowUsers = [ "natty" ]`, which list-merges with the shared `bosko`); host `environment.nix` files hold only host-unique packages/flatpaks.
- **`mkSystem` reworked** — now `{ name, system ? x86_64, nixpkgs ? unstable, modules }`; sets `networking.hostName` from `name` and injects `specialArgs` once. All per-host `hostName` assignments (including vpn-server's) and the repeated `specialArgs`/`inherit` boilerplate deleted. `system.stateVersion` moved out of `commonModules` into each host's own file (frozen at install time — a future host installed under a newer release must not inherit a shared value). financeguru import now uses `pkgs.stdenv.hostPlatform.system` instead of hardcoded `x86_64-linux`. `vpn.nix` moved into `desktopModules` (all three desktops are VPN clients).
- **DE-rot protection** — new `lib.deSmoke` flake output: the laptop config with each of the 11 DE modules swapped in (enumerated live from the directory, so new DE modules are picked up automatically). New CI `de-smoke` job deep-evals every one, gated to `schedule` (weekly, Mon 06:00 UTC) + `workflow_dispatch` so push/PR runs stay fast (~3 min); the job itself measured 4m57s. **It caught real rot immediately**: `pantheon.pantheon-terminal` no longer exists in nixpkgs (→ `pantheon.elementary-terminal`, verified via mcp-nixos) and `mate.nix` used three deprecated `mate.*` aliases (→ top-level `caja`/`mate-panel-with-applets`/`mate-terminal`). All fixed; all 11 DEs evaluate.
- **Docs/skills refreshed to the new layout** — CLAUDE.md (layout diagram, module composition, gaming=Niri fix, DE how-to), README (four-not-five hosts, diagrams, mkSystem description), six path-referencing project skills, the new-host flake-entry + host-file templates (old mkSystem signature replaced; templates also gained per-host `stateVersion` and lost `hostName`), and ten memory files that referenced old paths.
- **Verified, in order:** baseline `toplevel.drvPath` captured for all 4 hosts pre-change; post-change vpn-server drvPath **bit-identical**; the three desktop drvPaths differ only from package-list merge ordering — a field-level eval comparison (sorted package names, flatpaks, sshd settings, nameservers, firewall, wg addresses, hostName, stateVersion) against committed HEAD came back IDENTICAL for all three; `nix flake check` clean; dry-run on gaming a functional no-op (+3.06 KiB, 0 package changes); CI run `29064535668` green on both jobs (push-triggered run was cancelled by the workflow's own concurrency group when the dispatch run superseded it — expected, not a failure).
- **PENDING: nothing new needs activation.** The refactor is semantically a no-op on every host; it rides along invisibly with the already-pending rebuilds. The weekly de-smoke CI job fires on its own schedule.
