**qBittorrent/Qt apps under niri+DMS actually fixed — a missing `qt6ct.conf` was the real gap, now Home-Manager-managed (2026-07-09, session 35).** One commit this session: `b3bbb47`. A prior, unclosed session had already landed `8cabb8b` ("add qt6ct so Qt apps follow DMS dark/light theme") between session 34's close and this conversation — no transcript exists for it beyond the commit message, so its rationale isn't re-narrated here. User reported that fix didn't actually work (screenshot showed qBittorrent still light-themed); this session found and fixed the real cause via `/research` + live debugging.
- **Root cause, diagnosed from DMS's own logs/settings rather than assumed:** `8cabb8b`'s `qt6ct` package + `QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME=qt6ct` were necessary but not sufficient. DMS's `~/.config/DankMaterialShell/settings.json` had `qtThemingEnabled: false` — it always generates the matugen Qt color file (`~/.config/qt6ct/colors/matugen.conf`) but only *applies* it when this flag is on. Flipped it `true` live. Even then, DMS's Go backend only `chtimes` (touches mtime of) an existing `qt6ct.conf` to trigger a reload — it never creates the file (`journalctl --user -u dms.service`: `WARN go: Failed to touch qt6ct.conf: ... no such file or directory`). That seed file is normally created once via the `qt6ct` GUI, which had never been run on this host.
- **`niri.nix` (`b3bbb47`)** — added `.config/qt6ct/qt6ct.conf` as an HM `home.file` under `home-manager.users.bosko` (gaming + laptop only, same scoping as the existing `config.kdl` management): `custom_palette=true`, `style=Fusion`, `color_scheme_path` templated via `config.home.homeDirectory` pointing at DMS's still-dynamic `matugen.conf`. Deliberately did **not** pull `settings.json` or `matugen.conf` into Nix: `settings.json` is rewritten live by DMS on every UI interaction (a read-only Nix symlink would break the settings UI itself, per session 34's identical reasoning for `dms/*.kdl`), and `matugen.conf` is meant to be regenerated on every wallpaper/theme change.
- **Verified end-to-end, not just eval:** wrote the identical file directly to the live gaming host first, restarted qBittorrent (`kill` + relaunch — safe, resumes seeding, no torrent data touched), and confirmed via `grim` screenshots that it actually rendered dark before committing the Nix version. Then `nixos-dry-run` on gaming (clean, +2.33 KiB, only the new HM file) and a deep-eval of laptop's `toplevel.drvPath` (clean) since both hosts share `niri.nix`.
- **PENDING:** the Nix-managed `qt6ct.conf` reaches gaming/laptop only on their next `nh os boot` + reboot — the file already live on gaming (hand-written this session, identical content) is what's actually in effect there today. On a *fresh* install or a wiped `~/.config`, DMS's `qtThemingEnabled` toggle still needs to be flipped once by hand (Settings → Theme & Colors → "Apply Qt Themes", or edit `settings.json` directly) — that half is intentionally left unmanaged.

**Niri `config.kdl` brought under Home Manager management + keybind/layout tweaks, all staged pending reboot (2026-07-09, session 34).** Four commits, not yet pushed at close time: `b9913b5`, `e38f114`, `0cbeb1d`, `bf2ba35`. User confirmed gaming's Niri switch (session 33) is working well and asked what should move from ad-hoc `$HOME` state into dotfiles.
- **`dotfiles/common/configs/niri-config.kdl` (new) + `niri.nix` (`b9913b5`)** — `~/.config/niri/config.kdl` (input/layout/binds/window-rules) was a plain mutable file with no version history; copied it into the repo and wired it as a `home.file` symlink (`force = true`, same pattern as `katerc`/`kitty.conf`) inside `niri.nix` itself rather than shared `home.nix`, scoping it to the two hosts that actually import that module (gaming, laptop). `self` added to the module's args to resolve the source path. **Deliberately left unmanaged**: DMS's own generated state — `~/.config/DankMaterialShell/settings.json` and `~/.config/niri/dms/*.kdl` (colors/layout/outputs/binds/etc.) — since those are written live by DMS's settings UI; a Nix-managed symlink would make that UI's writes fail or get clobbered on rebuild.
- **Accepted tradeoff, discussed explicitly with user**: only `dms/outputs.kdl` is currently `include`d in `config.kdl`; other DMS-generated files (e.g. `colors.kdl`) carry comments implying DMS expects to auto-inject their own `include` line into `config.kdl` when a matching integration toggle is enabled (e.g. `matugenTemplateNiri`). Since `config.kdl` is now a read-only Nix symlink, a future DMS auto-injection would silently fail — user's call was to accept this as a minor, self-announcing papercut (the DMS toggle just wouldn't visually apply) in exchange for permanent reproducibility, rather than leave the file unmanaged.
- **Pruned the stock `spawn-at-startup "waybar"` default (`e38f114`)** — leftover from niri's default template; DMS's `dank-material-shell` is the actual panel/bar here and `waybar` isn't even installed, so the line would fail silently on login.
- **Added `Mod+Return` → `kitty` and `Mod+B` → browser keybinds (`0cbeb1d`)** — kitty is already installed system-wide on all three desktop hosts with its own dotfiles-managed config. The browser bind uses `xdg-open https:// || flatpak run app.zen_browser.zen`: confirmed live that `~/.config/mimeapps.list` already resolves `x-scheme-handler/https` to `app.zen_browser.zen.desktop`, so `xdg-open` alone would work today, but that mimeapps file isn't dotfiles-managed — the flatpak fallback covers a fresh install with no default set yet. Caveat: `xdg-open` can't force `--new-window` generically (no way to inject browser-specific flags through the desktop-file `Exec` line), so if Zen is already running this may open a new tab rather than a new window.
- **Gaps reduced 16px → 8px (`bf2ba35`)** — user-requested layout tweak, no other layout values touched.
- **Verified**: dry-run (`nixos-dry-run` skill) built clean on gaming for the `config.kdl` addition (+832 bytes, then +27.8 KiB after the keybind additions); `nix eval` confirmed laptop's `toplevel.drvPath` also evaluates (laptop imports the same `niri.nix`). Gaps change is a plain config value with nothing to evaluate.
- **PENDING**: nothing applied yet — this only takes effect once `home.file` actually links the tracked `config.kdl` over the live mutable one, which happens on the hosts' next `nh os boot` + reboot (gaming, laptop).

**gaming switched from Plasma 6 to Niri; nvidia.nix gained Niri-specific session vars, committed (2026-07-08, session 33).** One commit, not yet pushed at close time: `34a7dbe`. User wants to test gaming on this host and asked to switch its DE to Niri, plus have anything missing for that combination added. Confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before editing: Plasma's **Wayland** session (not X11) was already running on this GPU, so nvidia+Wayland is proven on this hardware — lowering risk relative to a first-ever nvidia+Wayland attempt. User opted to add the standard nvidia+Wayland-compositor safety-net variables preemptively rather than wait for a visible glitch, and to do a full DE swap (not a dry-run-only preview), accepting that the existing generation-rollback safety net (previous Plasma generation stays selectable at the boot menu) covers the risk instead of a dual-session setup.
- **`flake.nix`** — gaming's DE import line changed from `desktop-environments/plasma.nix` → `desktop-environments/niri.nix`. One-line change via the same pattern the `switch-de` skill uses.
- **`modules/nvidia.nix`** — added `environment.sessionVariables`: `GBM_BACKEND = "nvidia-drm"`, `__GLX_VENDOR_LIBRARY_NAME = "nvidia"`, `LIBVA_DRIVER_NAME = "nvidia"`. Rationale: **Niri is Smithay-based, not wlroots** — a distinction worth remembering, since most "nvidia+Wayland fix" folklore (e.g. `WLR_NO_HARDWARE_CURSORS`) is wlroots-specific and would be a no-op under Niri. The three vars added are the ones that actually matter here: GBM buffer allocation and GLX/VA-API vendor selection aren't auto-detected the way KWin does it. Scoped to `nvidia.nix` (not `niri.nix`) so they apply only on hosts that actually import nvidia — currently gaming only; laptop and natalie-laptop (non-nvidia) are unaffected.
- **Everything else needed for gaming-under-Niri was already in place**, verified by reading the relevant modules rather than assumed: Steam/gamescope/gamemode/mangohud/controller udev rules are DE-agnostic (`gaming.nix`), `xwayland-satellite` for Steam's X11-only windows is already in `niri.nix` (niri ≥ 25.08 spawns it itself), and SDDM+Niri as a session pairing is already proven working on `laptop`. No `services.displayManager.defaultSession` override exists in gaming's `environment.nix` to conflict with the DE-family boundary crossed (X11-capable Plasma → Wayland-only Niri).
- **Verified**: `nh os boot --dry` (via the `nixos-dry-run` skill) built clean — `niri` package, its XDG desktop portal, and `unit-niri.service` added; the KDE/Plasma closure removed (`PATHS: 3517 -> 3290`, `SIZE: 33.6 GiB -> 32.1 GiB`, `DIFF: -1.48 GiB`). No eval errors.
- **PENDING**: nothing applied yet — staged for next boot only. First activation requires a manual reboot (staging convention: `rebuild` = `nh os boot`, never auto-applied). If Niri turns out broken for gaming, the previous Plasma generation stays selectable at the boot menu — no dual-DE session was set up as a fallback, by user's explicit choice.

**natalie-laptop LAN IP drift fixed (`.103` → `.101`) + project `commit`/`push` skills retired in favor of the global pair (2026-07-03, session 30).** Two commits this session (`6ceb653`, `fb94dfc`), both pushed and both certified green by the deep-eval CI. A third commit sits in the close range but predates this conversation: `289b8f1` (dms + financeguru input bump, lock-only — no transcript rationale to record).

- **natalie-laptop's LAN IP changed from 10.0.0.103 to 10.0.0.101 (`6ceb653`).** Root cause is by-design, not a fault: the host has **no static address and no router DHCP reservation** — `hosts/natalie-laptop/networking.nix` just enables NetworkManager, and the live SSH check showed the Wi-Fi lease flagged `dynamic`. The router reassigned the address (lease expiry / router reboot / another device claiming .103 while the laptop was offline); nothing on the NixOS side changed. Updated the two live references — `.claude/hosts.json` (source of truth the skills read) and `dotfiles/common/configs/ssh.nix` (the HM-managed `ssh natalie-laptop` alias) — plus this doc's line 147. **Verified**: ping + SSH to .101 answers as `natalie-laptop`; dry-run clean (41 KiB, only the regenerated sshconfig); CI green (2m2s). **PENDING:** the ssh.nix edit reaches each host's `~/.ssh/config` only on that host's next rebuild — until then the `ssh natalie-laptop` alias on every desktop host still targets the stale .103 (use `ssh bosko@10.0.0.101` directly). **Prevention option (router-UI task, not repo):** add a DHCP reservation pinning the laptop's Wi-Fi MAC to .101 — gaming and laptop are equally unprotected and one router lease-table wipe away from the same drift.
- **Project-local `commit`/`push` skills deleted (`fb94dfc`).** After session 28 closed the behavioral gaps, the project pair and the global `git-commit`/`git-push` were near-identical duplicates with overlapping trigger phrases — skill selection was a coin flip (this session's own commits resolved to the global pair with zero loss). **nixpkgs unpinned (zen fixed upstream) + CI added and then hardened by a real incident + backups evaluated and scrapped (2026-07-02, session 29).** Six commits (`48c11d3`..`6cce517`), all pushed. Started as an open "what would you improve?" assessment; three of four proposals resolved, one scrapped:

- **nixpkgs pin lifted (`48c11d3`)** — `/flake-update-verify` bumped all 5 inputs; nixpkgs `567a49d1` → `b5aa0fbd` (2026-06-29), off the session-19 zen-kernel hold. Verified the pin's exact cause is gone: linux-zen **7.1.2** ships `bzImage` again (built the kernel derivation from cache and checked the file — 14 MB, present). The zen-pin memory was deleted per its own instruction. **PENDING:** no host has activated the new lock yet — first rebuild per host pulls zen 7.1.2 + ~2 weeks of unstable (large download).
- **GitHub Actions CI added (`2964759`), then hardened (`3015529`) after it passed a genuinely broken config.** `.github/workflows/check.yml` runs on every push/PR: `nix flake check --no-build` **plus a per-host deep-eval** of `config.system.build.toplevel.drvPath` (all four hosts; aarch64 vpn-server evals fine on the free x86 runner; sops stays encrypted — zero repo secrets needed). The deep-eval step exists because of the **pnpm/vesktop incident** (below): `nix flake check` verifies nixosConfigurations only **shallowly**, so it passed while every desktop host failed to evaluate. Verified green end-to-end (2m20s run).
- **pnpm/vesktop incident — the new lock was briefly un-rebuildable, caught by the `/improve-system` dry-run certification.** The bumped nixpkgs marks vesktop's pinned build tool `pnpm-10.29.2` insecure (CVE-2026-48995 + 6 more, pnpm CLI vulns — build-time only); vesktop is on all three desktop hosts, so **all three failed eval** while both local `flake check` and the fresh CI stayed green (shallow-check blind spot). Traced via `builtins.tryEval` bisection of `environment.systemPackages` (the option-eval trace pointed at `environment.etc.dbus-1.source` → `system-path`). Fix (`aa3d690`): temporary `nixpkgs.config.permittedInsecurePackages = [ "pnpm-10.29.2" ]` in `nix.nix`, loudly commented; nixpkgs master already switched vesktop to `pnpm_10` (`4b3d28a4`, 2026-06-29) but it hasn't reached nixos-unstable — **remove the waiver once it does** (tracked in Known Issues + memory). Re-verified after the fix: all four hosts deep-eval clean, gaming dry-run exit 0, CI green.
- **`/improve-system` pass (rest of it):** 6 gotchas across 5 skills (`c9b8c08`) — interview (AskUserQuestion 60s timeout ≠ an answer), flake-update-verify (pre-approved runs skip ON-mode pauses; flake-check alone is insufficient verification), flake-check (shallow for nixosConfigurations), bump-input (`nix flake lock --update-input` no longer exists in current nix — use `nix flake update <name> --flake`), skill-audit (`enumerate-skills.sh` lives in the skill's base dir, not repo `scripts/`). claude-rules + fewer-permission-prompts: clean/no-op. skill-audit: covered by session 28's full sweep (library unchanged since), not re-run.
- **New project-local `ci-status` skill (`6cce517`)** — Verification bucket; "is CI green" / "check CI" → latest `flake check` run, watches to completion, on red pulls `--log-failed` with per-host attribution. Live immediately (no rebuild).
- **Backup system evaluated and SCRAPPED (user decision, recorded in memory):** projects live in GitHub, photos/videos are already cloud-backed, games re-download, and the only unbacked data (Jellyfin state) is an accepted loss. Also corrected a wrong claim I made: the sops admin age key is on **both gaming and laptop** (two copies), per the existing sops memory — not single-copy. Auto-GC on desktops was also declined (user runs `cleanup` manually after rebuilds).

**OnlyOffice font detection FIXED and user-confirmed working; `/improve-system` skill-audit run + all 13 findings fixed (2026-07-02, session 28).** Two threads, 15 commits (`77c5014`..`07ad1d2`), all pushed.

- **OnlyOffice fonts — root cause finally isolated and fixed (`77c5014`), resolving the session-26 "outcome unverified" issue.** The session-26 fix (`57ec032`, real files instead of `symlinkJoin`) turned out necessary but not sufficient. Built the package directly and inspected the actual sandbox rootfs: `buildFHSEnv`'s own internal rootfs-merge step (a Rust `rootfs-builder` tool) re-symlinks every file from every `targetPkgs` derivation when assembling the sandbox's `/usr` — independent of whether the source was a real file. So every font was still one symlink hop away, which OnlyOffice's scanner (`ONLYOFFICE/DocumentServer#1859`) still refuses to follow. Fix: `modules/fonts.nix` now also overrides the `buildFHSEnv` argument itself, appending an `extraBuildCommands` step that runs after its rootfs merge and replaces the symlinks it just created with real copies. **Verified two ways**: built the package and confirmed the rootfs had 286/286 real files (0 symlinks, was 286/286 symlinks before); then user ran `nh os switch` on gaming (no reboot needed — userspace-only package) and **confirmed fonts now show up in the OnlyOffice font dropdown**. This is COMPLETE, not pending — see Known Issues cleanup.
- **`/improve-system` full run + all 13 skill-audit findings fixed, one at a time with a commit after each (`a5a0c82`..`07ad1d2`).** Ran all 5 sub-skills; `skill-upgrade` added a Gotchas entry to `rollback` (bare `sudo` fails without an interactive password — no NOPASSWD rule on any host, confirmed recurring across 9+ past sessions); `fewer-permission-prompts` added 5 allow-list entries; `claude-rules` found all 4 standing rules already present. `skill-audit` fanned 4 disjoint sub-agents across all 49 skills and found real bugs, all fixed:
  1. **`enumerate-skills.sh` (skill-audit's own script) was broken** — `set -euo pipefail` + `grep -v` aborted the whole script the moment a skill dir had only `SKILL.md` (29/49 skills); confirmed live (exit 1, zero output before the fix).
  2. **Stale `Claude Opus 4.8` co-author line** hardcoded in 4 skills (`commit`, `git-commit`, `session-closer`, `repo-creator`) — replaced with "whatever the harness's own instructions specify" so it can't drift again.
  3. **`nixos-gc`'s `scripts/gc.sh`** had 4 bare `sudo` calls with no user-handoff (same bug class as `rollback`) — added an unprivileged `gc-preview.sh` (reads generation symlinks directly, no sudo) and now hands the actual deletion off to the user.
  4. **`git-commit`/`git-push` (global) had drifted from `commit`/`push` (project-local)** — added the missing confirm-skip logic to `git-push` (it had *no* confirmation gate at all) and documented `git-commit`'s already-correct skip-confirm behavior; deliberately did **not** copy the `-C /home/bosko/NixOS` pinning, since that would break the global skills in the user's other repos (farmer, FinanceGuru).
  5. **`ssh-host`** promised a `server` alias in its description with no matching entry — added it.
  6. **`switch-de`**'s static DE table said natalie-laptop runs `cosmic.nix`; actual `flake.nix` says `plasma.nix` — fixed (not load-bearing, the skill already reads `flake.nix` live, but misleading to a human skimmer).
  7. **`new-peer`** hardcoded the VPN peer IP roster in prose — added a `vpnIp` field to each host's entry in `hosts.json` and made the skill read it live, cross-checked against the real `hosts/vpn-server/configuration.nix`.
  8. **`bump-input`/`pin-input`** both re-improvised an identical `nix flake metadata | python3 -c ...` parse — extracted to a shared `.claude/lib/list-flake-inputs.sh`.
  9. **`new-host`**'s sops age-key-derive sequence was retyped in prose — extracted to `scripts/derive-age-key.sh <hostname>`.
  10. **`new-skill`**'s global-vs-project-local scope pick was buried in a free-form question list — moved to the AskUserQuestion tool.
  11-12. **`audit-config`/`save-memory`** were both missing `## Arguments` sections despite taking real inputs — added.
  13. **team-member family** (`team-meeting`/`team-member-ingest`/`team-member-synthesize`/`new-team-member`) all hardcode the identical knowledge-base path with no cross-reference — added cross-references to all four (a real shared `config.json` isn't practical for separately-symlinked global skills, given the low actual drift risk of a static path).
  **PENDING:** the global-skill fixes (#1, #2's non-`commit` copies, #3's sudo-gotcha pattern already matches `rollback`, #4, #10, #13) reach `~/.claude/skills/` only after `nh os boot /home/bosko/NixOS` **+ a new session**. All project-local `.claude/skills/` fixes (#3's `gc-preview.sh`, #5, #6, #7, #8, #9, #11, #12) are **live now**.

**Leaked password hashes found on 5 stray public git branches, deleted (2026-07-02, session 27).** Not a repo-config change — a `secret-scan` run (part of this close) surfaced a real finding: the 2026-06-15 sops migration's `git filter-repo` purge only rewrote `main`. Five old version-tag branches (`3.5`, `4.0`, `4.1`, `4.1.1`, `4.1.2`) — pushed to the public `origin` remote and not ancestors of `main` — still carried commit `296a2bd` (2026-05-14) with the original plaintext `bosko`/`natty` `$6$` password hashes, publicly fetchable since. **Fixed**: all 5 branches deleted from `origin` (`git push origin --delete 3.5 4.0 4.1 4.1.1 4.1.2`) and pruned locally; verified via `git fetch --prune` plus a repo-wide `git log --all -G'\$6\$'` scan that no remaining ref carries the hash. User was offered password rotation given the ~7-week exposure window and **declined** — judged the practical risk low ("I doubt anyone looked at them"). Corrected the `project_sops_secrets` memory, which had wrongly recorded the purge as complete.

**Global `research` skill added (2026-07-02, session 27).** A pure Claude-tooling session; **no NixOS system config changed** (only `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/research/SKILL.md` and the `bosko-claude.nix` symlink wiring). One commit (`d8d5aef`), pushed. New global/repo-managed **Data Enrichment** skill: given a topic, runs `WebSearch`, then fans out `n` (default 10, overridable via `research <topic> <n>`) fresh `general-purpose` sub-agents in a single parallel batch — one per result URL — each instructed to `WebFetch` its page, extract findings/stance/caveats, and report back concisely (or explicitly flag "inaccessible: <reason>" for paywalled/PDF/JS-heavy pages rather than fabricating). Results are synthesized into a chat-only consensus report (majority view, notable dissent, per-source takeaways, and a coverage count of usable vs. inaccessible sources). Built via `/new-skill`, with the agent-type/count/output-format choices resolved through `AskUserQuestion` before drafting. Dry-run passed clean (+5.62 MiB, only the `corefonts` addition from the prior session's font work, no kernel/service changes). **PENDING:** repo-managed global skill — reaches `~/.claude/skills/` only after `nh os boot /home/bosko/NixOS` **+ a new session**.

**niri Xwayland fix landed; OnlyOffice font fix partially reverted — untracked session, no session-close (2026-07-01, session 26).** Five commits (`47e332f`..`873e824`), pushed; this session was never closed at the time, so this entry reconstructs it from commit messages only — no transcript rationale beyond what's written there.
- **niri X11 app support, resolved cleanly.** First pass (`47e332f`) added a manual `xwayland-satellite` user service + `DISPLAY=":0"` session export because niri has no built-in X server and `onlyoffice-desktopeditors` (Qt/GTK-X11) couldn't connect to any X display. That broke the SDDM greeter (`40a93bd`): the global `DISPLAY` export leaked into SDDM's Wayland greeter session, so Weston loaded its X11 backend instead of DRM and crashed with a black screen at boot. Fix: dropped the global `DISPLAY` export and removed the manual service entirely — niri ≥ 25.08 (the laptop runs 26.04) spawns `xwayland-satellite` itself and sets `DISPLAY` automatically for the session. End state in `modules/desktop-environments/niri.nix`: only the `xwayland-satellite` package stays in `systemPackages` (with a comment explaining niri handles the rest). This looks resolved.
- **OnlyOffice font detection — outcome unclear, needs verification.** `10a1faf` enabled `fonts.fontDir.enable` and added `corefonts` (+ existing `liberation_ttf`) in the shared `modules/fonts.nix`, reasoning that OnlyOffice builds its own font cache from fixed directories instead of using fontconfig. `371e08b` followed up with a `systemd-tmpfiles` symlink bridging `/usr/share/fonts` → the fontDir tree, reasoning that OnlyOffice's font engine only scans FHS paths (`/usr/share/fonts`, `/usr/share/X11/fonts`, etc.) which don't exist on NixOS, so `fontDir` alone (exposing fonts under `/run/current-system/sw/share/X11/fonts`) wouldn't be enough. **`873e824` then reverted that symlink commit** with no rationale beyond "Revert ..." in the message — unknown from git alone whether it broke something else, was found unnecessary, or was undone for another reason. **Current end state:** `fontDir.enable = true` + `corefonts` remain active (all hosts, via `commonModules`), but the FHS-path symlink bridge is gone. Per the now-reverted commit's own stated reasoning, that may mean OnlyOffice (installed on gaming, laptop, natalie-laptop) still can't see the fonts. **Needs verification next session** — see Known Issues / Next Steps.

**VirtualBox host added to natalie-laptop for a Windows VM (2026-06-30, session 25).** A one-commit config session (`3023af2`, pushed). New module `hosts/natalie-laptop/virtualisation.nix` enables `virtualisation.virtualbox.host` with `enableExtensionPack = true` (USB 2.0/3.0 passthrough, RDP, disk encryption — covered by the global `nixpkgs.config.allowUnfree` in `nix.nix`, so eval isn't blocked by the PUEL license), adds **both `natty` and `bosko`** to `vboxusers` via `users.extraGroups.vboxusers.members`, and is imported in the `natalie-laptop` flake entry. Followed the same host-module pattern as `hosts/gaming/virtualisation.nix`. **No separate Guest Additions package** — the VirtualBox host package bundles `VBoxGuestAdditions_7.2.8.iso` (confirmed present in the build plan), mountable from the running guest via *Devices → Insert Guest Additions CD*. Also **corrected a doc drift**: `CLAUDE.md` still said "Natalie-laptop: Cosmic / Wayland" — it's run **Plasma 6** since session 3 (2026-06-04/05); fixed to Plasma. (No memory files named Cosmic, so nothing to change there.) **Verification:** the local host is `gaming`, so `nh os boot --dry` would build the *wrong* host — instead ran `nix build .#nixosConfigurations.natalie-laptop.config.system.build.toplevel --dry-run`, which evaluated clean and showed the full VBox stack queued: `virtualbox-7.2.8` (from source, forced by the ext pack), `virtualbox-extpack-7.2.8`, `virtualbox-modules-7.2.8-7.0.12` (matching the zen 7.0.12 kernel), `vboxnet0` service, and the AppArmor-integrated `VBox*` security wrappers. **PENDING:** nothing applied — reaches natalie-laptop only on its next `nh os boot` + reboot (expect a **long from-source VirtualBox build** because of the ext pack), after which `natty`/`bosko` must **log out/in once** for the `vboxusers` group to take effect before creating the Windows VM.

**disko-on-other-hosts review — decided to keep it vpn-server-only; corrected a stale agent memory (2026-06-29, session 24).** A discussion/review session; **no repo changes** (docs-only close). Question: would adopting `disko` on the remaining hosts be advantageous? Conclusion: **no — keep disko on `vpn-server` only.** Its payoff is entirely at install/reinstall time (declarative partition + format + mount on a fresh disk), which fits the headless, reproducible Oracle Cloud host but gives **zero runtime benefit** on the already-installed, data-bearing desktops (`gaming`, `laptop`, `natalie-laptop`); retrofitting would mean maintaining two sources of truth (disko config + the generated `hardware-configuration.nix`) and a layout you'd only discover is wrong on a wipe — worst possible time. `natalie-laptop` was briefly floated as a candidate under a **wrong assumption** that it was still unprovisioned; that turned out to be stale **agent-memory** drift (a 49-day-old `project_natalie_laptop` memory still said "placeholder hardware config — replace during install"). Verified on disk: `hosts/natalie-laptop/hardware-configuration.nix` is the **real generated config** (Intel host — `kvm-intel`, nvme, ext4 root with real UUIDs), and the repo docs (README, this file) already reflected "installed & running." Fixed the memory file + its `MEMORY.md` index line to match; no repo-doc edits were needed.

**`session-closer` now reads the on-disk transcript + `brobot` module dropped + permissions consistency check (2026-06-29, session 23).** A small skills/config session; two commits, both pushed. (1) **`session-closer` reads the JSONL transcript (`3cdd88b`)** — STEP 2 of the global `session-closer` skill now reads the session's on-disk transcript at `~/.claude/projects/<cwd-slug>/*.jsonl` (newest file = live session) as ground truth, because the live context window gets summarized/truncated as a conversation grows so early decisions scroll out. Verified the dir-derivation (`pwd | sed 's#/#-#g'` → `-home-bosko-NixOS`) and two `jq` extractors (user turns + assistant text) against the live transcript before writing; guards against dumping multi-MB raw JSONL. **PENDING:** repo-managed global skill — reaches `~/.claude/skills/` only after `nh os boot` **+ a new session**; rides the existing session-18 pending rebuild. *(Ironic note: this very close ran on the OLD session-closer, since the edit isn't live yet.)* (2) **`brobot` module dropped (`2ca32be`)** — removed `hosts/gaming/brobot.nix` and its `flake.nix` import; the Brobot Discord bot is discontinued. Dry-run passed (−187 MiB on gaming: `yt-dlp` + closure removed; `ffmpeg`/`nodejs` stay, still pulled in by Jellyfin/tooling). **PENDING:** removed from the live gaming system only on its next `nh os boot` + reboot. (3) **Permissions consistency check (no code change)** — confirmed the managed `deny`/`ask`/fork-bomb-hook layer (`/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json`, from `claude-code.nix`) is a deliberately separate, higher-precedence layer from what `/improve-system` orchestrates (its `fewer-permission-prompts` step only ever appends `permissions.allow` to the **project** `.claude/settings.json`). They should **not** be synced/merged — `bosko-claude.nix`'s `trimClaudeSettings` activation already actively strips any `deny`/`ask`/`hooks` that drift into the personal settings, by design. No drift found.

**`/improve-system` first full run — bug-fix + consolidation + global-refactor pass over the skill library (2026-06-28, session 22).** A pure Claude-tooling session; **no NixOS system config changed** (only `.claude/` skills, `dotfiles/bosko/claude/` skill sources, and the `bosko-claude.nix` symlink wiring). Three commits (`c75e2eb`, `f418d4e`, `ece81ef`), pushed at close. Ran the session-20 `/improve-system` orchestrator **end-to-end for the first time**, then acted on what it found in three waves:

- **`fix` (`c75e2eb`) — 4 verified bugs + 4 drift fixes.** `skill-audit` fanned a read-only audit over **49 skills** via 5 disjoint sub-agents. Real bugs, each verified live before fixing: **`vpn-status` was broken again** — its inline peer roster used `10.0.0.x` (the **LAN** subnet) when the WireGuard subnet is `10.10.0.x`; replaced with a `hosts.json` `.vpn.peers` lookup (this is the *second* vpn-status drift bug, after session-18's `ubuntu@` user). **`add-package`** routed system packages to a **phantom `hosts/server/`** that doesn't exist. **`new-peer`** told new NixOS hosts to drop their key at `/etc/wireguard/private.key` when the repo wires wg keys via **sops-nix** (`config.sops.secrets."wg-private-key".path`). **`flake-check`** claimed "five hosts" (only four). Drift: `fleet-status` Notes described a removed `case` block; `journal`/`new-module` listed the phantom `server`; **`commit` still mandated a confirm-or-edit gate** that contradicts the `feedback_commit_confirmation` memory — removed. Also auto-applied: a cross-session gotcha to `improve-system` (the built-in `fewer-permission-prompts` has emitted invalid `settings.json` — empty-paren entries fail validation, and it shells out to `jq`/`python3` that aren't always on PATH), and a new `.claude/settings.json` allow-listing the `nixos-dry-run` script.

- **`refactor` (`f418d4e`) — cross-cutting consolidation.** Lifted the desktop-host roster + per-host config paths (re-typed inline across `add-flatpak`/`add-package`/`switch-de`/`new-module` — which is how the phantom `server` drifted in) into **`.claude/hosts.json`** as new `desktop` + `envFile` fields; the four skills now derive from `.desktop`/`.envFile`/`.flakeHosts`. Fixed `save-memory`'s `assets/memory-template.md` to emit `node_type: memory` + `originSessionId` (20/22 live memories carry these). **Deliberately skipped** the team-`*` `knowledge/config.json` consolidation — a config file there has a bootstrap paradox (you must hardcode `knowledge/`'s path to read its config), and the repo root is already hardcoded library-wide, so it'd add indirection for zero drift-reduction (the audit confirmed zero actual drift).

- **`refactor` (`ece81ef`) — global structural refactors** (fanned across 4 sub-agents, then centrally wired). Template → `assets/`: `create-loop` (the 85-line loop contract), `claude-rules` (four rule blocks), `new-skill` (scaffold). Deterministic → `scripts/`: `search-pkg` (+ **nixos MCP is now the primary path** per `project_mcp_nixos`, `nix search` demoted to offline fallback), `session-closer` (summary rotation; + **delegates the README secret-scrub to the `secret-scan` skill** instead of a duplicate rubric), `skill-audit` (enumeration loop). UX/docs: `new-module` + `switch-de` AskUserQuestion gates; `fleet-rollout` now **delegates its health sweep to `fleet-status`**; `## Arguments` added to `ask-team`/`team-member-ingest`/`team-member-synthesize`/`new-peer`/`journal`. **Architectural win:** the 6 global skills that gained sibling files were converted from per-file `SKILL.md` symlinks to **recursive-directory `home.file` symlinks** in `bosko-claude.nix` — so any future `assets/`/`scripts/` under them needs **zero** wiring (see [[feedback_global_skill_recursive_symlinks]]). Dry-run passed (HM `check-link-targets.sh` validated every symlink target). **PENDING:** every `dotfiles/`-based global-skill change (the asset/script extractions, Arguments, MCP/delegation edits, recursive-symlink wiring) reaches `~/.claude/skills/` only after `nh os boot` **+ a new session** — rides the existing session-18 pending rebuild. All project-local `.claude/skills/` changes (`vpn-status`, `add-*`, `flake-check`, `fleet-status`, `fleet-rollout`, `journal`, `new-module`, `new-peer`, `switch-de`, `commit`, `save-memory`, `settings.json`, `hosts.json`) are **live now**.

**`skill-upgrade` + `skill-suggestion` given transcript-history awareness (2026-06-28, session 21).** A small skills-only session; **no NixOS system config changed** (only two `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/` files). One commit, pushed. Asked whether the five skills `/improve-system` chains can read the session transcripts at `~/.claude/projects/`: only `fewer-permission-prompts` (a non-editable built-in) already did; `claude-rules`/`skill-audit` don't need them. The two **session-reactive** sub-skills were scoped only to the live conversation, so both were amended (`76142e0`) to also `grep` the project's past transcripts at `~/.claude/projects/<cwd-slug>/*.jsonl` (this repo → `-home-bosko-NixOS/`, 72 files): `skill-upgrade` now treats a misfire recurring **across sessions** as the highest-value gotcha; `skill-suggestion` treats a workflow repeated **across many sessions** as the strongest reuse signal. Both carry a **grep-not-read guard** (transcripts are multi-MB; reading whole would blow context). Dry-run clean (1.14 KiB `home-manager-files` diff, no kernel/service changes). **PENDING:** repo-managed global skills — the edits reach `~/.claude/skills/` only after `nh os boot` **+ a new session**; rides along with the session-18 pending rebuild (Next Steps item 1).

**`/improve-system` ecosystem orchestrator added + public README scrubbed of VPN network detail (2026-06-28, session 20).** A skills + docs session; **no NixOS system config changed** (only `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/`, the `bosko-claude.nix` wiring, and `README.md`). Two commits, both pushed:

- **`/improve-system` (`31ad852`)** — new global/repo-managed **Orchestration** skill (`dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/improve-system/SKILL.md`, wired into `bosko-claude.nix`'s `home.file`). One command that chains the five Claude-ecosystem maintenance skills — `skill-upgrade` + `skill-suggestion` (session-reactive) and `claude-rules` + `skill-audit` + `fewer-permission-prompts` (proactive) — into a single pass, **auto-applying low-risk additive changes** (gotchas, CLAUDE.md rules, allow-list entries) and **gating structural ones** (new skills, audit refactors) behind confirmation. It *invokes* the sub-skills via the Skill tool rather than reimplementing them, so each stays independently runnable (the user picked "everything" scope + "auto-apply low-risk" mode). **PENDING:** appears in `~/.claude/skills/` only after `nh os boot` + a new session.

- **Public README scrubbed of VPN network detail + `session-closer` guardrail (`a80664d`)** — redacted the Oracle endpoint IP and the VPN/LAN subnets + per-host addresses from the **world-readable** `README.md` (placeholders + a rewritten Secrets note), and added a "No sensitive or secret information — the README is public" guardrail to the `session-closer` skill's README step so future closes keep it clean. **README-only scrub by deliberate choice:** the literal values still live (correctly) in the host config, `.claude/hosts.json`, the state docs, and git history — `wg-quick` needs the endpoint string, and a repo-wide scrub was **declined** because WireGuard's security doesn't depend on endpoint secrecy, so it would risk the tunnel for little gain. The `project_sops_secrets` memory was updated so a future close won't re-add the IP to the README *or* strip it from the configs.

**Flake inputs updated; nixpkgs held back on a zen-kernel upstream breakage (2026-06-26, session 19).** A one-commit maintenance session (`4598fa0`, pushed). Ran `/update` (all 6 inputs bumped), then `/nixos-dry-run` — which **failed**: the 2026-06-26 `nixos-unstable` rev (`e73de5b`) ships a `linux-zen-7.0.12` whose realized output contains only `vmlinuz` + `System.map` (no `bzImage`), yet the kernel derivation still declares its `target` as `bzImage` and `system.boot.loader.kernelFile` follows that — so the toplevel bootloader sanity check (`Expecting …/bzImage`) hard-fails the build. The broken kernel came **from `cache.nixos.org`** (it was one of the 3053 cache downloads), confirming it's an upstream Hydra regression, not a local fault. It would break **all three zen-kernel desktop hosts** (gaming, laptop, natalie-laptop all pull `linuxPackages_zen` from `bootloader.nix`); **vpn-server is unaffected** (forces standard `linuxPackages`). Resolution: **temporarily pinned `nixpkgs` back** to the previous good rev `567a49d1` (2026-06-16) via a **lock-only** `--override-input` (so a future `/update` auto-un-pins once upstream is fixed), keeping the other 5 input bumps (`dms`, `financeguru`, `home-manager`, `nixpkgs-stable`, `sops-nix`). Re-ran the dry-run with the pin — **passed clean** (built `nixos-system-gaming-26.11.20260616.567a49d`, +28 KiB, 0 errors). Committed the `flake.lock`. **PENDING:** nothing applied — this is a lock bump only; it activates on the next `/fleet-rollout` (or per-host `rebuild`). **WATCH:** re-run `/update` in a few days; once the zen kernel ships `bzImage` again the pin lifts on its own — see Known Issues.

**Full Claude skill-library audit + overhaul — 7 commits, all pushed (2026-06-23, session 18).** A pure skills-infrastructure session; **no NixOS system config changed** (only `.claude/` skills, `dotfiles/` skill sources, and `bosko-claude.nix` wiring). Work, in order of leverage:

- **New skills + `new-skill` hardening (`0a7bd2d`, `dc3e1a4`).** Added the **4-bucket single-responsibility gate** to `new-skill` (every skill must fit cleanly in Utility / Verification / Data Enrichment / Orchestration; split straddlers). Created **`bump-input`** (Data Enrichment — bump one flake input to latest, fills the gap between `/update` and `/pin-input`) and **`save-memory`** (Utility — write one memory file + MEMORY.md index line). Split the straddling `team-member-ingest` into ingest (save raw) + new **`team-member-synthesize`** (build profile). Later hardened `new-skill` again so its **global-scope path auto-wires repo-managed skills** into `bosko-claude.nix` (write to `dotfiles/`, add the `home.file` entry, git add, dry-run) instead of the old wrong advice to write into the read-only `~/.claude` symlink.

- **Drift bug fixes (`0853706`) — these were real, some skills were broken.** Inline-duplicated constants had rotted: **`vpn-status` was outright broken** (used `ubuntu@150.136.232.63`, which is **denied** — the box is a NixOS host where **`bosko@` works with passwordless sudo**, verified live); `ssh-host`/`journal`/`new-peer` carried the same wrong user; `commit`/`git-commit` stamped `Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.6` (repo standard is **Opus 4.8**); `audit-config` listed a phantom `server` host; `new-host`'s DE list named **five nonexistent modules** (`kde-mobile/labwc/phosh/river/sway`) and omitted five real ones; `pin-input`'s input roster was stale. Fixes: corrected the user fleet-wide, fixed the trailer, and made the DE list + input roster **enumerate live** instead of hardcoding.

- **`.claude/hosts.json` — single source of truth for host data (`f179f8b`).** Host SSH targets, IPs, and the WireGuard peer→host map were re-typed inline in 8 skills and had **drifted** (the `bosko`/`ubuntu` split). Centralized into `.claude/hosts.json`; rewired `fleet-status.sh` + `vpn-status.sh` (read via `jq`, both tested live) and the prose skills `ssh-host`/`journal`/`remote-rebuild`/`nix-repl`/`fleet-rollout`/`verify-service` to resolve from it. Also resolved the **phantom `nixos-server`** (not a flake host, doesn't resolve): dropped from `remote-rebuild`/`nix-repl`/`ssh-host`; **`pi-hole` kept** as a non-flake reference (user decision). `remote-rebuild` rewritten **vpn-server-only** with `boot`+reboot as the primary flow (matching its own Gotcha, which the old `switch` command contradicted).

- **Templates → `assets/`, deterministic work → `scripts/` (`a25c339`, `f2e2ec6`).** **`new-host` shrank 493→180 lines** (9 Nix templates moved to `assets/`). Extracted scripts: `diff-generations` (and fixed a latent bug — it compared against generation **1**, not current−1), `fmt`, `add-secret` (verify + host-age-key), `audit-config` (sweep), `rollback` (read-only listing; activation stays gated), and a **shared `.claude/lib/flake-lock-diff.sh`** now used by `update`/`bump-input`/`pin-input`. Project-local template extractions: `save-memory`, `new-module`, `new-peer`, `commit`. Added **AskUserQuestion** guidance for pick-one prompts/destructive gates and **`## Arguments`** prose sections across ~11 skills.

- **New meta-skill `/skill-audit` (`f56b9b1`).** Global Verification skill that reproduces this whole workflow: enumerate every skill, fan a read-only audit out over **disjoint** sub-agent partitions, score each against a 6-point rubric (single-bucket fit + the 5 lenses), synthesize one prioritized report (bugs first), and report-not-apply (offers to implement on "fix it"). Bakes in the lessons learned.

**Deliberately skipped:** global-skill template→`assets/` extraction (each asset needs a `home.file` symlink entry + rebuild — not worth it for small templates), and the `arguments:` YAML **frontmatter** field (unconfirmed SKILL.md support — used `## Arguments` prose instead). **PENDING:** the `dotfiles/`-based skill changes (`new-skill`, `git-commit`, `team-member-ingest`, new `team-member-synthesize` + `skill-audit`) only appear in `~/.claude/skills/` after `nh os boot /home/bosko/NixOS` **+ reboot**; all the project-local `.claude/skills/` changes are **live now**.

**Loop run-logs gitignored; FinanceGuru bumped (2026-06-22, session 17).** Two small changes. (1) FinanceGuru flake input bumped `9cb935a` → `6b506fe` for an upstream FinanceGuru update (`889d6bc`, pushed; lock-only, nothing else moved — applies on next `/fleet-rollout`). (2) Added a `.gitignore` rule for **loop per-run artifacts** (`90279df`, pushed): `.claude/loops/**/output-*.md` and `memory-*.md` are now ignored so each loop run's dual-file logs stay local instead of cluttering `git status`. The clarifying distinction: the **loop skills themselves live in `.claude/skills/`** and are already tracked/pushed (so they're portable to any clone) — the ignored files are only the dated run-logs, not the skills. **Loop config files under `.claude/loops/` stay tracked** (e.g. `public-repo-guard/baseline-allowlist.md`), so the glob deliberately targets only `output-*`/`memory-*`. Also note: before this conversation, the `/flake-update-verify` loop had been run (bumped dms/financeguru/home-manager/sops-nix, `b541303`) and gained a Step-7 push to the remote on the success path (`b9e85c2`) — both already pushed.

**First three real loops generated via `/create-loop` — `/create-loop` now confirmed working end-to-end (2026-06-21, session 16).** Used the session-15 meta-skill to create three **project-local** self-orchestrating loop skills (committed `bdc9434`, pushed), which simultaneously closes the session-15 PENDING item ("verify `/create-loop` resolves after a fresh session and run it once") — it resolved and ran three times, and the dual-file output dirs + Training Mode toggle behave as documented. The three loops:
  1. **`/fleet-rollout`** (`.claude/skills/fleet-rollout/`) — deploys the committed config across `gaming → laptop → natalie-laptop → vpn-server` one host at a time. Per host: dry-run gate → `switch` (activate live) → full `fleet-status`-style health sweep (new generation, zero failed units, no reboot-pending, fresh WG handshake on vpn-server), advancing only on green. Local host (`gaming`) deploys via `nh os switch`; the other three via the `remote-rebuild` skill. Fail-fast order puts the server last so a bad change is caught on a desktop first.
  2. **`/flake-update-verify`** (`.claude/skills/flake-update-verify/`) — `nix flake update` → `flake-check` → on clean eval **commits the `flake.lock` bump but never applies it** (applying is `/fleet-rollout`'s job); on broken eval **restores the previous lock** via `git checkout -- flake.lock` (safe because the loop requires a clean tree first) and names the offending input.
  3. **`/public-repo-guard`** (`.claude/skills/public-repo-guard/`) — `secret-scan` + `audit-config` over the whole repo, every finding triaged against a seeded **baseline allowlist** (`.claude/loops/public-repo-guard/baseline-allowlist.md`: sops-ciphertext-is-expected, fwupd ESP warning, AppArmor SDDM PAM override, audit-rules sentinel, repo-is-intentionally-public). Passes only at **zero genuine, non-allowlisted findings**; a pre-push / periodic guard for the public repo. The allowlist is the convergence mechanism — the loop proposes additions (approval required in Training Mode ON) so false positives shrink run over run.
  All three carry Loop Training Mode **ON**, retry cap 3, and dual-file run output under `.claude/loops/<name>/`. They resolve immediately (project-local, no rebuild). Natural chain: `/flake-update-verify` (bump+commit) → `/fleet-rollout` (apply) → `/public-repo-guard` (pre-push gate). Empty `loops/fleet-rollout/` and `loops/flake-update-verify/` dirs are untracked (git skips empty dirs); they populate on first run.

**`/create-loop` meta-skill added — generates self-orchestrating loop skills (2026-06-20, session 15).** New global/repo-owned skill `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/create-loop/SKILL.md`, wired into `bosko-claude.nix`'s `home.file` list (committed `b6e5d9d`, pushed). It interviews the user for a task's *goal*, *ordered steps* (each with its own done-rule), and *overall done-rule*, then writes a **project-local, standalone loop skill** to `<repo>/.claude/skills/<loop-name>/SKILL.md` — runnable immediately as `/<loop-name>` with no rebuild (only `/create-loop` itself, being repo-owned, needs a rebuild to go live). Every generated loop carries, baked in: (1) **Loop Training Mode** as a top-of-file toggle, **ON by default** — ON pauses at each step for approval, skips steps already passing their done-rule, re-runs only failures, respects a retry cap; OFF runs autonomously but keeps done-rule checks + the cap; (2) **retry cap = 3** attempts/step (configurable); (3) **dual-file output every run** → `.claude/loops/<loop>/output-<date>.md` (the artifact) + `memory-<date>.md` (what worked/failed/remember-next, read back at the start of the next run); (4) a **verification plan** section where the done-rule is the bar. `/create-loop` also has a built-in workspace-scan capability (propose loop-worthy repeatable tasks) and a built-in verification step (proves the generated skill is well-formed + runs its verification once). Key design decisions, all confirmed via `/interview`: generated loops are **project-local** (not repo-global — avoids a rebuild per loop), **standalone** invocation (self-orchestrating; can still be wrapped by built-in `/loop` for intervals), **per-loop log dir** for the two files (not the global memory system). Dry-run clean (+10.2 KiB, no kernel/service changes). ✅ **RESOLVED (session 16):** `/create-loop` is live in `~/.claude/skills/` and was used to generate three real loops — it resolves and runs as documented.

**`Parallelize with Sub-Agents` rule rewritten to actually trigger (2026-06-18, session 14).** User feedback: the rule rarely fired even when several files were being touched. Diagnosis — the old text described a *value* ("fan out aggressively") with no *trigger* condition, and it was fighting two counter-pressures: (a) the harness's built-in default is "don't spawn agents unless the user asks," which silently suppressed it, and (b) "multiple files" is the wrong signal because multi-file edits here are usually *coupled* (a module + its `flake.nix` import + `environment.nix`), which is correctly serial. Rewrote both the repo `CLAUDE.md` section and the global `claude-rules` skill's canonical text (`dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/claude-rules/SKILL.md`) to: (1) **grant standing authorization** to spawn agents ("you do not need to ask first") — directly overriding the harness default; (2) frame it as a **mandatory pre-task gate** ("required step, not an aspiration") with **observable triggers** (2+ independent research areas / 2+ unreferenced files / planning "A→B→C where B doesn't need A"); (3) name the **coupled exception** so the rule stops reading as self-contradictory. Committed `ea77515`, pushed. The `CLAUDE.md` change is live now; the skill edit only reaches `~/.claude` after a `rebuild` (it's a `/nix/store` symlink). The docs themselves are tracked Markdown, so no NixOS rebuild was needed for them.

**`nixpkgs-stable` bumped from EOL `nixos-25.05` to `nixos-25.11`; vpn-server deployed & live on 25.11 (2026-06-17, session 13).** `nixos-25.05` (Warbler) reached EOL — the live channels API shows current stable is **25.11 (Xantusia)** (the user's "26.05" doesn't exist yet). Bumped the `nixpkgs-stable` input `nixos-25.05` → `nixos-25.11` in `flake.nix` (vpn-server is its only consumer; `system.stateVersion` was already `25.11`), `nix flake update nixpkgs-stable`, eval-verified the vpn-server toplevel. Committed `d2a245d`. The deploy surfaced **three latent issues**, all now fixed or captured:
  1. **Tunnel MTU truncation (`fix(vpn)` `eb69c43`)** — wg-quick defaults `wg0` to MTU 1420, but the path to the Oracle endpoint only carries ~1400-byte inner packets; with PMTU discovery black-holed, full-size packets were silently dropped, corrupting large cache.nixos.org nar downloads (zstd "premature end") while pings/narinfo passed. Clamped `wg0` to **`mtu = 1380`** in the shared `modules/vpn.nix`. **PENDING:** applies to the three desktop clients on their next `rebuild`; the live `ip link set` change on laptop was lost when wg toggled, so laptop is back at 1420 (works, just not robust for large transfers) until rebuilt.
  2. **Cross-arch deploy needs `--build-host`** — the x86_64 laptop can't build vpn-server's aarch64 derivations (no emulation): uncached host-specific drvs fail with "platform mismatch". Deploy with `--build-host bosko@150.136.232.63` so the ARM box builds its own closure and pulls cached paths over its own link. Also `nixos-rebuild-ng` deprecates `--use-remote-sudo` → `--elevate=sudo`. Both folded into the project-local `remote-rebuild` skill (`80c5b9c`).
  3. **`switch` over SSH is killed mid-activation → corrupts firewall/NAT** — the first `switch` deploy hit `exit 255` while activation restarted networking. Root cause: `nixos-rebuild-ng` runs `switch-to-configuration` as `systemd-run --pipe` tied to the SSH connection; the network restart drops SSH → broken pipe → activation torn down half-applied (`MASQUERADE` present but FORWARD/conntrack at zero; clients connect but get no routing). This — **not a 25.11 regression** — is what broke the VPN fleet-wide. Proved it by static-diffing gen 17 (25.05) vs gen 18 (25.11): `firewall-start`, `postUp.sh` (NAT), and `wg0.conf` are byte-identical except store-path bumps; same iptables 1.8.11, same nft backend. **Recovery:** rolled the server back to gen 17 via a *detached* `systemd-run --collect` (survives the network restart) to restore service, then redeployed 25.11 cleanly via **`nixos-rebuild boot` + reboot** (boot sets the bootloader default with no runtime activation, so SSH never drops; the reboot does a clean boot-time activation). Post-reboot verified on 25.11: no failed units, `ip_forward=1`, wg0 up, MASQUERADE in nft, nixos-fw populated, user confirmed VPN works. Both failure modes are now documented in the `remote-rebuild` skill's Gotchas (`8b3429e`). All four commits pushed to `origin/main`.

**nixos MCP server promoted from project scope to user scope, made reproducible (2026-06-17, session 12).** The `mcp-nixos` server that session 11 wired up as project-scoped (`.mcp.json` in this repo) was promoted to **user scope** so Claude Code can query live NixOS package/option/version data in *every* project, not just this one. `claude mcp add nixos mcp-nixos --scope user` wrote the entry to `~/.claude.json`, then the redundant `.mcp.json` was `git rm`'d so the server lives in exactly one place. To keep it reproducible — `~/.claude.json` is mutable state Claude rewrites constantly and can't be symlinked read-only — a new `home.activation.claudeMcpServers` block in `dotfiles/bosko/bosko-claude.nix` idempotently reconciles `.mcpServers.nixos` with `jq` on every rebuild (rewrites only when missing/different, leaves the rest of the file untouched), mirroring the existing `claudeAllowList`/`claudeNixdPlugin` pattern. The `mcp-nixos` package comment in `users.nix` was repointed from `.mcp.json` to the new user-scope location. The reboot at the start of this session also confirmed session 11's work is live: the MCP server is connected (verified with a live `stats` call — 134,973 packages) and the wg-quick `::/0` fix activated cleanly. Dry-run clean (1.80 KiB diff, no kernel/service changes). Committed `f9a66e8`, pushed. **PENDING:** takes durable effect on next `rebuild` — it's already global *now* via the `claude mcp add` write; the activation block just guarantees it survives a `~/.claude.json` reset / fresh machine. Note: the `enabledMcpjsonServers` entry session 11 added to `.claude/settings.local.json` is now a harmless no-op (it only gated the deleted project-scoped `.mcp.json`).

**WireGuard `::/0` allowedIPs dropped — fixes wg-quick startup failure introduced by the IPv6 disable (2026-06-17, session 11).** ✅ Activated & confirmed live after the session-12 reboot (`wg-quick-wg0` no longer tears down). A `rebuild` on gaming surfaced `wg-quick-wg0.service` failing with exit 4 during activation. Root cause: session 10 disabled IPv6 system-wide (`networking.enableIPv6 = false`) but **left `::/0` in the peer's `allowedIPs`**. `wg-quick` honours `allowedIPs` by installing routes, so it ran `ip -6 route add ::/0 dev wg0`, which fails with *"IPv6 is disabled on nexthop device"* — and on any error wg-quick rolls back and `ip link delete`s the interface. So the tunnel had actually been failing to come up on **every boot since session 10** (the journal showed the same teardown at 08:15 and 15:18), not merely warning. This corrects the session-10 decision that deliberately kept `::/0` "so the tunnel stays correct if IPv6 is ever added" — that reasoning was wrong: with no IPv6 stack there is nothing to leak, so a v4-only `allowedIPs = [ "0.0.0.0/0" ]` still gives a full tunnel and lets the service start. Fixed in the shared `modules/vpn.nix` (applies to all three clients); a `NOTE:` comment now warns against re-adding `::/0` while IPv6 is off. Dry-run clean (128-byte diff, only the `wg-quick-wg0` unit/config rebuilds, no kernel change). Committed `2f67083`, pushed. **PENDING:** gaming needs `rebuild` + **reboot** (user is doing this next) — confirm `wg-quick-wg0` is active and the handshake is live afterward. laptop/natalie-laptop pick it up on their next rebuild.

**Project-scoped mcp-nixos MCP server added (2026-06-17, session 11).** `pkgs.mcp-nixos` added to bosko's user packages and a project-scoped `.mcp.json` added so Claude Code in this repo can query live NixOS package/option/version data via the `nixos` MCP server. Uses the Nix-packaged binary on PATH (not uvx/PyPI) for reproducibility/offline use. The server is enabled in `.claude/settings.local.json` (`enabledMcpjsonServers`). Committed `720fe36`. (Plus a routine `nix flake update`, `56983dd`.)

**IPv6 disabled on VPN clients to fix Jellyfin metadata/image fetches (2026-06-17, session 10).** Investigating "Jellyfin images won't load" on gaming: the journal showed `Provider TheMovieDb failed to retrieve search results` with `TaskCanceledException … HttpClient.Timeout of 100 seconds`, all on `TMDbClient.GetConfigAsync()` (the call that fetches TMDb's image base URL). Diagnosis: TMDb resolves to **IPv6-only** addresses, but the full-tunnel WireGuard client routes `::/0` into `wg0` while the tunnel is **IPv4-only** (no IPv6 address; Oracle Cloud server doesn't route IPv6) — so every IPv6 connection black-holed. Confirmed with `curl`: IPv4 → TMDb returns 401/404 in <0.1s; IPv6 → instant `000` failure. Fix: added `networking.enableIPv6 = false;` to the shared `modules/vpn.nix`, forcing DNS/connections to fall back to IPv4 through the tunnel — fixes the black-hole while keeping the full-tunnel guarantee (no IPv6 leak around the VPN). Applies to **all three VPN clients** (gaming, laptop, natalie-laptop). Dry-run clean (+88 bytes, restarts firewall/systemd-sysctl, no kernel change). Committed `81cf1a2`. **PENDING:** gaming needs `rebuild` + **reboot** to tear down the v6 stack, then re-run the Jellyfin metadata refresh to confirm artwork loads. laptop/natalie-laptop pick it up on their next rebuild.

**Sudo password masking enabled fleet-wide (2026-06-16, session 9).** Added `security.sudo.extraConfig = "Defaults pwfeedback";` to `modules/security.nix` so the sudo prompt prints a `*` per typed character — the entered length is now visible. Because `security.nix` is in `commonModules`, this applies to all hosts. A comment notes CVE-2019-18634 (the historical `pwfeedback` stack overflow) is fixed in sudo ≥ 1.8.31, so current sudo is unaffected. Dry-run clean (regenerated `sudoers`, +10.4 KiB, no kernel/service changes). The user ran `switch` on gaming and **confirmed the `*` masking works**; committed and pushed (`eeb03bc`).

**Jellyfin media server live on gaming — confirmed working on all devices (2026-06-16, session 8).** New `hosts/gaming/jellyfin-server.nix` runs native `services.jellyfin` backed by the spare 1TB Samsung SSD (reformatted ext4, mounted at `/mnt/media`, pinned by UUID), with NVENC hardware transcoding via the RTX 3070 (jellyfin user in `render`/`video`). Firewall is scoped to LAN (`enp4s0`) + WireGuard (`wg0`) only — **no internet-facing ports**. A shared `media` group with setgid Movies/Shows dirs keeps manual drops readable by the scanner. `jellyfin-media-player` was added to `desktopModules` (`modules/jellyfin-client.nix`) so every desktop host ships the client. The user **confirmed playback on all client devices on 2026-06-16** — this is COMPLETE, not a pending item. In the same session, the headless `qbittorrent-nox` service (localhost Web UI, finding H-6) was replaced with the `qbittorrent` desktop GUI app to drop the listening Web UI, saving to `/mnt/media/downloads` (setgid `bosko:media`) so finished torrents stay readable by Jellyfin. A new project-local **`verify-service`** skill (read-only post-rebuild service health sweep) was distilled from the by-hand verification done this session. Commits `1a207f3..ace485c` (4), all pushed.

**`claude-rules` skill gained a fourth standing rule (2026-06-15, session 7).** Added **Use Existing Skills First** to the global `claude-rules` skill source (`dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/claude-rules/SKILL.md`) and to this repo's `CLAUDE.md`: reach for an existing skill before doing a task by hand. The skill's count/ordering and Step-2 heading-match list were updated so the new section is detected and inserted last. It is distinct from the existing "Skill Awareness" section (which is about *authoring* a skill when a pattern repeats) — this one is about *using* one that already exists. Committed (`411acfc`). The skill's `SKILL.md` only reaches `~/.claude` after a rebuild (it's a `/nix/store` symlink); the `CLAUDE.md` change is live now.

**ssh.nix famdash login corrected (2026-06-15, session 6).** `dotfiles/common/configs/ssh.nix` `famdash` host: `User = "natalie"` → `User = "natty"` (the valid account). Committed `5a5b58e`.

The configuration manages five NixOS hosts from a single flake. **The repository is now public** (`github.com/CommanderBosko/NixOS`). The WireGuard VPN is fully deployed and operational: Oracle Cloud ARM vpn-server is live, all three client hosts (gaming, laptop, natalie-laptop) have the shared `vpn.nix` module imported, and each host's private key is now supplied by sops-nix (encrypted in-repo). All three client interfaces are active.

**fwupd ESP override removed as a no-op (2026-06-15, session 5).** Investigated the fwupd ESP warning on gaming after rebuild+reboot. The prior session's switch to `uefiCapsuleSettings.OverrideESPMountPoint` did NOT fix detection: that key is dead in fwupd 2.1.4 (it survives only in bash-completion stubs, not in `libfwupdengine`/`libfwupdplugin`/`libfwupdutil`), and `EspLocation` no longer overrides 2.x's partition-type-based ESP enumeration either. gaming's ESP (`nvme0n1p1`, **MBR/`dos` table**, type code `0x0c` W95 FAT32 LBA — not `0xEF` EFI System) therefore stays undetected regardless of config; `fwupdtool esp-list` still reports "No ESP or BDP found". **Decided not to fix it**: ASRock X570 Phantom Gaming 4 ships no firmware via LVFS (`get-updates` → "No updatable devices"), Secure Boot is **disabled** (so UEFI dbx revocation updates are moot), and the only updatable device fwupd sees (Samsung 870 EVO SSD, fw `SVT02B6Q`) updates over the ATA plugin, not the capsule path. The cosmetic warning has zero practical impact here. Dropped the entire `uefiCapsuleSettings` block from `firmware.nix` and documented why it's intentionally unset (revisit only on a move to GPT + Secure Boot or an LVFS-supported board). Dry-run clean (−88 bytes). **Staged for next boot on gaming via `nh os boot`; not yet activated.**

**Post-sops: new skills + fwupd ESP fix attempt (2026-06-15, session 4 continued).** Added three NixOS project-local skills — `add-secret` (manage sops secrets), `fleet-status` (read-only multi-host health sweep), `secret-scan` (scan tree + history for leaked secrets) — and upgraded `new-host` to register a new host as a sops recipient (without it, a new host can't decrypt the shared password secrets). Reworked the global `session-closer` skill to bias toward curating `project-state.md`/README over exhaustive logging, with rotation (active `session-summary.md` keeps ~5 entries; older ones in `session-summary-archive.md`). (The fwupd `OverrideESPMountPoint` change made this session was later found ineffective and removed in session 5 — see above.)

**Repository made PUBLIC; secrets migrated to sops-nix (2026-06-15, session 4).** All in-repo secrets were migrated to [sops-nix](https://github.com/Mic92/sops-nix) so the repo could be published safely. The `bosko`/`natty` login password hashes (`secrets/common.yaml`) and all four per-host WireGuard private keys (`secrets/hosts/<host>.yaml`) are now committed **encrypted**. `users.nix` uses `hashedPasswordFile`; `vpn.nix` and `hosts/vpn-server/configuration.nix` use `privateKeyFile = config.sops.secrets."wg-private-key".path`. Each host decrypts with an age key derived from its SSH ed25519 host key (`sops.age.sshKeyPaths`); the **admin age key lives at `~/.config/sops/age/keys.txt`** (out of repo — must be backed up). New module `modules/sops.nix` is in `commonModules`; `.sops.yaml` holds the recipient map; `sops-nix` added as a flake input. All four hosts were rebuilt and verified — live WireGuard handshakes on every peer, and every WG key confirmed identity-preserving (derived pubkeys match the server's registered peers). Git history was rewritten with `git filter-repo` to purge the old plaintext password hashes (183 occurrences → 0); all clones realigned to the rewritten `main`. The endpoint IP `150.136.232.63` is intentionally left in-repo (public endpoint, not a secret). A pre-rewrite backup bundle (contains the old hashes) is at `~/nixos-pre-sops-rewrite.bundle` — local only, never published.

**Declarative settings trim added to bosko-claude.nix (2026-06-09, session 3).** `home.activation.trimClaudeSettings` added to `dotfiles/bosko/bosko-claude.nix`. On every rebuild, once `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json` exists, the activation script uses `jq` to strip `permissions.deny`, `permissions.ask`, and `hooks` from `~/.claude/settings.json` while leaving `permissions.allow`, `model`, and `enabledPlugins` intact. Idempotent; no-op until the managed file is present. Replaces the one-shot `~/.claude/trim-after-managed.sh` script deleted earlier this session. Scoped to bosko only. Module signature updated to `{ self, pkgs, lib, ... }`.

**Claude Code managed policy deployed (2026-06-09).** `modules/claude-code.nix` deploys `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json` on every host via `environment.etc`. The managed file enforces deny rules for destructive commands, ask rules for sensitive operations, and a PreToolUse fork bomb guard (jq pinned to its Nix store path). Wired into `commonModules` so all hosts share the enforced, tamper-resistant policy. On gaming (where the rebuild+reboot was done today), the managed file is active and the personal `~/.claude/settings.json` has been trimmed to personal preferences only. **laptop and natalie-laptop still need rebuild+reboot to activate the policy.**

**gemini-cli added to natty's user packages (2026-06-09).** `pkgs.gemini-cli` added to `users.users.natty.packages` in `users.nix`, matching bosko.

**Package consolidation (2026-06-09).** `nodejs`, `pnpm`, `p7zip`, and `jq` moved from per-host `environment.nix` files (gaming, laptop, natalie-laptop) into `modules/shell.nix` `systemPackages`. These tools now apply consistently across all hosts.

**natalie-laptop switched to Plasma 6 (2026-06-04/05).** Changed DE import from `cosmic.nix` to `plasma.nix` in `flake.nix`. `krohnkite` (KWin tiling script) added to the shared `plasma.nix` module so both gaming and natalie-laptop receive it. natalie-laptop rebuild+reboot needed to activate.

**FinanceGuru integrated (2026-06-04/05).** `github:CommanderBosko/FinanceGuru` added as a flake input and installed on gaming and natalie-laptop. Not on laptop.

**lutris re-enabled on gaming (2026-06-03).** The upstream `openldap-2.6.13-i686-linux` binary cache regression is resolved in nixpkgs-unstable; `gaming.nix` un-commented `lutris`.

**Security hardening pass completed 2026-06-03.** A multi-agent security audit found three real findings. Two were remediated immediately:

1. **Root SSH on vpn-server disabled** (`hosts/vpn-server/configuration.nix`) — `PermitRootLogin` set to `"no"`. `security.sudo.wheelNeedsPassword = false` added so `nixos-rebuild --use-remote-sudo` works non-interactively as `bosko`. Deployed and verified: root SSH is blocked; passwordless sudo for bosko confirmed working.
2. **SDDM auto-login on gaming disabled** (`hosts/gaming/environment.nix`) — `autoLogin.enable = false`. Activated on gaming and confirmed working — DONE, not pending.
3. **Avahi mDNS `openFirewall = true` in printing.nix** — deferred; closing it breaks printer discovery. Proper fix (interface-scoped firewall rules) is tracked in Known Issues.

The `remote-rebuild` skill has been updated to deploy as `bosko@150.136.232.63` instead of `root@150.136.232.63`.

**Local LLM stack deployed on gaming (2026-05-26).** A new `hosts/gaming/hermes-agent.nix` module provisions the entire local AI stack: `services.ollama` using `pkgs.ollama-cuda` (CUDA-accelerated, RTX 3070) with `loadModels = [ "mistral-nemo:12b" ]`, and `services.hermes-agent` configured to use the local Ollama OpenAI-compatible API at `http://localhost:11434/v1`. The `hermes` CLI is on the system PATH via `addToSystemPackages = true`; bosko is in the `hermes` group so the CLI can traverse `/var/lib/hermes` (mode 2770). `mistral-nemo:12b` was selected after iterating through four models: it is the only evaluated model satisfying all three constraints simultaneously — 128K context (above Hermes Agent's 64K minimum), excellent tool-calling support, and ~7GB Q4 quantisation that fits in the RTX 3070's 8GB VRAM. The `hermes-agent` flake input (`github:NousResearch/hermes-agent`) is wired into the gaming host in `flake.nix`.

**SSH config API corrected (2026-05-26).** The `dotfiles/common/configs/ssh.nix` module was migrated from the deprecated `programs.ssh.matchBlocks` API to `programs.ssh.settings`, option keys capitalised to match `ssh_config(5)`, a global `ServerAliveInterval`/`ServerAliveCountMax` stanza added, and `enableDefaultConfig = false` set to suppress implicit-defaults warnings.

**`~/.ssh/config` migrated into Home Manager declaratively (2026-05-25).** A new `dotfiles/common/configs/ssh.nix` module uses `programs.ssh.settings` to define all five SSH hosts: `natalie-laptop` (10.0.0.101, user bosko; was .103 until the router's DHCP reassigned it ~2026-07-03), `laptop` (10.0.0.227, user bosko), `gaming` (10.0.0.251, user bosko), `pi-hole` (10.0.0.20, user bosko), and `famdash` (10.0.0.21, user natalie). Imported from `home.nix` so both users receive the managed SSH config. Dry-run confirmed clean build.

**`vpn-on` / `vpn-off` shell aliases confirmed working (2026-05-22).** `vpn-on = "sudo systemctl start wg-quick-wg0"` and `vpn-off = "sudo systemctl stop wg-quick-wg0"` are now in `modules/shell.nix`. The systemd unit name uses a hyphen (`wg-quick-wg0`) which is how systemd renders the `wg-quick@wg0` template. Using `systemctl` avoids `wg-quick` binary PATH issues for non-root shell sessions.

**Non-login shell Home Manager session variables now sourced (2026-05-22).** `zsh.shellInit` in `shell.nix` now sources `~/.nix-profile/etc/profile.d/hm-session-vars.sh` when the file exists. This makes `sessionPath` (including `~/.local/bin`) and `sessionVariables` available to non-login zsh sessions (terminal emulators, scripted tool calls) — previously only login shells received them.

**`nixfmt-classic` replaced with `nixfmt` (2026-05-22).** The deprecated `nixfmt-classic` package was updated to `nixfmt` in both `shell.nix` (system package) and `helix.nix` (formatter reference). `flake.lock` updated accordingly.

**vpn-server: `audit=0` kernel param added (2026-05-22).** `boot.kernelParams = lib.mkAfter [ "audit=0" ]` in `hosts/vpn-server/configuration.nix` suppresses the Oracle Cloud ARM kernel's broken audit subsystem, which was flooding `kauditd` and dropping SSH connections mid-rebuild. The `mkAfter` placement ensures it wins over `security.nix`'s `audit=1` at boot without touching the shared module.

**nixpkgs channel split is complete (2026-05-21).** Desktop hosts (gaming, laptop, natalie-laptop) follow `nixos-unstable`. Server hosts (vpn-server, server) are pinned to `nixos-25.05` via a `nixpkgs-stable` input in `flake.nix`. vpn-server was deployed and confirmed running `25.05.20260102.ac62194 (Warbler)`. The `server` host placeholder was removed from `flake.nix` and `hosts/server/` (2026-05-21) — no hardware exists yet; the entry will be recreated via the `/new-host` skill when physical hardware arrives.

**Printing module added to desktopModules (2026-05-21, late session).** `modules/printing.nix` provides CUPS + Avahi mDNS printing with drivers (gutenprint, hplip, brlaser) for all desktop hosts. The module is now in `desktopModules` in `flake.nix`. Per-host printing entries previously duplicated in each host's `environment.nix` were removed. `kdePackages.print-manager` (the KDE print-job GUI) remains in `plasma.nix` — it is Plasma-specific and does not belong in the DE-agnostic printing module.

**dbus-broker transition is COMPLETE on all 5 hosts (2026-05-21).** `services.dbus.implementation = "broker"` is set as a plain (non-default) assignment in `security.nix`. This beats `nix-flatpak`'s bundled older nixpkgs whose `mkDefault "dbus"` was silently winning during module merge. All five hosts have been rebooted and verified running `dbus-broker-launch`.

**Claude Code skill library is at 22 skills (2026-05-21).** Lives under `.claude/skills/`. The `/nixos-rebuild` skill was removed after investigation confirmed `nh os boot` requires a real TTY for sudo and cannot be driven from a Claude Code subprocess. The apply step is always done by the user running `rebuild` in their terminal. Skills cover: dry-run, GC, VPN status, module scaffolding, commit, push, flake update, package/flatpak addition, desktop environment switching, new peer, SSH to any host, remote rebuild (headless targets), generation rollback, package search, new host scaffolding, flake input pinning, diff-generations, flake-check, journal, nix-repl, and fmt.

**`~/.local/bin` added to bosko's sessionPath (2026-05-21).** `home.sessionPath = [ "$HOME/.local/bin" ]` added to `dotfiles/bosko/bosko-claude.nix`. This ensures the native Claude Code binary installed at `~/.local/bin/claude` is discoverable in PATH, fixing the `/doctor` native binary check.

**Starship config inlined (2026-05-17).** The external `starship.toml` dotfile has been eliminated. The full Gruvbox-themed Starship prompt configuration now lives as a native `programs.starship.settings` attrset in `shell.nix`. `dotfiles/common/configs/starship.toml` and the `xdg.configFile` symlink in `home.nix` were both removed.

**Helix auto-format disabled globally (2026-05-17).** All language blocks in `helix.nix` now have `auto-format = false` set uniformly.

**Stale NordVPN configs deleted (2026-05-17).** The `dotfiles/vpn/` directory (two `.conf` files and an `auto-auth.txt`) was removed. These were dead code from before the WireGuard approach.

**Nerd Font v3 symbol spacing fixed (2026-05-17).** Nine starship module symbols in `shell.nix` had a trailing space added to render correctly in Nerd Font v3.

**tmux added to gaming (2026-05-17).** `tmux` is now a system package on the gaming host.

**Repository restructured (2026-05-15).** Host-specific files moved from `dotfiles/<hostname>/` into a new top-level `hosts/<hostname>/` directory. Bosko-specific Home Manager configs (`bosko-claude.nix` and `claude/agents/`) moved from `dotfiles/common/configs/` into `dotfiles/bosko/`. All `flake.nix` paths updated; dry-run passed cleanly after both moves.

**`rebuild` alias now uses `nh os boot`.** As of 2026-05-12, the `rebuild` shell alias stages changes for the next reboot rather than attempting a live switch.

**AMD card swap preparation in progress.** `nvidia.nix` was removed from `desktopModules` on 2026-05-14 and is now listed explicitly per-host. When the physical NVIDIA card is replaced with AMD on gaming, removing `nvidia.nix` from gaming's module list in `flake.nix` is the only change needed.

**Home Manager reorganised.** `home-manager.nix` was refactored into a single nested `home-manager { }` block; `stateVersion` was moved here from `home.nix`; natty's block now correctly imports `home.nix` (previously missing).

**`claude-code` and `gemini-cli` moved to user-level packages.** Both tools are now declared in `users.users.bosko.packages` in `users.nix` rather than repeated per-host in `environment.nix`.

**Security module is active.** `modules/security.nix` is in `commonModules` and applied on all hosts. Includes AppArmor MAC enforcement, auditd (running; rules-loader disabled), kernel image protection, ASLR, PAM wheel enforcement, SDDM PAM override workaround, and an explicit `services.dbus.implementation = "broker"` assignment (dbus-broker, COMPLETE on all hosts).

**Security audit completed 2026-05-06.** All Critical and High findings remediated. Most Medium and Low findings remediated. Three items explicitly deferred (see Known Issues).
