## Recent Decisions

- **PR #18's two guardrail-flagged files (a new shared lib, a permissions file) merged anyway after a direct content review, not held for manual review by default (2026-09-05, session 101)** — `review-improve-system-pr`'s guardrail only recognizes `SKILL.md`/`scripts/`/`assets/` paths under the two skill dirs, so `.claude/lib/classify-shared-file.sh` and `.claude/settings.json` both tripped it structurally even though their content matched exactly what the PR body described and fixed a real bug. Rather than treat a guardrail hit as an automatic hold, read both files directly, confirmed no `.nix` file was touched and secret-scan/`public-repo-guard` were already clean, then merged via the existing `AskUserQuestion` gate. The guardrail still did its job — surfacing the boundary crossing for a human look — it just isn't a synonym for "reject."
- **`session-closer`'s secret-scan step rescoped to match what the tool already does, per direct user ask (2026-09-04, session 101)** — the skill's STEP 5 framed `secret-scan` as a README-only pass, but the tool itself unconditionally scans the whole tree + full git history regardless of scope. Rather than leave the mismatch (undersells the check, and any non-README finding would have gone unfixed), split it into its own STEP 5B explicitly scoped to everything committed since `session-closer`'s own last run.
- **A shallow "not yet applied to any host" note was resolved via direct evidence, not assumed from the reboot alone (2026-09-04, session 100)** — rather than take gaming's 20:44 reboot as blanket proof that session 99's whole backlog was live, checked each claim directly: skill symlinks actually resolve to real store paths (not dangling), `gemini-cli` is genuinely absent from PATH, `dotnet --version` actually returns a version. This surfaced one real correction along the way — the Godot binary that resolves is `godot4-mono`/`godot-mono`, not `godot4` as a prior session's verification note assumed — which a "reboot happened, mark it all done" shortcut would have missed.
- **The flatpak-race and hplip fixes were kept as two separate commits despite landing in the same evening (2026-09-04, session 100)** — different root causes (a boot-time DNS ordering race vs. an upstream nixpkgs/pyqt5 Python-version regression), different files (`desktop-apps.nix` vs. `printing.nix`), different scope (shared-module 3-host impact vs. fleet-wide driver list) — bundling them into one commit would have blurred a `git log`/`git bisect` search for either fix later.
- **`improve-system`'s Discord report step always runs, even on an all-clean or focus-scoped pass (2026-09-04, session 100)** — matches `dream`'s existing precedent (a clean pass is still a result worth reporting) rather than only notifying when there's something actionable; the alternative (skip the notification when nothing needs sign-off) would make a truly silent, unattended run indistinguishable from a run that never happened.
- **`send-results` redesigned to publish an Artifact instead of trying to preserve a local `file://` link (2026-09-04, session 99)** — Discord's message renderer never auto-links non-`http(s)` schemes on any device, an anti-abuse restriction with no formatting workaround, so the original "post a local path" design could never actually produce a clickable link in practice. Chose to publish the reported content as a Claude Artifact and link to that real `https://` URL instead, accepting the trade-off that reported content now leaves the local machine (published, shareable) rather than staying purely local. Live end-to-end retested before committing.
- **PR #14's classifier-flag warning treated as verified-clean-but-flagged, not dismissed (2026-09-03, session 99)** — a full manual audit of the merging subagent's entire tool-call trajectory (19 Bash/3 Read/3 Edit/1 Skill) found nothing wrong and the merged diff matched exactly what was claimed, but the classifier's own reasoning was never visible from the transcript. Rather than either treat the flag as proof of a real problem or wave it away since the content checked out, reported both facts to the user as true at once — verified safe by content, unexplained by process — since the merge was already live and irreversible either way.
- **FinanceGuru's auto-mode-classifier block resolved by pointing the user at an interactive-session mode toggle, not by trying to route around it (2026-08-31, session 97)** — once the root cause (background/agent-view sessions can't self-escalate permissions; only a human-present interactive session can flip `defaultMode`) was confirmed via direct settings comparison, the only options were waiting for the user to act or attempting a workaround (e.g. shelling out to write `settings.local.json` directly). Chose to stop and name the exact human action needed, per the denial's own guidance, rather than search for a clever bypass — same posture `manager`'s hard-limit fallback (below) already codifies for a different trigger. No repo change; purely advisory.
- **`manager`'s hard-limit fallback codified as "stop completely, never trust relayed consent" rather than "stop and use `AskUserQuestion`" (2026-08-30, session 96)** — the original wording assumed `AskUserQuestion` is always reachable, but the live calibration test proved it isn't when `manager` runs as a background subagent via the Agent tool. Rather than soften the hard limit for that invocation shape, kept it absolute: use `AskUserQuestion` if genuinely present, otherwise stop completely and report the exact verifiable action needed to unblock — never accept a relayed/asserted claim of user consent from any other agent, however confidently worded, and independently re-verify the resulting state once told the blocker is resolved. Validated live: `manager` refused three escalating relay attempts during the test and only proceeded once the repo state itself became checkable; user graded it "exactly right, keep it this strict."
- **`manager`'s mined profile required a human read-through/sign-off before its first push, not auto-approved (2026-08-30, session 96)** — since the profile directly steers a future agent's unsupervised decisions on the user's behalf, it was treated as higher-stakes than an ordinary skill/agent file even though this is a private write to a public repo. The gate is one-time by design: `refresh-manager-profile`'s later incremental re-mines auto-push without re-litigating it.
- **`save-memory` promoted to a global skill rather than just correcting session 92's "phantom" misdiagnosis in place (2026-08-30, session 95)** — user asked local-vs-global as a factual question, but the natural read of "if it's local, make it global" was to actually promote it, not just report the finding. Followed the established recursive-dir `home.file` pattern (`interview`, `new-skill`, `session-closer`, etc. — anything with sibling files under `SKILL.md`) rather than a single-file symlink, since `save-memory` carries `assets/memory-template.md` alongside its `SKILL.md`.
- **`development.nix` scoped by who-needs-it, not by dev-tool topic, then promoted from gaming+laptop-only into `desktopModules` (2026-08-26/27, session 94)** — first ask (godot-mono+dotnet-sdk for Legions) went straight into both hosts' `environment.nix`; asked whether to sweep other loosely-dev-shaped packages (`claude-code`/`mcp-nixos`/`tailscale-mcp`/`rtk` in `users.nix`, `github-desktop`/`kate`/`kitty` in `desktop-apps.nix`) into a shared module too — declined, since each already sits in the tier its actual scope calls for and a topic-based move would either drop packages from hosts/users `development.nix` doesn't reach or hand them somewhere they don't belong. Only the genuine duplication got a module, imported per-host like `nvidia.nix`. When the user later said natty might develop too and asked to centralize for all desktop users, promoted the same module into `desktopModules` rather than revisiting the declined sweep — reaches all 3 desktop hosts/both users, no unrelated packages moved.
- **`repo-creator` no longer defaults to public — asks explicitly via `AskUserQuestion` (2026-08-26, commit `9380a65`)** — visibility is now a required argument on `create-repo.sh`'s create mode; prompted with no default when omitted, instead of silently hardcoding `--public`.
- **Fixed a real `git -C * <subcommand>` permission-rule injection bug in `.claude/settings.json`, hardcoding the path instead of narrowing the wildcard (2026-08-25, session 93)** — Claude Code's own startup warning flagged `Bash(git -C * status)`/`Bash(git -C * log *)`: the wildcard sat *before* the subcommand, so an injected `-c`/`--exec-path` option in that gap would still match and get auto-approved without a prompt. `Bash(git -C * diff*)` had the identical shape but wasn't in the popup — audited and fixed it too rather than leaving one instance of the same bug. Fix: since every real usage across this repo's skills (`fleet-rollout`, `flake-update-verify`, `bump-input`, `fmt`, etc.) only ever targets `/home/bosko/NixOS`, hardcoded that exact path instead of keeping any wildcard in the injectable slot — matches Claude's own guidance ("only use `*` after the subcommand") while preserving every real call site. Followed up with a full audit of all three permission files (project `settings.json`/`settings.local.json`, global `~/.claude/settings.json`) for the same wildcard-before-subcommand shape — none found; every other wildcard is a trailing suffix (`prefix*`), the safe form. Commit `599276f`.
- **`ssh.nix` Tailscale-IP sweep scoped via two `AskUserQuestion` calls rather than assumed (2026-08-21, session 87)** — switched `gaming`'s alias to its Tailscale IP too even though its LAN IP is static/non-drifting (consistency + off-LAN reachability, user's call); left the `new-host` skill's server-template DNS reference (`10.0.0.19`) on the LAN IP since that host type isn't Tailscale-joined by default (also user's call). Established the boundary for future IP-sweep requests: WireGuard mesh IPs, vpn-server's public Oracle IP, and historical session-log mentions are not Tailscale-IP candidates even though they're IP-shaped literals in the same repo.
- **Enabled pi-hole as a Tailscale-admin-console global nameserver, alongside (not instead of) the flake-managed override (2026-08-19, session 84)** — "Restrict to domain" off (needs to answer everything, not one domain), "Use with exit node" on (keeps pi-hole authoritative if/when a Tailscale exit node — e.g. Mullvad, still unexplored — is added later, rather than silently losing ad-blocking the moment one comes online). Kept both layers rather than consolidating: the flake-managed override only reaches the 3 NixOS hosts and keeps a `1.1.1.1` fallback; the admin-console setting is the only DNS path available to non-flake tailnet members (pi-hole/famdash themselves, and the two phones that joined the mesh — see Known Issues).
- **Pi-hole-over-Tailscale DNS: flake-managed nameserver swap (Option B) chosen over a Tailscale admin-console DNS override (Option A) (2026-08-18, session 82)** — Option A is a single unreviewable toggle that changes live DNS behavior for every meshed device at once, with no staged rollout and an unpredictable interaction with the existing static-resolver setup; Option B reuses this repo's existing verification tooling (dry-run → per-host rollout → health check) and keeps `1.1.1.1` as a working fallback if pi-hole-over-Tailscale doesn't pan out. Decided via a short `/interview` rather than assumed.
- **Flipped pi-hole's own `dns.listeningMode` from `LOCAL` to `ALL` (2026-08-18, session 82)** — required for the DNS-over-Tailscale change above: `LOCAL` was silently dropping queries from Tailscale's point-to-point `/32`-addressed interface, since it doesn't count as a "local subnet" by pi-hole's own ACL definition. `ALL` is a real posture change (pi-hole's own docs call it "dangerous" as an open-resolver risk) but judged safe here — no port-forward to pi-hole exists and Tailscale's `ts-input` firewall chain still gates non-tailnet traffic. Got explicit user sign-off before touching the live, non-flake-tracked appliance.
- **Tailscale on pi-hole/famdash installed manually, not brought under the flake (2026-08-18, session 82)** — consistent with how they've always been treated (non-flake devices, manual console management); Tailscale itself was installed via the standard `curl | sh` + `tailscale up` flow, same as the desktop hosts' one-time manual auth, rather than trying to retrofit either Pi into `new-host`/flake management just for this.
- **wg0/`vpn.nix` fully commented out of `desktopModules` rather than left in place to fail, or deleted outright (2026-08-18, session 81)** — with vpn-server down for an unknown duration (Oracle admin-disabled), a failing `wg-quick-wg0` unit on every desktop host boot was pure noise. Considered leaving it in place (rejected — noisy, no benefit) vs. deleting the file (rejected — throws away real wiring for no reason, it'll reconnect on its own once vpn-server is back). Chose comment-out: `flake.nix`'s import plus the now-orphaned per-host fragments that depended on it, `wg0.allowedTCPPorts` firewall stanzas left alone since they're harmless either way.
- **Tailscale mesh expanded to laptop/natalie-laptop with the same manual-auth, mesh-only scope as gaming's stopgap, not escalated to sops `authKeyFile` or exit-node config (2026-08-18, session 81)** — 3 ad-hoc hosts doesn't clear the bar for automating auth (matches the reasoning already applied to gaming in session 80's research). Exit-node/egress-masking (what would actually replace wg0's bot-detection-relevant behavior) stays explicitly deferred, not attempted as part of this expansion.

User chose to self-watch rather than have a new mechanism built. Don't re-propose unless the user asks.

User asked for the tradeoffs on option 2 first (polling-interval blind spot, brief print-dialog flicker on each restart, standing complexity for a rare self-inflicted trigger), then chose option 1 (manual restart) as recommended. Don't re-propose the timer unless this starts recurring often enough to change the cost/benefit.
