- **VPN monitoring split into a local watchdog + a cloud staleness check, not one combined mechanism (2026-08-07, session 69)** — the audit that found vpn-server needed monitoring also found a cloud routine genuinely can't do live health checks (no SSH access to vpn-server from the sandbox, nor should it get one without a deliberate decision), while an update-cadence check needs no credentials at all (public GitHub/channel data only). Split accordingly: a gaming-only systemd user timer for live health (SSH-reachable, notify-send capable), a cloud routine for the nixpkgs-staleness nudge (works from a public checkout alone). Rejected combining them into either single mechanism.
- **NixOS itself, not OPNsense/VyOS/an appliance, is the target for a future router (2026-08-06, session 69)** — `/research` explicitly optimized for *this* user's priorities (declarative, git-managed, Claude-drivable), a different axis than the general "best home firewall" question `~/projects/home-lab/docs/router-options.md` answered in May. Chosen because it reuses the exact same flake/skills/dry-run workflow already in place, with zero new credential surface for Claude to manage — at the cost of weaker router-specific community support and no turnkey IDS/IPS story. VyOS was rejected despite having the technically best AI-safe change mechanism (atomic commit + auto-rollback-via-reboot) because of the same paid-LTS-squeeze pattern the doc already rejected pfSense for. This decision lives in a sibling repo's doc, not this one — see Current Project State.
- **Pinchflat: native nixpkgs module over Docker, shared Jellyfin group over a standalone one, sops secret over `selfhosted = true` (2026-08-03, session 67)** — three deliberate choices scoped via `AskUserQuestion` rather than assumed: (1) `services.pinchflat` exists natively in nixpkgs, so no Docker/OCI container layer was needed at all, unlike every guide found during research; (2) `mediaDir` points at a new `/mnt/media/YouTube` folder sharing Jellyfin's existing `media` group, rather than the module's own `/var/lib/pinchflat/media` default, so no separate permission-bridging scheme was needed; (3) `SECRET_KEY_BASE` goes through a real sops-nix secret rather than the module's `selfhosted = true` escape hatch, to stay consistent with every other credential in this repo (nixpkgs docs themselves warn `selfhosted` isn't for production use).
- **Accepted two Pinchflat rough edges rather than chasing them further (2026-08-03, session 67)**: `collection_type: playlist` for the Yes Theory source (a confirmed-still-open upstream bug, `#607`, not fixed by re-adding with either URL form tried) is left as-is since the only known cost is cosmetic (missing series banner art); the `download_cutoff_date` filtering out all current videos is left as-is too, since the user decided they want future-uploads-only rather than a backlog, so the current cutoff is actually correct as configured, not a bug to fix.
- **SponsorBlock: enabled "mark chapters," not segment removal (2026-08-03, session 67)** — Pinchflat's native toggle only marks chapters (non-destructive: a wrongly-flagged segment just becomes an extra chapter marker), while true removal needs a custom yt-dlp flag Pinchflat doesn't expose as a first-class option and would permanently cut content on a bad match. Chose the reversible option.
- **Rejected adding a NOPASSWD sudo rule to let skills run `switch` unattended (2026-07-28, session 61)** — considered as a way to remove the recurring "sudo needs an interactive password" friction across `rollback`/`nixos-gc`/`fleet-rollout`. Declined: `nixos-rebuild switch` activates an arbitrary system config, which is effectively unrestricted root code execution, so even a rule scoped to just that one command would remove the last human checkpoint against a compromised or prompt-injected agent session. Kept the existing hand-off-to-user pattern as the standing design instead of trading that checkpoint away for convenience.
- **Network printer detection fixed via `cups-browsed`'s `CreateIPPPrinterQueues` setting, not a firewall/avahi change (2026-07-27)** — user reported niri wasn't detecting a network printer (Canon TS9500). Live diagnosis on gaming showed avahi + cups-browsed were both discovering it fine (`avahi-browse`, `lpstat -e`), but `lpstat -p` showed no actual CUPS destination — cups-browsed's compiled-in default `CreateIPPPrinterQueues=LocalOnly` only auto-creates queues for IPP-over-USB printers, never real network ones. Added `services.printing.browsedConf = "CreateIPPPrinterQueues Everywhere"` to `modules/printing.nix` (commit `6877f4b`), deep-eval-passing on all 4 hosts. Confirmed fixed on gaming after `nh os switch`. Separately, Zen Browser (Flatpak) didn't pick up the fix until manually relaunched — a stale-session-environment issue, not a second bug (see Known Issues / `feedback_session_daemon_staleness` memory). **natalie-laptop was rebuilt and fully rebooted but the printer is still not detected there** — not yet diagnosed, needs its own live investigation rather than assuming the gaming fix generalizes.
- **`improve-system` cloud routine's stale-memory bug fixed by removing the mechanism, not patching around it (2026-07-27, session 57)** — first response to the discovered bug (a persisted session trusting its own wrong "nothing changed since commit X" belief) was a targeted freshness-check patch requiring a fresh `gh`-sourced timestamp comparison each run. When the user asked how to harden the routine toward parity with a live session, went further: removed the reuse-shortcut entirely (always run the full audit fresh) and set `persist_session: false` (no cross-week memory at all), rather than keep the freshness-check band-aid. Rationale: the shortcut's only purpose was efficiency, its cost was low (~10 min for a full run, well under the 1-hour cron floor), and nothing about the routine's design actually needed cross-week memory — all durable state already lives in git/GitHub, so a stateless run is strictly safer with no real downside.
- **New "self-verifiable structural fixes" auto-apply tier added to the routine, scoped tightly (2026-07-27, session 57)** — previously every structural skill-audit finding was report-only forever, which is why the phantom 6-item backlog sat unfixed for 3 weeks even after a human had already fixed it. The new tier lets the routine auto-apply (as a PR, never merged) ONLY when a finding touches `SKILL.md`/`scripts/`/`assets/` under a skills dir (never `.nix`, so flake-eval risk is structurally zero) AND has a scriptable verification. Deliberately excludes anything touching `.nix` files (still no real dry-run possible in the cloud sandbox — confirmed via the `schedule` skill that only one CCR environment exists with no setup-script mechanism to install `nix`/`nh`) and mandates re-reading the current file content before applying anything, specifically so a stale claim (in either direction — false "still broken" or false "this is broken") gets caught before acting on it.
- **Reviewed and merged PR #4 manually rather than building a review skill first (2026-07-27, session 57)** — the deferred "does reviewing the routine's PRs need its own skill" question (open since session 46) came up again; did the review+merge by hand this time (independently re-verified all 7 claimed fixes against live repo content, not just trusting the PR body) since it was a one-off first real test. Still deferred as a skill until this becomes routine enough to be worth automating.
- **`flake-update-verify`'s commit+push stays gated even under a "do the whole thing" invocation (2026-07-26)** — Training Mode's default flip to `OFF` removed pauses from steps 1-5, but the user drew a hard line at step 6: pushing to shared `origin/main` unattended is a different risk class than the local update/verify work, which is already well-guarded by deep-eval + auto-revert. The new gate is deliberately carved out of the existing "pre-approved runs skip pauses" precedent rather than folded into it.
- **`/create-secret-scan`: no global generic scanner, every project gets a generated one (2026-07-23)** — considered keeping the NixOS repo's `secret-scan` project-specific while adding a generic global fallback for other projects; rejected in favor of always generating a project-tuned skill via interview, since a generic scanner would miss each project's actual secret-handling conventions (this repo's sops-nix integrity markers, for example).
- **`git-commit` asks before dropping unrelated uncommitted work rather than silently ignoring it (2026-07-23)** — previously committed only the files matching the current task's scope, leaving anything else untouched with no comment; genuinely unrelated work could sit uncommitted until noticed by chance. Now flags extras via `AskUserQuestion` instead of guessing intent.
- ~~niri cursor fix uses `home.pointerCursor` with `gtk.enable` off (2026-07-25)~~ — **REVERSED (2026-08-09, session 71):** that reasoning held only while `gtk-theme-name`/`gtk-icon-theme-name` were hand-set outside Nix; `944b64b` made the whole `gtk-3.0`/`gtk-4.0` `settings.ini` file declarative (theme + iconTheme + cursorTheme all set via the `gtk` module), so `home.pointerCursor.gtk.enable = true` is now correct and turned on — there's nothing left for it to silently wipe. Don't cite this old entry as a reason to leave `gtk.enable` off in a future change.
- **Extended `skill-suggestion`'s transcript-dir dedup fix to touch `skill-upgrade` and `session-closer` too, beyond what the finding named (2026-07-20, session 52)** — the routine's skill-audit finding only flagged `skill-suggestion`'s prose transcript-dir resolution as duplicating logic already scripted in the other two skills, and recommended extracting `scripts/find-transcript-dir.sh`. Rather than give `skill-suggestion` its own private copy of that logic (satisfying the letter of the finding but leaving the original duplication between the other two intact), created a genuinely shared `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/lib/find-transcript-dir.sh` (new convention, mirrors the project-local `.claude/lib/` pattern) and rewired `skill-upgrade/scripts/find-skill-misfires.sh` + `session-closer/scripts/scan-session.sh` to call it too. Chose this despite the added risk of touching two already-stable scripts because the change was small (2-3 lines each), both were smoke-tested before/after with identical output, and the alternative would have left the exact future-drift risk the finding was warning about. New `.claude/skills/lib` `home.file` entry added to `bosko-claude.nix` (recursive dir, so future shared global scripts need no further wiring).
- **`improve-system` weekly cloud routine's silent-failure root cause found and fixed: GitHub App was authorized but never installed (2026-07-20, session 52)** — Following up on session 51's third redesign (checkpoint-per-step GitHub issue), the routine was triggered manually and re-checked ~20h later: `RemoteTrigger get` confirmed it had fired, but `gh issue list`/`gh pr list` on the repo were still completely empty — the tracking issue's own creation (meant to be the routine's literal first action) never landed on GitHub at all, ruling out a mid-run hang or crash. Traced with the user through GitHub's UI: `github.com/settings/installations` (classic GitHub Apps) showed nothing; `github.com/settings/applications` → Authorized OAuth Apps also showed nothing; → **Authorized GitHub Apps** tab showed "Claude" (owned by `anthropics`) with Issues/PRs permissions listed as allowed — but cross-checking "Installed GitHub Apps" showed it wasn't there either. This is the actual bug: GitHub Apps need both user-level *authorization* (had it) and repo-level *installation* (didn't have it) — authorization alone grants no access to any specific repo. Fixed by installing the app at `github.com/apps/claude`, scoped to `CommanderBosko/NixOS`. Re-triggered the routine immediately after: tracking issue #1 created within 15 seconds, all 6 checkpoints posted, closed with a full report ~90 seconds later — first-ever clean end-to-end run. Confirms sessions 46/51's prompt-design work was correct all along; the blocker was entirely infra-side and invisible to any local tool (`WebFetch` on the routine's `claude.ai/code/routines/<id>` page 403s with no login session, so GitHub was genuinely the only reachable diagnostic channel until the App-install angle was checked).
- **`improve-system` weekly cloud routine redesigned twice for observability, not yet confirmed working (2026-07-19/20, session 51)** — its first fire (session 46) produced no PR/issue/branch and `persist_session:false` meant the run's transcript was discarded, so there was no way to distinguish "ran clean" from "failed silently." First fix: `persist_session:true`. Re-ran — still silent after 78 minutes of active polling, and the original design only ever writes anything to GitHub as its very last action (an end-of-run PR/issue), so a mid-run hang or crash looks identical to a clean pass from outside either way. Second fix (the one now live): the prompt opens a GitHub tracking issue as its literal first action, before reading any skill file, then posts a checkpoint comment after every step and only closes the issue as the final action — turning "silence" into "check whether the issue exists yet, and how far its checkpoints got." Rejected relying on the claude.ai routines web UI as the diagnostic path (`https://claude.ai/code/routines/<id>`) since `WebFetch` 403s on it (needs real browser login), so a durable public-GitHub artifact was the only channel actually reachable from a future Claude Code session. Third run kicked off 2026-07-20 00:58 UTC; outcome unknown as of session close — see Next Steps.
- **`vpn-server`'s `rtk` fix left unstable-only rather than force-installed some other way (2026-07-18, session 50)** — asked directly whether to deploy the eval fix. User declined: "no reason to do it until the stable nixpkgs is updated" — the `pkgs ? rtk` guard is the correct long-term shape regardless (self-heals on the next stable backport), so there was no pressure to work around the gap with an overlay or pinned-unstable-package-on-a-stable-host hack just to get `rtk` onto vpn-server sooner.
- **Chose an existence-guard (`pkgs ? rtk`) over a host-specific exclusion for the vpn-server break (2026-07-18, session 50)** — could have special-cased `vpn-server` out of the package list instead. Rejected: an attribute-existence check is generic or channel/arch-agnostic, self-corrects once nixpkgs-25.11 catches up, and generalizes to any future package with the same unstable-only gap, where a host-specific exclusion would need updating (or forgetting to update) every time.
- **Desktop-host rebuilds (gaming/laptop/natalie-laptop) left to the user, not offered as an in-session action (2026-07-18, session 50)** — user stated this explicitly ("I'll handle the 3 desktop host rebuilds myself"). Noted here so a future session doesn't re-offer or assume they're still pending on Claude's side.
- **Skill-audit Tier 4 findings (a `claude-rules` 4-line grep script, `new-skill`'s scope-detection mechanics) explicitly skipped (2026-07-18, session 50)** — both are global/repo-managed skills, so extraction costs a `bosko-claude.nix` symlink entry + rebuild; neither finding's payoff (a handful of lines saved) clears that bar. User's explicit call ("Ignore Tier 4").
- **Kept `niri.nix`'s Plasma-era systemd `ConditionEnvironment` gates after removing Plasma from natalie-laptop, rather than deleting them (2026-07-18, session 50)** — they're harmless no-ops with Plasma absent (the condition just always evaluates true) and correct again if a second session ever returns; ripping them out would be premature cleanup for a change the user framed as "for now," not necessarily permanent.
- **Dropped KDE apps for file-opening entirely rather than keep patching `kded6` around them (2026-07-17, session 48)** — asked directly: keep fighting niri's lack of a `kded6` session (which `ksycoca`-dependent apps like Dolphin/Ark silently need to resolve "open with" at all), or switch to apps that don't need it. A working `kded6` systemd service was drafted and flake-checked clean as the "keep KDE" option, but the user chose the swap instead: Thunar/xarchiver/zathura/imv (plain XDG resolution) over Dolphin/Ark/Okular/Gwenview/Double Commander. Net -123 MiB, and removes an entire class of bug (this session hit it three separate times before the swap) rather than mitigating it.
- **`new-skill` gained an untested-output reminder instead of absorbing smoke-testing itself (2026-07-17, session 48)** — user asked whether `/skill-suggestion` should hand off to `/ship-skill` instead of `/new-skill`. Answer was no (backwards — `ship-skill`'s Step 1 already calls `skill-suggestion`), but the underlying gap was real: `add-default-app` shipped uncommitted-then-committed without ever being smoke-tested. Chose a one-line reminder in `new-skill`'s final report over baking testing into `new-skill` itself, to keep it a clean single-bucket Utility skill rather than duplicating `ship-skill`'s Verification/Orchestration job.
- **Per-host niri overlay uses niri's native `include` directive, not Nix-level text concatenation (2026-07-17, session 47)** — initially told the user niri's KDL had no include mechanism and proposed assembling `config.kdl` by concatenating base+overlay text in the HM module. Found evidence to the contrary mid-implementation: the existing config already had `include "dms/outputs.kdl"` for DMS's own generated file. Course-corrected before implementing — surfaced the finding, got the user's go-ahead, then used a plain `include "niri-overlay.kdl"` line in the shared base plus a second `home.file` entry per host. Simpler than the original plan and validated natively via `niri validate`.
- **Gaming keeps its monitor-pinning window-rules; laptop/natalie-laptop start empty, keybinds stay identical across all 3 hosts (2026-07-17, session 47)** — user's explicit split during the interview: gaming's 7 named-workspace/window-rule pairs (tuned for its two-monitor Asus+Dell setup) aren't relevant to single-monitor laptop/natalie-laptop, so those hosts get the shared "standard" rules (WezTerm fix, Firefox PIP) only, with an empty overlay file ready for future host-specific content. Keybinds were confirmed identical across all three and stayed in the shared base unchanged.
- **Weekly `/improve-system` cloud routine opens a PR rather than pushing to `main` directly (2026-07-16, session 46)** — asked the user directly: since this is a brand-new, never-yet-run unattended workflow, a PR gives a review checkpoint before anything lands, unlike the local session-closer convention of pushing straight to `main`. Revisit once a few clean runs have built confidence.
- **`/improve-system`'s `AskUserQuestion` structural gates overridden to "report, don't apply" for the unattended cloud run (2026-07-16, session 46)** — the skill's own design assumes a human answers Approve/Skip for new-skill proposals and skill-audit refactors; a scheduled cloud run has nobody to ask. Rather than guess an answer or block, the routine's prompt explicitly tells it to only auto-apply the categories `/improve-system` already treats as low-risk (Gotchas, CLAUDE.md rules, permission allow-list additions) and to leave every structural item as a report-only line item in the PR body for manual follow-up.
- **Declined to build a "review the weekly PR" skill before the routine has ever run (2026-07-16, session 46)** — asked directly whether one was warranted. The component steps (review a PR, merge, pull, rebuild) are already covered by existing tools; building a skill around an untested workflow's output shape risks encoding wrong assumptions. Revisit after the first real Sunday run.
- **qBittorrent's login autostart disabled outright rather than patching the window-rule (2026-07-16, session 45)** — the window-rule matching `qbittorrent` was already correct; the bug was that qBittorrent's own "run on startup" preference created a pre-existing single-instance window before Mod+G ever ran, which the rule can only act on at window-creation time. No window-rule change could fix a race against an app's own autostart. Asked the user directly (three options: disable autostart, drop it from Mod+G but leave autostart, or leave everything alone) — user chose disabling autostart, since Mod+G is now the single, reliable entry point for the whole app set.
- **`add-niri-fullscreen-rule` built as a separate skill from `add-niri-window-rule`, not folded into it (2026-07-16, session 45)** — same single-responsibility split as the keybind/window-rule sibling pair: `add-niri-window-rule` sets `open-on-workspace`/`open-on-output`, this one only ever sets `open-maximized true`. Justified by real repetition — the same "add `open-maximized true` to an app's rule" edit was done 6 times by hand in one session (5 kept, 1 reverted) before the skill existed.
- **`add-niri-window-rule` built as a separate skill from `add-niri-keybind`, not folded into it (2026-07-16, session 44)** — the two workflows share verification steps (live output/app-id lookup, cross-host caveat check) but are triggered by different requests: a new keybind vs. a standalone monitor pin with no keybind. Kept as siblings per the single-responsibility bucket rule, with a one-line cross-reference in each pointing at the other so future requests route correctly rather than guessing.
- **Smoke-test's real config edit (qBittorrent→`dell-4`) kept rather than reverted (2026-07-16, session 44)** — asked the user directly whether the `add-niri-window-rule` smoke test's output (a real workspace + window-rule addition) should be treated as throwaway or kept. User chose to keep it, since they'd already confirmed it for real during the test — the smoke test doubled as a legitimate feature request.
- **Swapped the pnpm insecure waiver for an electron one rather than letting the bump fail (2026-07-16, session 43)** — pnpm-10.29.2's CVE fix landed upstream (vesktop now builds against generic `pnpm_10`), so that waiver became dead weight; the same nixpkgs bump immediately introduced a new insecure flag on `electron-40.10.5` (EOL), which vesktop wraps its runtime binary around. Asked the user directly ("is the electron waiver a safe option?") before adding it — confirmed and added, same waiver mechanism and same removal condition (retest at each future bump) as the pnpm one it replaced.
- **`improve-system`'s new-skill handoff borrows just `ship-skill`'s smoke-test step, not a full switch to `ship-skill` (2026-07-16, session 43)** — user asked directly whether improve-system should call `ship-skill` instead of `new-skill` for its skill-suggestion handoff. Declined: `ship-skill`'s per-skill commit + push-confirmation pause would fragment improve-system's single consolidated end-of-run commit into multiple, and add a second confirmation gate mid-run. Instead added just the smoke-test step, so an untested new skill doesn't ride along unverified into that consolidated commit.
- **kdeglobals ColorScheme write no longer gated on `XDG_CURRENT_DESKTOP` (2026-07-16, session 43)** — the guard only ever passed for an interactive live-session `nh os switch`; this repo's actual workflow (`nh os boot` + reboot) activates before any session exists, so it silently no-op'd on every real rebuild. Made the write unconditional — safe because a real Plasma session re-applies its own color scheme on login regardless.
- **niri.nix's DMS fix scoped to all niri hosts, not just natalie-laptop (2026-07-12, session 42)** — the bug (DMS HM wiring targeting only `home-manager.users.bosko`) was reported on natalie-laptop, but `niri.nix` is shared by gaming, laptop, and natalie-laptop. Asked the user directly via `AskUserQuestion` rather than assuming scope: fixing it in the shared module (chosen) means natty gets working DMS-on-niri everywhere niri is used; a natalie-laptop-only override was the alternative, rejected as inconsistent with how every other user's HM config is composed in this repo.
- **Hand-ported `omarchy-nix`'s good ideas rather than importing it as a flake dependency, even after seeing how much deeper it is (2026-07-11, session 41)** — asked directly for the downside of just importing the flake. Six concrete conflicts, not hypothetical ones: (1) architecturally a 15-file external tree vs. this repo's flat one-file-per-DE convention that `lib.deSmoke` assumes; (2) its `system.nix` redeclares options (`hardware.bluetooth.enable`, `services.pipewire`, etc.) already set by this repo's `desktopModules`, real conflicting-definition risk; (3) it replaces SDDM with greetd+tuigreet, breaking the SDDM-everywhere convention; (4) it builds Hyprland from source via its own pinned input instead of the nixpkgs cache; (5) it flips `nixpkgs.config.allowUnfree` globally and wants personal-identity options (`full_name`/`email_address`); (6) it's unmaintained with its own drifting dependency graph. Traded upstream's extra depth for staying inside a well-understood, repo-consistent module.
- **`nix-colors` added as a new flake input for real base16 theme-switching (2026-07-11, session 41)** — the previous Tokyo Night palette was hand-copied hex scattered across Hyprland/Waybar/Hyprlock, which only replicated Omarchy's *look*. `nix-colors` gives the actual *mechanism* (one `colorScheme` line feeds every component) without adding real dependency risk — it only pulls a lib-only nixpkgs repackage and the tinted-theming schemes data repo, not full nixpkgs, so no `follows` was needed.
- **`de-smoke-check` built as its own skill rather than folded into `deep-eval-check` (2026-07-11, session 41)** — `deep-eval-check` loops over the four *real* hosts; this covers the 9 DE modules no host imports, using `lib.deSmoke.<name>` instead of `nixosConfigurations.<host>`. Different target set and a different reason to reach for it (verifying an edit vs. a pre-rollout gate), so kept as a separate Verification-bucket skill per the single-responsibility rule, with each skill's docs cross-referencing the other.
- **`omarchy.nix` built from scratch rather than importing the `omarchy-nix` community flake (2026-07-10, session 40)** — a 10-source `/research` pass found `omarchy-nix` (Henry Sipp) is architecturally reusable (clean `nixosModules`/`homeManagerModules` pair) but hard-assumes greetd+PipeWire+its own NetworkManager/Bluetooth stack, directly conflicting with this repo's shared SDDM and `desktop-networking.nix`, plus ~8 months of dormancy (last commit 2025-11-13). Building a self-contained module matching this repo's existing DE-module pattern avoided that conflict entirely and kept consistency with the other 10 modules.
- **`omarchy.nix` kept fully independent of `hyprland.nix`, not layered on top (2026-07-10, session 40)** — user's explicit call. Omarchy's real shell (Waybar+Hyprlock+Walker) is unrelated to the DMS integration `hyprland.nix` just gained, so sharing a base would mean conditionally disabling DMS-specific bits rather than a clean reuse.
- **`hyprland.nix` deliberately left without a Nix-managed `hyprland.conf`/keybinds (2026-07-10, session 40)** — discovered by reading the actual `dms` flake input source that, unlike niri, DMS has no declarative NixOS/HM module for Hyprland: since Hyprland 0.55 it manages Hyprland's config **imperatively** via `dms setup` (Lua-based, migrates/backs up any existing `hyprland.conf`). A Nix-managed file would just get swept into DMS's own backup folder on first `dms run` — this was surfaced back to the user mid-task (original ask was "mirror niri-config.kdl's binds") and the scope was revised live rather than forcing an approach that would silently break on first login.
- **`ship-skill` pauses for confirmation before push, not before commit (2026-07-10, session 39)** — considered making the whole build→test→commit→push chain fully autonomous once kicked off, since the user had already approved building the skill. Rejected: by the time commit happens, the skill has made two consequential decisions in a row (what to build, and that it works) with zero user checkpoint since kickoff — pushing a third (making it public/permanent on `origin`) without a pause felt like one decision too many. Commit stays automatic since it's local and trivially reversible; push gets the explicit `AskUserQuestion` stop.
- **A managed-global skill goes live in the *same* session once its symlink exists, no restart needed (2026-07-10, session 39)** — previously assumed (session 38 note) that global-skill changes need a fresh session after rebuild+reboot. Directly falsified: after the user ran `nh os boot`, `ship-skill` was immediately invocable in this same running session — the skill list is read from disk per-invocation, not cached at session start. Rebuild is still required (untracked/uncommitted files aren't visible to flake eval), but the extra "new session" step in prior guidance was unnecessary.
- **Dolphin's `kdeglobals` fix applied via a `kwriteconfig6` `home.activation` script, not a `home.file` symlink (2026-07-10, session 38)** — every other piece of Qt/KDE glue in `niri.nix` (`qt6ct.conf`) is a force-managed, read-only `home.file` symlink, and that was the first approach considered here too. Rejected for `kdeglobals` specifically because, unlike `qt6ct.conf`, KDE apps actively write real state back into it (recent files, window geometry, `KFileDialog` settings) — a read-only symlink would silently break Dolphin's ability to persist its own settings. `kwriteconfig6` (from `kdePackages.kconfig`) sets just the one `[UiSettings] ColorScheme=*` key idempotently on every activation, leaving the rest of the file mutable.
- **`modules/` moved top-level; `dotfiles/` reserved for actual HM/dotfile content (2026-07-09, session 37)** — `dotfiles/common/modules/` contained system-level NixOS modules (security, bootloader, nvidia…), which misled readers of a public repo. Chose the minimal move (only `modules/` relocates; `dotfiles/common/configs` + `dotfiles/bosko` stay put) over a full `modules/` + `home/` restructure — after the move the `dotfiles/` name is already truthful, and the smaller blast radius kept the change verifiable as a pure rename.
- **`vpn.nix` moved into `desktopModules`, accepting a new-host coupling (2026-07-09, session 37)** — all three desktop hosts are VPN clients, so per-host imports were pure repetition. Known cost, discovered during the work: `vpn.nix` references `secrets/hosts/<hostname>.yaml` unconditionally, so a **future desktop host now needs its sops secrets file to exist at first eval** (wg keypair encrypted to admin; host key added post-boot as usual). The `new-host` skill and its templates were updated to say so. `nvidia.nix` deliberately stays per-host (the AMD-swap escape hatch is real; VPN membership isn't per-host policy on this fleet).
- **DE smoke checks run weekly + on dispatch, not on every push (2026-07-09, session 37)** — `lib.deSmoke` (laptop config × each DE module) exists because unused DE modules are never evaluated by host builds and rot silently — proven immediately when the first local run caught pantheon/mate breakage. Gated the CI job to `schedule`/`workflow_dispatch`: DE rot accumulates on the timescale of nixpkgs bumps, not pushes, and push/PR CI stays at ~3 min. The job measured ~5 min, so moving it on-push later is a one-line `if:` deletion if ever wanted.
- **Refactor verified by drvPath comparison, not just eval success (2026-07-09, session 37)** — for a pure reorganization the bar was "provably nothing changed": captured every host's `toplevel.drvPath` before touching anything; vpn-server came back bit-identical, and the desktop hosts (whose drvPaths legitimately shift from package-list merge order) were compared field-by-field via `nix eval --apply` against committed HEAD (`git+file://` = baseline, `.#` = dirty tree) — package sets, flatpaks, sshd, DNS, wg, stateVersion all identical. Worth reusing for any future no-op-intended refactor; a dry-run alone only proves the local host.
- **Kept the 3 new redundant `Bash(*)` permission entries rather than pruning them (2026-07-09, session 36)** — asked directly whether to prune project-level Bash allow entries now that they're all functionally superseded by the global `Bash(*)` wildcard. Decided to keep: removing them buys zero reduction in prompts (the global wildcard already suppresses everything), but loses a documented, already-vetted "these are genuinely read-only" list that would need re-deriving if the global `Bash(*)` is ever tightened later. `mcp__nixos__nix` is the one entry type that's genuinely load-bearing, since `Bash(*)` doesn't cover MCP tools.
- **`qt6ct.conf` pulled into Nix; DMS's `settings.json`/`matugen.conf` stay unmanaged (2026-07-09, session 35)** — Asked directly whether any of the three files touched while debugging the Qt-theming bug should move into the repo. `qt6ct.conf` is genuinely static glue config that DMS only touches (mtime bump, never rewrites content), so a read-only Nix copy is low-risk (worst case a cosmetic touch warning) and prevents this exact bug recurring on reinstall. `settings.json` and `matugen.conf` were rejected: both are actively rewritten by DMS itself at runtime (every settings-UI change, every wallpaper/theme switch), so a read-only Nix symlink over either would break DMS's own live writes — the same reasoning session 34 already applied to `dms/*.kdl`.
- **Niri `config.kdl` managed via Home Manager despite a known future-friction risk (2026-07-09, session 34)** — Considered leaving the file unmanaged to avoid ever blocking a DMS auto-injected `include` line (DMS is designed to add more `include`s into `config.kdl` as its integration toggles are enabled, and a Nix-managed symlink is read-only). Chose to manage it anyway: the failure mode is mild and self-announcing (a DMS setting just wouldn't visually apply, fixed with a 30-second manual edit + rebuild), versus the alternative of the file staying an unversioned, non-reproducible mutable state permanently — the same tradeoff already accepted for `katerc`/`kitty.conf`.
- **DMS's own generated state deliberately left unmanaged (2026-07-09, session 34)** — `DankMaterialShell/settings.json` and `niri/dms/*.kdl` are written live by DMS's own settings UI (themes, colors, per-output layout). Managing those declaratively would fight the point of having a GUI theme picker: a read-only Nix symlink would make every live UI change fail to persist or get overwritten on next rebuild.
- **Project `commit`/`push` skills deleted; the global `git-commit`/`git-push` pair is now the only commit/push path (2026-07-03, session 30)** — Partially supersedes session 28's "fixed for drift but NOT made identical" call: closing the behavioral gaps left the two pairs as near-duplicates whose overlapping triggers made skill selection unpredictable. Deleted the **project** pair rather than the global one because the global pair must keep working in the user's other repos (farmer, FinanceGuru) — exactly the reason session 28 refused to copy the `-C /home/bosko/NixOS` pinning into it. The repo-specific commit-format reference moved to CLAUDE.md (a doc, not a skill), and the hardcoded `-C`/`origin main` conventions died with the files — sessions run inside the repo anyway.
- **natalie-laptop keeps DHCP; drift is handled at the router, not in NixOS config (2026-07-03, session 30)** — When its LAN IP moved .103 → .101, a static IP in `networking.nix` was considered and rejected: the machine is a roaming laptop, and a hardcoded address breaks on every other network it joins. Correct fix if recurrence matters is a router-side DHCP reservation (MAC → .101); the repo just records whatever the router currently hands out, in `hosts.json` + `ssh.nix`.
- **Backups scrapped after scoping — no unbacked irreplaceable data exists (2026-07-02, session 29)** — A restic/B2/WireGuard-tier backup architecture was proposed and interview-scoped, then the user killed it on the facts: projects are in GitHub repos, photos/videos are already backed up on an existing cloud service, games re-download, and the only genuinely unbacked data (Jellyfin state on gaming) is an explicitly accepted loss. Recorded in memory so backups are never re-proposed as pending. The sops admin age key was confirmed **two-copy** (gaming + laptop), closing the last residual.
- **pnpm insecurity waived temporarily rather than re-pinning nixpkgs or overlaying vesktop (2026-07-02, session 29)** — Three options for the vesktop/pnpm-10.29.2 eval breakage: (a) hold nixpkgs back again (rejected — throws away two weeks of security updates the unpin just gained, for a build-tool-only CVE), (b) overlay vesktop to use `pnpm_10` like master (rejected — needs a new `pnpmDeps` fetch hash and a from-source build; master's fix arrives on the channel by itself), (c) scoped `permittedInsecurePackages` waiver with a removal condition (chosen — the CVEs are in the pnpm CLI at build time, not vesktop's runtime; the standard nixpkgs mechanism for exactly this). Removal is tracked in Known Issues, memory, and the waiver's own comment.
- **CI must deep-eval each host; `nix flake check` alone is not a gate (2026-07-02, session 29)** — Proven empirically: flake check verifies `nixosConfigurations` only shallowly (toplevel-is-a-derivation, not full eval), so both the local check and the first CI run passed a config on which every desktop host failed to evaluate. The workflow now forces each host's `toplevel.drvPath`; the same rule was written into the flake-check and flake-update-verify skills as gotchas. Lesson: "flake check passed" means "no shallow errors," nothing more.
- **`git-commit`/`git-push` fixed for behavioral drift, but NOT made identical to `commit`/`push` (2026-07-02, session 28)** — The skill-audit flagged the global (any-project) `git-commit`/`git-push` as having drifted from the NixOS-repo-specific `commit`/`push`. Fixed the genuine gaps (git-push had zero confirmation gate at all; git-commit's skip-confirm behavior was undocumented) but deliberately did **not** copy `commit`/`push`'s `-C /home/bosko/NixOS` pinning or hardcoded `origin main` — those are correct only because those two skills are project-local and NixOS-only; copying them into the global pair would break git-commit/git-push in the user's other repos (farmer, FinanceGuru), which need to operate on the current cwd/branch instead.
- **Team-member knowledge-path duplication resolved via cross-reference, not a shared config.json (2026-07-02, session 28)** — Same call as session 22's `[[deliberately skipped]]` team-`*` config consolidation: the four skills are separately-symlinked global skills with no shared-file mechanism without new `home.file` wiring, and the actual drift risk of a static repo-internal path is low. Added a one-line cross-reference in all four copies instead, so a future rename gets caught rather than silently drifting.
- **Declined password rotation after the branch-leak fix (2026-07-02, session 27)** — Offered rotating `bosko`/`natty` login passwords given the ~7-week public exposure window on the deleted branches (see above). User's call: skip it, judging it unlikely anyone found/used the hashes in that window. Recorded as a deliberate accepted-risk decision, not an oversight — revisit if any sign of unauthorized access surfaces.
- **Deleted the leaking branches outright rather than rewriting them (2026-07-02, session 27)** — Two options were on the table: `git filter-repo --replace-text` to scrub and keep the branches, or delete them outright. User chose deletion — simpler, and these were old version-tag branches with no indication of active use, so preserving their history had no clear value against the cost of a second filter-repo pass.
- **`research` skill: fresh `general-purpose` sub-agents, configurable count (default 10), chat-only output (2026-07-02, session 27)** — Three open questions from the interview: (1) sub-agent type — chose fresh `general-purpose` over `fork`, since each review agent only needs the topic + its one URL, not the coordinator's conversation history; forking would carry unneeded context and cost. (2) result count — made it an optional second argument (`research <topic> <n>`) rather than hardcoding 10, so a quick lookup doesn't always pay for a full 10-way fan-out; default stays 10 to match the original ask. (3) output — chat text only, not an Artifact; the user picked this directly over the recommended Artifact option, so a future revisit (e.g. if reports get long enough to want a scannable page) should re-ask rather than assume.
- **niri X11 support relies on niri's built-in `xwayland-satellite` spawn, not a manual service (2026-07-01, session 26)** — First attempt manually managed `xwayland-satellite` + a global `DISPLAY` export, which broke the SDDM greeter (Weston picked the X11 backend over DRM and crashed to a black screen) because the session-variable export isn't scoped to the graphical session only. Corrected by removing the manual service/export and relying on niri ≥ 25.08's built-in auto-spawn + auto-`DISPLAY`, keeping only the package on `systemPackages`. Lesson: `environment.sessionVariables` is global to every session type (including the display manager's own greeter), so exports meant only for a compositor's user session need a session-scoped mechanism, not a system-wide one.
- **`session-closer` reads the transcript as ground truth, not the live context window (2026-06-29, session 23)** — Added transcript-reading to STEP 2 of `session-closer` so a close mines the unsummarized on-disk JSONL rather than reconstructing from a context window that drops early turns as it grows. Complements the session-21 change that gave `skill-upgrade`/`skill-suggestion` transcript-*history* awareness — same store (`~/.claude/projects/`), but session-closer reads the *current* session for accuracy where those two grep *past* sessions for recurrence. Kept the multi-MB guard (extract turns with `jq`, never dump raw).
- **Managed permission layer stays separate from the `/improve-system` allow-list — do not sync (2026-06-29, session 23)** — The destructive-command `deny`/`ask` rules and fork-bomb hook live in the NixOS-managed `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json` (highest precedence, system-deployed, Claude can't auto-edit it). `/improve-system`'s `fewer-permission-prompts` step only appends `permissions.allow` to the per-project `.claude/settings.json`. Merging them was considered and **rejected**: it would either duplicate the guardrails into a lower-precedence Claude-editable file or hand the orchestrator the power to loosen safety rails — and `trimClaudeSettings` already deletes any deny/ask/hooks that leak into the personal settings on every rebuild. Separation is intentional. (Aside noted, not acted on: the global `~/.claude/settings.json` allows `Bash(*)`, so the project allow-list entries are effectively redundant and `fewer-permission-prompts` is near-no-op for this setup.)
- **Transcript-history awareness added to the two session-reactive skills only (2026-06-28, session 21)** — `/improve-system`'s five sub-skills were checked against `~/.claude/projects/` (the session transcript store). Only `skill-upgrade` and `skill-suggestion` got the addition: they previously looked at just the live conversation, and cross-session signal is exactly their value (a recurring misfire / a workflow repeated across sessions). Deliberately **not** added to `claude-rules` (edits `CLAUDE.md` only), `skill-audit` (audits skill files, not history), or `improve-system` itself (pure orchestrator — the work belongs in the sub-skills it invokes). `fewer-permission-prompts` already scans transcripts but is a non-editable built-in. Both edits mandate `grep`, never whole-file reads — transcripts run multi-MB and would blow context.
- **`/improve-system` is a thin orchestrator, not a merged mega-skill (2026-06-28, session 20)** — The user wanted "one skill to upgrade my Claude ecosystem." Rather than flatten `skill-upgrade`/`skill-suggestion`/`claude-rules`/`skill-audit`/`fewer-permission-prompts` into one giant skill, `/improve-system` *invokes* each via the Skill tool and gates their effects. Rationale: each sub-skill stays single-responsibility and independently runnable (matching the bucket gate), there's no logic to drift out of sync, and the orchestrator only owns sequencing + the auto-apply-low-risk / confirm-structural policy. Rejected the merge because it would duplicate five skills' logic and re-introduce the drift class `/skill-audit` exists to catch.
- **VPN endpoint IP + subnets scrubbed from the public README only, not repo-wide (2026-06-28, session 20)** — User asked the README to stop carrying sensitive network detail. Redacted the endpoint IP / VPN+LAN subnets / per-host addresses from `README.md` and added a guardrail to `session-closer` so future closes stay clean. **Deliberately scoped to the README:** the same values remain in host configs, `.claude/hosts.json`, the state-doc history, and git history, because `wg-quick` needs the endpoint string and scrubbing it everywhere risks breaking the tunnel for no real security gain (WireGuard's security doesn't depend on endpoint secrecy). Supersedes, *for the README only*, the 2026-06-15 "endpoint IP intentionally left in-repo" decision. The user was shown the residual exposure and chose to leave it at the README.
- **Pinned `nixpkgs` back lock-only rather than reverting the whole update or patching `kernelFile` (2026-06-26, session 19)** — The new unstable rev broke the zen kernel (`vmlinuz` shipped where `bzImage` is declared). Three options weighed: (a) `git restore flake.lock` to drop all 6 bumps, (b) override `system.boot.loader.kernelFile = "vmlinuz"` in config, (c) pin only `nixpkgs` back via `--override-input` and keep the other 5. Chose (c): it preserves the good `home-manager`/`sops-nix`/etc. bumps while dodging the one broken input, and a **lock-only** pin (no `flake.nix` edit) means the next `/update` naturally re-resolves nixpkgs forward and lifts the pin once upstream is fixed. Rejected (a) as throwing away good updates, and (b) because forcing `kernelFile` would actually mis-name the boot image at activation (the real bug is upstream's target/output mismatch, not our config). `home-manager`/`dms`/`financeguru`/`sops-nix` all `follows = "nixpkgs"`, so they ride the pinned-back nixpkgs too — fine, they build against it.
- **`.claude/hosts.json` is the single source of truth for host data (2026-06-23, session 18)** — Host SSH targets, IPs, and the WireGuard peer→host map were duplicated inline across 8 skills and had drifted into a real bug. Centralized into one JSON file that scripts read via `jq` and prose skills resolve at runtime. Rejected keeping per-skill copies (that's what rotted) and rejected deriving everything from `flake.nix`/`networking.nix` (SSH user@host targets aren't fully encoded there). Skills now read it instead of re-hardcoding.
- **vpn-server SSH login is `bosko@`, not `ubuntu@` (2026-06-23, session 18)** — Verified live: `ubuntu@150.136.232.63` is **denied**; `bosko@150.136.232.63` works with passwordless sudo (it's a NixOS host now, `commonModules` defines `bosko`). Several skills had documented the Oracle cloud-init `ubuntu` user — that was stale and made `/vpn-status` fail outright. Lesson applied going forward: verify a constant against the live system before trusting an inline copy; the docs can be the stale side.
- **Dropped the phantom `nixos-server`/`server` host, kept `pi-hole` (2026-06-23, session 18)** — `server` is not in the flake (only 4 hosts) and `nixos-server` doesn't even resolve, so `remote-rebuild #server` and `nix-repl`'s `nixosConfigurations.server` were broken. Removed it from `remote-rebuild`/`nix-repl`/`ssh-host`/`hosts.json` and `audit-config`'s host list. `pi-hole` is a real LAN DNS appliance (10.0.0.20) but not a flake host and `bosko@` SSH is denied — kept as a non-flake reference per user choice (vs. removing both).
- **Skill quality is governed by a bucket gate + 5 structural lenses (2026-06-23, session 18)** — Every skill must fit one bucket (Utility / Verification / Data Enrichment / Orchestration; Orchestration coordinating other skills is *not* straddling) and is audited against: deterministic→`scripts/`, templates→`assets/`, re-entered-config→shared JSON, pick-ones→AskUserQuestion, invocation inputs→`## Arguments`. Baked into `new-skill` (the gate) and the new `/skill-audit` meta-skill (the sweep). Two things deliberately *not* adopted: global-skill `assets/` extraction (per-file symlink overhead) and an `arguments:` YAML frontmatter key (unconfirmed support — use `## Arguments` prose).
- **Loop run-logs are gitignored, loop config is tracked (2026-06-22, session 17)** — Added `.claude/loops/**/output-*.md` and `memory-*.md` to `.gitignore`. Rationale: these are machine-generated, per-run, ever-accumulating artifacts that the next run reads back locally — committing them buys nothing and grows history noise. Crucially this does **not** affect skill portability: the loop *skills* live in `.claude/skills/` and are already tracked, so they travel with any clone. Chose a narrow `output-*`/`memory-*` glob rather than ignoring all of `.claude/loops/` so genuine config like `public-repo-guard/baseline-allowlist.md` stays tracked. Verified with `git check-ignore` that run-logs match and the allowlist does not.
- **First three loops + their per-loop policy choices (2026-06-21, session 16)** — Built `/fleet-rollout`, `/flake-update-verify`, `/public-repo-guard`. Key baked-in decisions: (1) **rollout uses dry-run-gate-then-`switch` with a full health sweep per host**, not the repo's usual `boot`-only convention — rejected `boot`+dry because health checks are meaningless against an inactive config, and rejected bare `switch` because the dry-run gate catches eval breaks before any activation; (2) **`flake-update-verify` commits the lock but never applies, and restores the previous lock on eval failure** rather than leaving a broken tree or auto-pinning — chose plain `git checkout -- flake.lock` over a saved-copy/`pin-input` path for the revert (the loop's clean-tree precondition makes git the reliable source of truth); applying stays a separate, deliberate `/fleet-rollout` step; (3) **`public-repo-guard` converges via a committed baseline-allowlist file**, not run-memory carry-forward — a durable, diffable canonical list of accepted findings is more reliable than re-deriving intentional-vs-genuine each run, and the loop only appends to it with approval. All three default Training Mode ON; `/fleet-rollout` especially should stay ON since it activates live configs.
- **Generated loops are project-local, not repo-global (2026-06-20, session 15)** — `/create-loop` writes generated loops to `<repo>/.claude/skills/`, not into `dotfiles/bosko/claude/skills/`. Rationale: repo-global skills are symlinked read-only from `/nix/store` and need a `bosko-claude.nix` edit + rebuild *per loop* — unacceptable friction for an artifact meant to be spun up on demand. Project-local skills are writable and resolve immediately. The tradeoff (loops aren't available in other projects) is fine: loops are task-specific to the repo they're created in. `/create-loop` *itself* stays repo-global so it's available everywhere.
- **Loops are standalone/self-orchestrating, not thin `/loop` wrappers (2026-06-20, session 15)** — Each generated loop contains its own orchestration (steps, done-rule checks, Training Mode, retry cap) and runs as one command `/<loop-name>`. The built-in `/loop` can still wrap it for interval scheduling. Chose this over generating plain task-skills driven by `/loop` so a loop is a complete, inspectable artifact. Caveat documented in the template: Training-Mode-ON pauses need an interactive turn, so unattended/`/loop`-scheduled runs should flip to OFF.
- **Per-loop log dir for Output + Memory, not the global memory system (2026-06-20, session 15)** — Each run writes `.claude/loops/<loop>/output-<date>.md` and `memory-<date>.md`. Kept separate from `~/.claude/.../memory/` so a loop's run history is self-contained and diffable run-over-run, and so machine-generated loop logs don't pollute the curated project memory index.
- **Deploy vpn-server via `boot` + reboot, not `switch` (2026-06-17, session 13)** — `nixos-rebuild switch` over SSH runs `switch-to-configuration` as a `systemd-run --pipe` unit tied to the SSH connection; vpn-server's activation restarts networking, dropping SSH and tearing the activation down half-applied (corrupts firewall/NAT). `boot` writes the bootloader default without a runtime activation, so SSH never drops; the subsequent reboot does a clean boot-time activation. Detached `systemd-run --collect` is the alternative for no-reboot deploys/rollbacks. Captured in the `remote-rebuild` Gotchas.
- **`wg0` MTU clamped to 1380 (2026-06-17, session 13)** — The Oracle path carries only ~1400-byte inner packets; wg-quick's default 1420 + black-holed PMTUD silently dropped full-size packets, corrupting large downloads. `mtu = 1380` in shared `vpn.nix` fixes it for all clients. Chose lowering the interface MTU (bounds inner MSS directly) over relying on TCP MSS clamping/PMTUD.
- **`nixpkgs-stable` → `nixos-25.11` (2026-06-17, session 13)** — 25.05 hit EOL (dropped from the supported channel list); 25.11 (Xantusia) is current stable. Bumped the input; stateVersion was already 25.11. vpn-server is the only consumer. Supersedes the 2026-05-21 "pinned to nixos-25.05" decision below.
- **fwupd ESP left undetected on gaming, override removed (2026-06-15, session 5)** — Confirmed empirically that neither `OverrideESPMountPoint` (dead key in fwupd 2.1.4) nor `EspLocation` can force fwupd 2.x to accept gaming's ESP, because 2.x enumerates ESPs strictly by partition type and the partition carries MBR type `0x0c` instead of `0xEF`. The only real fix is a partition-type change (`parted /dev/nvme0n1 set 1 esp on`), which was **deliberately declined**: no LVFS firmware exists for this ASRock X570 board, Secure Boot is off (dbx updates moot), and the lone updatable device updates over ATA not the capsule path — so the fix buys zero benefit while touching the boot partition table. Removed the config workaround entirely rather than carry a no-op that implies it works. Revisit only if gaming moves to GPT + Secure Boot or an LVFS-supported board.
- **Declarative settings trim via HM activation script (2026-06-09, session 3)** — `home.activation.trimClaudeSettings` in `bosko-claude.nix` replaces the one-shot `~/.claude/trim-after-managed.sh` script. On every rebuild it automatically strips redundant deny/ask/hooks keys from `~/.claude/settings.json` (idempotent; no-op if the keys are already absent or the managed file does not yet exist). Scoped to bosko only; if natty ever uses Claude Code it should be added for her separately. Chose Home Manager activation over a systemd unit or login hook because activation runs in the correct user context and is guaranteed to execute on every generation switch.
- **Managed Claude Code policy via NixOS module (2026-06-09)** — `claude-code.nix` deploys the policy through `environment.etc` so it is system-managed, version-controlled, and tamper-resistant. `jq` is pinned to its Nix store path inside the JSON so the fork bomb hook works regardless of the shell's `$PATH`. Chose `environment.etc` over a declarative file symlink because the content is derived from Nix expressions (package store paths) that must be interpolated at build time.
- **Personal `~/.claude/settings.json` trimmed to prefs only (2026-06-09)** — After confirming the managed file was active, all deny/ask rules and the fork bomb hook were removed from the personal file. The managed layer handles policy; the personal file now holds only tool preferences.
- **Package consolidation to `shell.nix` (2026-06-09)** — `nodejs`, `pnpm`, `p7zip`, and `jq` moved to `commonModules` shell.nix. Rationale: these are general-purpose tools with no desktop or host-specific justification; consolidation removes duplication and ensures consistency across all hosts including vpn-server.
- **natalie-laptop switched to Plasma (2026-06-04)** — Switched from Cosmic to Plasma 6 to align with gaming and share the `krohnkite` tiling configuration without per-host duplication.
- **krohnkite added to shared `plasma.nix` (2026-06-05)** — Any Plasma host should get the tiling script automatically; keeping it per-host creates duplication. Added directly to `plasma.nix` `systemPackages`.
- **lutris re-enabled (2026-06-03)** — The `openldap-2.6.13-i686-linux` binary cache regression that blocked `lutris` since May 2026 is resolved in nixpkgs-unstable. Un-commented in `gaming.nix` and flake lock bumped.
- **Root SSH disabled on vpn-server (2026-06-03)** — `PermitRootLogin = "no"` replaces `"prohibit-password"`. Rationale: the server is internet-facing (TCP 22 open); key-based root login still presents an elevated-privilege surface. `bosko` with passwordless sudo is the new deploy path. `wheelNeedsPassword = false` is safe because bosko requires SSH key auth to reach the machine at all.
- **SDDM auto-login disabled on gaming (2026-06-03)** — `autoLogin.enable = false`. Gaming is a physically accessible workstation; an unauthenticated session from physical access is a meaningful risk. Now active on gaming and confirmed working.
- **Avahi `openFirewall` deferred (2026-06-03)** — Closing UDP 5353 would break mDNS-based printer discovery on all three desktop hosts. The correct fix (interface-scoped rules) requires careful testing. Accepted as a known risk for now; tracked in Known Issues.
- **remote-rebuild skill updated to bosko@ (2026-06-03)** — All skill documentation now targets `bosko@150.136.232.63`. The old `root@` path is permanently blocked as of this session's deploy.
- **`mistral-nemo:12b` selected as Hermes Agent model (2026-05-26)** — The only evaluated model satisfying all three constraints: 128K context (above Hermes Agent's hard 64K minimum), excellent tool-calling support, and ~7GB Q4 quantisation within the RTX 3070's 8GB VRAM. `qwen2.5:7b` (32K context), `llama3.1:8b` (poor tool-calling), and `qwen2.5:14b` (~9GB, VRAM OOM) were all rejected. `hermes-3-llama-3.1:8b` was rejected by Hermes Agent's context-window gate at startup.
- **Dummy `api_key = "ollama"` in Hermes settings (2026-05-26)** — The OpenAI client library validates that `api_key` is non-empty before making the request; Ollama ignores the value. A non-empty dummy string is the correct workaround.
- **Ollama config colocated in `hermes-agent.nix`, not a separate file (2026-05-26)** — A single-module approach keeps the gaming LLM stack coherent; `hosts/gaming/ollama.nix` was created and then absorbed into `hermes-agent.nix` in the same session.
- **`bosko` in `hermes` group, declared in `hermes-agent.nix` (2026-05-26)** — The `hermes` group only exists on gaming; group membership belongs in the same gaming-specific file. `users.nix` (shared across all hosts) is not the right place for a group that only exists on one host.
- **SSH config API corrected to `programs.ssh.settings` (2026-05-26)** — Migrated from deprecated `programs.ssh.matchBlocks`; `enableDefaultConfig = false` added to prevent implicit defaults.
- **SSH config migrated into Home Manager (2026-05-25)** — All five SSH hosts declared in `dotfiles/common/configs/ssh.nix` with explicit `user` fields. Version-controlled, reproducible across hosts, rebuilt automatically on each generation. Both users inherit the same block via `home.nix`. The previous hand-maintained `~/.ssh/config` file should be removed on each host after the next rebuild.
- **`vpn-on`/`vpn-off` aliases use `systemctl start/stop wg-quick-wg0` (2026-05-22)** — `wg-quick` is not in PATH for non-root zsh sessions; `systemctl` avoids the PATH dependency entirely. The systemd unit name is `wg-quick-wg0` (hyphen-joined), which is how systemd renders the `wg-quick@wg0` template instance.
- **`audit=0` appended on vpn-server via `lib.mkAfter` (2026-05-22)** — The Oracle Cloud ARM kernel's broken audit subsystem floods `kauditd` on boot, causing PAM D-Bus timeouts that drop SSH sessions during `nixos-rebuild switch`. Using `lib.mkAfter [ "audit=0" ]` in `hosts/vpn-server/configuration.nix` appends after `security.nix`'s `audit=1`; last value wins at boot. This avoids touching `security.nix` or disrupting other hosts.
- **`hm-session-vars.sh` sourced in `zsh.shellInit` (2026-05-22)** — Non-login shells (terminal emulators, scripted tool calls) do not automatically source the HM session vars file. Adding sourcing to `shellInit` (not `interactiveShellInit`) covers both interactive and non-interactive non-login sessions. The `if [ -f ... ]` guard is safe on hosts that have not yet been rebuilt.
- **`nixfmt-classic` replaced with `nixfmt` (2026-05-22)** — The `nixfmt-classic` package is deprecated upstream; `nixfmt` is the actively maintained successor. Updated in `shell.nix` (system package) and `helix.nix` (formatter reference).
- **Printing module extracted to `printing.nix` (2026-05-21, late session)** — Replaced duplicated per-host CUPS/Avahi config with a shared `modules/printing.nix` (CUPS, Avahi mDNS, gutenprint/hplip/brlaser drivers). Added to `desktopModules`. `kdePackages.print-manager` retained in `plasma.nix` — it is a KDE-specific print-job GUI and does not belong in a DE-agnostic module. Two-step commit: `print-manager` was first moved into `printing.nix` then corrected back to `plasma.nix`.
- **`~/.local/bin` added to bosko sessionPath (2026-05-21, evening)** — `home.sessionPath = [ "$HOME/.local/bin" ]` added to `dotfiles/bosko/bosko-claude.nix`. Diagnosed via `/doctor`: native claude-code binary installed at `~/.local/bin/claude` was not in PATH. Fix scoped to bosko's HM config (not shared with natty). Dry-run confirmed clean (+2.94 MiB closure, no package changes, no service restarts).
- **`server` placeholder removed from flake (2026-05-21, evening)** — `hosts/server/` directory and `flake.nix` entry deleted. The `server` host has no physical hardware; the placeholder was dead weight. Will be re-created via `/new-host` when hardware is available.
- **dbus-broker transition COMPLETE on all 5 hosts (2026-05-21)** — Root cause of resistance: `nix-flatpak` bundles its own older nixpkgs (`da5ad661`) whose `dbus.nix` defaults to `mkDefault "dbus"`, silently winning over nixpkgs-unstable's `mkDefault "broker"`. Fix: explicit (non-default) `services.dbus.implementation = "broker"` in `security.nix` beats all `mkDefault` values regardless of source. All five hosts verified via `systemctl status dbus`.
- **nixpkgs channel split (2026-05-21)** — Desktop hosts (gaming, laptop, natalie-laptop) stay on `nixos-unstable` for rolling updates. Server hosts (vpn-server, server) are pinned to `nixos-25.05` via a dedicated `nixpkgs-stable` input in `flake.nix`. Rationale: server stability and predictability outweigh the value of rolling updates for headless hosts; desktop hosts benefit from latest drivers and DE releases. vpn-server deployed and confirmed healthy on 25.05 with WireGuard peers intact.
- **CLAUDE.md corrections (2026-05-21)** — Three inaccuracies fixed: (1) `virtualisation` removed from `desktopModules` description — it is gaming-only; (2) `+ disko` added to vpn-server module composition; (3) directory layout tree corrected (`bosko/` indentation, `disko.nix` added to vpn-server listing). natty's wheel/trusted-user status corrected — she has both, same as bosko; the only differences are no user packages and no SSH keys.
- **Five new skills added (2026-05-21)** — `diff-generations` (nix store diff-closures between generations), `flake-check` (validate flake across all 5 hosts), `journal` (tail journald, local or remote), `nix-repl` (print repl command + host-specific starter expressions), `fmt` (alejandra with nixpkgs-fmt fallback). `nix-repl` prints rather than execs to avoid the TTY issue.
- **`/nixos-rebuild` skill removed (2026-05-20, night)** — `nh os boot` requires a controlling TTY for sudo; it cannot be run from a Claude Code subprocess. The correct workflow is: Claude edits config → user runs `rebuild` in their terminal. The `/nixos-dry-run` skill is unaffected (uses `--dry`, no sudo). `switch-de` next-steps updated to reference the `rebuild` alias directly.
- **Skill library extended to 18 skills (2026-05-20, second session)** — `ssh-host`, `remote-rebuild`, `rollback`, `search-pkg`, `new-host`, and `pin-input` added. The library now covers the full NixOS workflow surface: SSH, remote deployments, rollbacks, package discovery, host provisioning, and flake input management.
- **`pin-input` warns about follow-inputs (2026-05-20)** — When pinning `nixpkgs`, the skill explicitly warns that `home-manager` and `disko` follow nixpkgs by default and will also be pinned unless the user breaks those follows first.
- **`new-host` does not auto-edit `flake.nix` (2026-05-20)** — Shows the exact flake.nix entry to add (including correct `lib.mkSystem` call and module list) but requires the user to apply it. Same safe-by-default convention as `new-module`.
- **agenix deferred (2026-05-20)** — Explored agenix for managing WireGuard private keys declaratively; decided against it. VPN is fully working, no reinstalls expected, and adding agenix would require a full re-keying of all hosts. Plan saved at `/home/bosko/.claude/plans/agentix-wireguard-setup.md` for reference if a future reinstall happens.
- **Private keys confirmed out-of-repo (2026-05-20)** — WireGuard private keys live at `/etc/wireguard/private.key` on each host, referenced by path in the Nix config. They are never in the git repository. `.gitignore` already covers `*.key`.
- **Project-local Claude Code skill library started (2026-05-20, first session)** — Seven skills added initially: `nixos-dry-run`, `nixos-rebuild`, `nixos-gc`, `vpn-status`, `new-module`, `commit`, `push`. Skills travel with the repo and are version-controlled.
- **`new-module` skill does not auto-edit `flake.nix` (2026-05-20)** — Shows the exact import line to add but requires the user (or explicit follow-up) to apply it. Avoids silent modifications to the most critical file in the repo.
- **`commit` skill stages specific files, never `git add -A` (2026-05-20)** — Prevents accidentally committing private keys, `.env` files, or large build artifacts; falls back to `-A` only on explicit user request.
- **`nixos-gc` keeps 3 generations (2026-05-20)** — Matches the `cleanup` alias in `shell.nix`; retains rollback headroom without the destruction of `nix-collect-garbage -d`.
- **Full-tunnel VPN routing chosen (2026-05-18)** — `allowedIPs = ["0.0.0.0/0" "::/0"]` routes all client traffic through the Oracle server, hiding the client's real IP. Split-tunnel (VPN subnet only) was briefly in place but replaced in the same session.
- **`::/0` dropped from allowedIPs — both fixes are needed together (2026-06-17, session 11)** — Session 10 disabled IPv6 but kept `::/0`, reasoning it would keep the tunnel "correct if IPv6 is ever added." That was wrong and broke `wg-quick-wg0` on every boot: wg-quick installs a route for each allowedIP, `ip -6 route add ::/0 dev wg0` fails on the IPv6-disabled device, and wg-quick tears the interface down (exit 4). Dropped `::/0`, leaving `allowedIPs = [ "0.0.0.0/0" ]`. With no IPv6 stack there is nothing to leak, so it is still a full tunnel — the privacy argument for keeping `::/0` only holds when IPv6 is *enabled*. **If IPv6 is ever restored on the server, flip `networking.enableIPv6 = true` AND re-add `"::/0"` together — never one without the other.** A `NOTE:` in `vpn.nix` records this trap.
- **IPv6 disabled on VPN clients over dropping `::/0` (2026-06-17, session 10)** — The full tunnel's `::/0` pulls IPv6 into a v4-only `wg0`, black-holing IPv6-only hosts (TMDb) and hanging Jellyfin's image fetches for 100s. Two fixes were weighed: (a) `networking.enableIPv6 = false` on the clients, vs (b) removing `::/0` from `allowedIPs`. Chose (a) to preserve the full-tunnel privacy guarantee (option (b) alone would route IPv6 natively around the VPN, leaking the real IP for any v6 destination). _Superseded in session 11:_ keeping `::/0` alongside disabled IPv6 broke wg-quick — the two changes belong together (see entry above). The end state is **both**: IPv6 off **and** `::/0` removed.
- **DNS promoted to shared `vpn.nix` (2026-05-18)** — `dns = [1.1.1.1 8.8.8.8]` was first added as a natalie-laptop-specific workaround (LAN resolver `10.0.0.20` is unreachable via full-tunnel), then recognized as universally correct and moved to the shared module. All three client hosts now inherit it automatically on `vpn.nix` import.
- **ARM server builds natively (2026-05-18)** — `server-rebuild` now SSHs to the Oracle ARM server and builds there; `binfmt` aarch64 emulation is available on gaming as an offline fallback. Cross-compiling `aarch64` on `x86_64` without explicit cross-compilation config fails with a platform mismatch.
- **VPN subnet `10.10.0.0/24` (2026-05-18)** — Chosen to avoid overlap with Oracle's internal LAN (`10.0.0.x`). Server: `10.10.0.1`; gaming: `10.10.0.2`; laptop: `10.10.0.3`; natalie-laptop: `10.10.0.4`.
- **`trustedInterfaces + checkReversePath = "loose"` on vpn-server (2026-05-18)** — Required for the firewall to forward peer packets and accept asymmetric NAT return traffic arriving on `enp0s6`.
- **Starship config inlined into `shell.nix` (2026-05-17)** — Eliminates the external TOML dotfile and its `xdg.configFile` symlink. The configuration is a first-class Nix attrset evaluated at build time; the full Gruvbox-themed prompt with all module settings was preserved. Single source of truth, no runtime file management.
- **Helix auto-format disabled globally (2026-05-17)** — Applied uniformly with `auto-format = false` across all configured languages. Prevents unexpected reformatting during editing sessions; format manually when needed.
- **NordVPN `dotfiles/vpn/` deleted (2026-05-17)** — Dead code from before the WireGuard approach. No path back to NordVPN in the current design.
- **Host directories moved to `hosts/` (2026-05-15)** — All five host-specific directories (`gaming`, `laptop`, `natalie-laptop`, `server`, `vpn-server`) relocated from `dotfiles/<hostname>/` to `hosts/<hostname>/`. Clarifies the repo layout: `dotfiles/` now holds only shared and user-specific HM configs; `hosts/` holds machine-specific NixOS config.
- **Bosko-specific HM configs moved to `dotfiles/bosko/` (2026-05-15)** — `bosko-claude.nix` and `claude/agents/` relocated from `dotfiles/common/configs/` to `dotfiles/bosko/`. Makes the user-scope of these files explicit; `dotfiles/common/configs/` now contains only configs shared by both users.
- **`nvidia.nix` scoped per-host (2026-05-14)** — Removed from `desktopModules`; each desktop host (gaming, laptop, natalie-laptop) now imports it explicitly. Preparation for the gaming AMD card swap: dropping NVIDIA from gaming will require only a one-line flake change.
- **`rebuild` alias switched to `nh os boot` (2026-05-12)** — Avoids live-switch activation failures. Changes are staged; rebooting applies them.
- **`claude-code` and `gemini-cli` moved to `users.nix` user packages (2026-05-14)** — User-scoped tools belong in `users.users.bosko.packages`, not duplicated across per-host `environment.nix` files.
- **Cleanup retention reduced to 3 builds (2026-05-14)** — 3 generations is sufficient rollback headroom; saves disk space.
- **natty now gets `home.nix` applied (2026-05-14)** — The `home-manager.nix` reorganisation added the missing `home.nix` import to natty's HM block. Previously natty had a HM block with no imports.
- **`lutris` commented out in gaming.nix (2026-05-12)** — `lutris` transitively depends on `openldap-2.6.13` for `i686-linux`; that derivation has no binary cache entry and attempting to build it from source blocks `nh os switch` for hours. Workaround: `# lutris` comment in `gaming.nix`; `faugus-launcher` (already in the list) covers the use-case. Restore once a binary cache entry exists.
- **`audit-rules-nixos.service` disabled (2026-05-11)** — `auditctl` 4.1.2-unstable rejects blank lines in `audit.rules`; nixpkgs hard-codes one before `-e 1`. Disabling the service via `systemd.services.audit-rules-nixos.enable = lib.mkForce false` is cleaner than patching nixpkgs. `auditd` continues running (required for AppArmor). If custom audit rules are needed in future, load them via a separate unit.
- **`natty` restored as non-wheel user (2026-05-11)** — Removed in the 2026-05-06 audit for being in the wheel group. Now back with no elevated privileges (no wheel, no trusted-user) for the Natalie laptop host.
- **`vpn.nix` deleted (2026-05-11)** — File had been commented out in `flake.nix` since April; deleted to reduce dead code. Will be rewritten from scratch when VPN deployment is imminent.
- **`amd.nix` scoped to gaming host only (2026-05-11)** — The laptop has no AMD GPU; `amd.nix` was incorrectly in `desktopModules`. Now imported only in the gaming host's module list.
- **dbus pinned to classic implementation (2026-05-10)** — nixpkgs-unstable rev `4bd9165` silently changed `services.dbus.implementation` default to `"broker"`. Fix: `services.dbus.implementation = lib.mkDefault "dbus";` in `security.nix`. Broker should not be re-enabled until validated against this AppArmor configuration.
- **Full security audit remediation (2026-05-06)** — All Critical, High, and most Medium/Low findings addressed in a single session. Rationale: the gaming host is blocked by an unrelated upstream regression anyway; best to get the config into a hardened state before the next activation.
- **L-6 declined** — User chose not to change `edit = "sudo hx"` alias to `sudoedit`; alias retained as-is.
- **chrony replaces ntpd** — Applied to all three hosts; handles intermittent connectivity better.
- **WireGuard keys deferred** — Placeholder keys are not a real risk until VPN is deployed; generating them without a live peer adds no security value.
- **Stable nixpkgs pin deferred (M-9)** — Requires adding a second nixpkgs input to flake.nix; scoped to a future session.
- **Claude agents backed up via HM** — `bosko-claude.nix` symlinks agent definitions from the repo into `~/.claude/agents/` on rebuild. Confirmed on laptop (switch succeeded 2026-05-10); gaming pending its switch.
- **nix-ld consolidated into gaming.nix** — All gaming-specific config is colocated in one module.

- ~~natalie-laptop: `cups-browsed`'s `BindsTo=avahi-daemon.service` coupling can still stall permanent-queue creation after any avahi restart~~ (session 59, 2026-07-27) — **PARTIALLY RESOLVED (session 71, `841eb05`):** `modules/printing.nix` now has a `cups-browsed-fixup` oneshot unit that retries `systemctl try-restart cups-browsed` once `network-online.target` is reached, fixing the specific boot-time trigger (avahi reporting active on `lo` before the real interface joins). **New distinct trigger found (2026-08-15, gaming)**: the same "discovered via avahi but no CUPS queue" symptom recurs when the *printer itself* (not the network) isn't up yet when `cups-browsed` does its browse-and-create pass — e.g. PC boots, `cups-browsed-fixup` fires once network is online, but the printer is powered on *after* that. `cups-browsed-fixup` only runs once early in boot, so it doesn't help here; confirmed via journal that `cups-browsed` had zero activity between its boot-time start and the manual fix. Live fix is still `sudo systemctl restart cups-browsed` (verified via `lpstat -p -d`) — user evaluated a periodic-timer mitigation and declined it as overkill for how rarely this triggers (see Recent Decisions). No further repo fix planned; this is an accepted, understood gap in `cups-browsed`'s discovery model, not a config bug.
- ~~natalie-laptop: Kate print-dialog discrepancy unresolved~~ (session 58) — **RESOLVED (session 59, 2026-07-27):** not a stale memory — Kate's system CUPS client does live DNS-SD printer discovery and can show a network IPP printer before any permanent queue exists; OnlyOffice's bundled print dialog (running in its own `buildFHSEnv` sandbox) only ever sees printers that are already real, permanent CUPS destinations. See Current Project State for the full root cause and fix (`cups-browsed` restart).

The Plasma-login half of this check is now moot — session 50 removed `plasma.nix` from natalie-laptop entirely (niri-only "for now"), so there's no Plasma session left to test the DMS/swayidle/kdeglobals gating against. - **TEMPORARY: `rtk` package unavailable on `vpn-server`, PLUS a separate `doCheck` waiver now needed on the hosts that do get it** (session 50, updated 2026-07-24/26) — `modules/users.nix`/`modules/claude-code.nix` guard the package and its PreToolUse hook on `pkgs ? rtk` because `rtk` only exists in nixpkgs-unstable, not the `nixpkgs-25.11` stable channel `vpn-server` pins. Eval is fixed (all 4 hosts pass `deep-eval-check`), but `vpn-server` itself doesn't actually get the `rtk` binary or its Claude Code token-optimization hook until a future 25.11 point release backports the package — no action needed, the guard self-heals. **Separately (2026-07-24, commit `839cbc0`)**: once `pkgs ? rtk` started evaluating true on the unstable hosts, rtk-0.43.0's own `cargo test` checkPhase started failing under `-D warnings` (dead-code lints), breaking `nh os boot`'s real build — patched with `pkgs.rtk.overrideAttrs (_: { doCheck = false; })` in `modules/users.nix`. Reconfirmed still both required 2026-07-26 via a real `nix build` of the unmodified package. **On each future nixpkgs bump, check both**: whether `rtk` resolves on stable now (removes the `pkgs ? rtk` guard's practical relevance for vpn-server), and whether `nh os boot` still needs `doCheck = false` (remove once nixpkgs ships an rtk revision whose tests pass cleanly).
- **New desktop hosts need `secrets/hosts/<hostname>.yaml` before their first eval** (session 37) — a consequence of `vpn.nix` living in `desktopModules`: its `sops.secrets."wg-private-key".sopsFile` path must exist at eval time even before the host joins the VPN. The `new-host` skill documents the flow (generate wg keypair, encrypt to admin key at scaffold time; add the host's age key post-first-boot). Not an issue for any existing host.
